  A TRIAL OF İSMAİL ENVER PASHA DURING THE FIRST WORLD
              WAR: WOMEN WORKERS BATTALION
                                                    Burak CAN*

Abstract
In the First World War, the Ottoman Empire had to fight on many fronts. This multi-front war has led
to a significant decline in the male population. This decrease in the male population has created new
employment areas for women in social life. During this period, the Women's Employment Society was
founded in 1916 under the chairmanship of Enver Pasha and with the participation of his wife Naciye
Sultan. This society had an important place in terms of allowing Muslim Ottoman women to quickly
enter the working life. The society received a record number of applications for participation in a short
period of 2.5 months after its establishment. The presence of Enver Pasha at the head of the society
provided a significant advantage to the society, thus it had the opportunity to participate in military
tenders and create new employment opportunities. This society, which has an increasingly important
place in working life, also undertook a service such as the creation of labor battalions consisting of
Muslim women due to the conditions arising from the war, and as a result, Enver Pasha, through his
aide Captain Hasan Tahsin Bey, took the task of establishing the First Women Labor Battalion in the
First Ottoman Army on July 26, 1917. He gave the commander to Ferik Esad Pasha. The women worker
battalion to be established; After the regulation, operation and rules were determined, albeit difficult,
the final form of the regulation was given by Enver Pasha, the president of the society. Recruitment of
workers to the battalion, whose regulations were completed, was announced to the public through
newspaper advertisements. The Women Worker Battalion that would be formed would not be sent to
the front, but would only deal with work in the background. During the recruitment phase for the
battalion, it was decided that the officers to be recruited for higher positions will be determined by the
officials of the Islamic Society for the Employment of Women, provided that they are selected from
among those who can read and write. The main purpose of infecting this ability is II. It includes the
place of Turkish women in social life during the Constitutional Monarchy Period and the stages until
they achieved this place. The aim of this study is to contribute to the field. The study will run a
comprehensive review model in accordance with qualitative research designs. It consists of periodic
press, archive documents, copyright and review studies on working conditions.
Keywords: Woman, Enver Pasha, women's labor battalions, Naciye Sultan, Women's Employment
Society of Islam.

can.burak@ogr.ahievran.edu.tr

      A METHOD TO PREVENT CERVICAL CANCER IN WOMEN'S
                    HEALTH: HPV VACCINE
                                     1. Burcu YILMAZ*, 2. Nihal ALTUN†

Abstract
Cervical cancer is the second most common cancer after breast cancer, killing one woman every two
minutes worldwide. It accounts for 3.6% of female cancers in developed countries and 15% in
underdeveloped countries. Although it is most common between the ages of 50-59, 65% of invasive
cervical cancers are seen in the 40-60 age group in our country. It is emphasized that Human Papilloma
Virus (HPV) must be present for the development of cervical cancer and that other risk factors either
increase the rate of exposure to the virus or that viral persistence is important in accelerating the
carcinogenic process. With the combination of HPV infection and facilitating factors, metaplastic
squamous cells in the transformation zone undergo dysplasia and form precancerous lesions. The
majority of HPV infections are transient and resolve spontaneously. Cancer does not arise from such
transient infections. In cases of persistent infection, cervical intraepithelial premalignant lesions and
adenocarcinoma may develop. It takes approximately 10-20 years for cervical cancer lesions to develop
into advanced cancer. Since HPV is a sexually transmitted infection, age at first sexual intercourse,
number of partners, tobacco use, immune system status are important risk factors in the development
of cervical cancer. The importance of early diagnosis stems from the fact that it makes treatment
possible. For this reason, various screening programs have been developed for cervical cancers, aiming
at early diagnosis and treatment. In screening, Pap smears and HPV-DNA testing are used to detect
precancerous lesions and HPV infection. In controlling the incidence of cervical cancer, primary
prevention is the prevention of precancerous lesions and secondary prevention is the detection of
precancerous lesions before they develop into invasive cancer. Primary prevention is against HPV
infection. There are currently 4 prophylactic HPV vaccines approved by the World Health Organization
(WHO). All vaccines protect against HPV 16 and 18, which cause 70-80% of cervical cancers. For the
vaccine to be most effective, it should be given before exposure to HPV. The WHO recommendation
is to vaccinate girls aged 9-14 years. While 2 doses of vaccine are required for this age group, 3 doses
of vaccine are required for those over 15 years of age. A study conducted in 2023 found that women's
knowledge about HPV vaccination was not at the expected level and access to HPV vaccine was low.
The most important reason for this is the high cost of HPV vaccine. These results emphasize the
importance of educating women more about HPV vaccination and facilitating access to the vaccine.
Keywords: Women's health, cervical cancer, HPV vaccine.

         GENDER-NEUTRAL LANGUAGE IN FEMINIST UTOPIAN
                         LITERATURE
                                       Burçak Tuba TAYHAN GÜZEL*

Abstract
Feminist utopian literature critically interrogates and seeks to redefine constructs related to sex, gender,
and the role of women within a patriarchally structured society. Feminist utopias explore the multiple
layers of oppression of women in detail. Specifically, these texts challenge and reconstruct the
conventional roles assigned to women—primarily as wives and mothers—by envisioning alternative
worlds from a feminist perspective. Within these alternative ideal worlds, authors of feminist utopias
address the primary factors contributing to such oppression, in contrast to traditional utopian literature
dominated by male perspectives, which typically engages with themes such as the state, citizenship,
religion, art, the economy, and hierarchical structures. However, the woman has been forgotten behind
their sexist expressions. Feminist utopias deconstruct the logic of natural rights based on a patriarchal
mentality and replace traditional governments and nation-states with social structures based on
community and global ecology. One of the essential men-centered structures that feminist utopia
writers must change has been patriarchy’s sexist language. In this context, feminist utopian writers
have created alternative worlds and offered gender-neutral languages for their feminist worlds. Gender-
neutral language helps us to understand the basis of women’s oppression and inferiority. Gender-neutral
language in feminist utopias seeks to change linguistic usage as a part of the attempt to change society
and patriarchal forms of thought. The function of gender-neutral language in feminist utopias
transcends mere lexical adjustments; it serves as a critical tool for disassembling systemic gender
biases and encouraging an inclusive society free from the limitations of traditional gender roles. In this
context, this paper will explore the deployment and implications of gender-neutral language in feminist
utopian narratives.
Keywords: Feminist utopias, gender-neutral language, utopian literature.

burcak.guzel@ahievran.edu.tr

     WOMEN REPRESENTATION AND ITS REFLECTION IN THE
     MEDIA: THE IMAGE OF WOMEN ON MEDIA PLATFORMS IN
                          TURKEY
                      1. Büşra GÜRCÜ*, 2. Nilgün AVCI†, 3. Meryem METİNOĞLU‡

Abstract
The Turkish Language Association defines media as “the name given to all major communication and
broadcasting organs” and “communication media, communication tools, all mass communication
tools”. Today, when it comes to media, newspapers, magazines, theater, video, cinema, radio, television
and internet come to mind. With the developing technology, the power of the media in society is
increasing. The media is such a great power that it is defined as the fourth power after the legislature,
executive and judiciary. The first radio broadcasting in Turkey started in 1927. Mostly music programs,
news and interview programs were also included in radio broadcasts. In radio broadcasts that included
programs on mother-child-health or women's issues, the traditional roles of women were emphasized.
In Turkey, programs for women started for the first time in 1939 with a program called “Ev Saati”
(Home Hour) on the radio, which was renamed “Ev İçi” (In Home) in 1970 and continued with topics
such as childcare, health and family. The fact that private television channels in Turkey, which gained
legal status in the 1990s, were concerned about viewership led them to resort to various methods to
attract viewers to the screen. With the beginning of television broadcasting after radio, many programs
were made for women. Women appear in the media in different forms of representation such as natural-
equal being; wife/mother/faternal woman; third page magazine object; sexual object-object of pleasure;
organization-subject of action; instrumental being. These representations of women both legitimize the
media's view of women and shape society's perception of women. As if the imposition of the model of
a woman who behaves in accordance with the expectations of society is not enough, a perception is
also created that the female body should be beautiful and delicate. The characteristics attributed to
women in print and visual media are far from giving them any power and status. Many products in the
media are promoted and marketed using the female body, whether they are related to women or not.
Women in TV series, movies, advertisements, music videos and magazine programs are often exploited
by the media by highlighting their sexuality. Women are taught how to cook better, how to take care of
children better, how to have a more beautiful body, what cosmetics should be used to beautify
themselves, in a way to make women more admirable to men. It should not be forgotten that this is
actually abuse of women and necessary regulations and measures should be taken.
Keywords: Media, women, representation, platform, image.

mmetinoglu@nku.edu.tr

                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

                  INDUSTRY 4.0 AND WOMEN’S EMPLOYMENT
                                               Cemile EVKAYA*

Abstract
Industrial revolutions have brought about social and economic transformations, significantly impacting
labor markets. Traditional employment models, where workers were tied to a single employer and
workplace, performing the same job for extended periods, have undergone radical changes. Physical
strength is no longer the sole requirement; mental skills and abilities are now valued, especially in the
context of Industry 4.0. The term “Industry 4.0” was first coined in Hannover in 2011. It represents a
production and business environment based on technology and innovation. Key technologies in this
era include the Internet of Things (IoT), artificial intelligence (AI), augmented reality, virtual reality,
cloud computing, big data, and additive manufacturing. As new job opportunities emerge, the
distribution of employment shares is expected to shift based on the nature of work. Women have
historically entered the workforce during industrial revolutions, and their role remains crucial in the
current transformation. With Industry 4.0, it is essential to examine how women’s positions in the labor
market evolve, their ability to adapt to emerging technologies, and the conditions for benefiting from
new job opportunities. The purpose of this study is to explore the characteristics of Industry 4.0, its
impact on the labor market, and its implications for gender equality. The analysis considers
employment distribution across sectors and occupations, gender-specific differences in education and
skills, and the specific context of female employment in technology within Turkey. Just like previous
industrial revolutions, the Fourth Industrial Revolution will profoundly affect female employment. The
Fourth Industrial Revolution, much like its predecessors, will profoundly impact women’s
employment. In this study, we discuss how women can participate in the new jobs and professions
created by the Industry 4.0 environment, provided they possess adequate knowledge and skills and are
granted equal opportunities. Within the scope of all these purposes, the status and position of women
were examined descriptively within the framework of researches and statistical indicators in the
national and international literature. The main question is whether this effect will be a threat or an
opportunity for women and how potential threats can be transformed into opportunities. Currently,
various activities are being carried out by governments, the private sector, and Non-Governmental
Organizations (NGOs) to convert threats into opportunities.
Keywords: Industry 4.0, women’s employment, new generation employment.

                                                                                            Turkish Presentation

5680, email: cemile.evkaya@bilecik.edu.tr

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

      CONTRIBUTION OF LAW NO. 7406 IN THE FIGHT AGAINST
                 VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN
                                         Cengiz Topel ÇİFTCİOĞLU*

Abstract
Violence against women seen all over the world; It is a legal and social problem that describes women
being threatened, coerced and deprived of their freedom by all kinds of acts that cause physical, sexual,
economic, psychological, pain or suffering due to their gender. This problem has been comprehensively
addressed by the UN Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination against Women
(CEDAW), adopted in 1979, and the Council of Europe Convention on Preventing and Combating
Violence Against Women and Domestic Violence (Istanbul Convention), signed in 2011. With the
increase in social sensitivity and awareness on this issue in Turkey, Law No. 4320 on the Protection of
the Family was adopted in 1998; However, since it was not deemed sufficient, it was abolished in 2012
by the Law No. 6284 on the Protection of the Family and the Prevention of Violence Against Women.
Law No. 6284 clearly states "prevention of violence against women" and regulates the measures to be
taken in such cases of violence. However, despite these measures, femicide could not be prevented; In
order to strengthen this struggle, the Law on Amendments to the Turkish Penal Code and Certain Laws
dated 12.05.2022 and numbered 7406 was adopted and the Turkish Penal Code (TPC) included
intentional killing (art. 82/1-f), intentional wounding (art. 86/2); in the crimes of torture (art. 94/1),
torment (art. 96/1) and threat (art. 106/1), the fact that the victim is a woman has been accepted as an
aggravating reason for the punishment. Although these regulations refer to crimes committed by male
perpetrators against women, the fact that this is not clearly stated in the Law will lead to the application
of these provisions to female perpetrators if these crimes are committed by female perpetrators, which
is incompatible with the purpose of the Law. On the other hand, within the scope of this Law, the crime
of stalking, which has been expected to be made a crime by the public for a while and aims to protect
women from the grave consequences of violent incidents, has also been regulated (art. 123/A). Again,
with this Law, article 62/2 of the Turkish Penal Code was amended and it was prohibited to use the
perpetrator's behavior and actions at the hearing as a reason for discretionary discount, known publicly
as "tie discount". With the Law No. 7406, the Code of Criminal Procedure was also amended and the
crime of intentional injury committed against women was included among the catalog crimes (art.
100/3-i) in terms of arrest measures. Women are given the right to request the appointment of a lawyer
by the bar association in cases of persistent stalking, intentional injury, torture or torment crimes
committed against women during the investigation (art. 234/1-a.3) and prosecution (art. 239/1) phases.
Finally, the crime of persistent stalking is also included in the scope of the prohibition of mediation
(art. 253/3). In this study, the regulations made by Law No. 7406 within the scope of combating
violence against women will be examined in the light of judicial decisions and suggestions for the
solution of the problem will be presented.
Keywords: Violence against women, fight against violence, persistent stalking, crime victims,
femicides.

  EXPERIENCES OF NURSING STUDENTS IN WOMEN'S HEALTH
 AND DISEASES NURSING INTERNSHIP AS DETERMINED BY THE
             REFLECTIVE-THINKING METHOD
            1. Ceyda Su GÜNDÜZ*, 2. Hamide ARSLAN TARUS†, 3. Nurdan DEMİRCİ‡

Abstract
To examine the experiences of nursing students determined using the reflective thinking method during
their internships in women's health and diseases units. The research was conducted between December
2023 and January 2024. The population of the research consisted of students (n = 98) who studied in
the nursing department of a state university, took the Gynecology and Diseases Nursing Internship
course, and went on internship in 5 hospitals where the researchers were responsible. The sample of
the research consisted of 91 students who volunteered to participate in the study. Data were collected
using the "Student Introduction Form" and "Reflective Report Directive Form". Ethics committee
approval, institutional permission and participants' consent were obtained to conduct the research.
Frequency, percentages, arithmetic mean and standard deviation were used in the analysis of
quantitative data. Qualitative data were examined using the content analysis method. The average age
of the students is 21.59±2.26 and 86.8% are women. Students mostly prepared reports about their
experiences in the gynecology clinic (n=25), obstetrics clinic (n=22) and delivery room (n=20). They
had most of their experiences with patients/mothers/pregnant women (n=35) and nurses/midwives
(n=31). During their experiences, they frequently felt sadness (n=30), surprise (n=15), disappointment
(n=14), anxiety (n=13) and shame (n=10). The learning experiences they reflected in their reflective
reports were explained in 3 main themes. In the study, it was determined that students mostly felt
negative emotions during their internship experiences in units related to women's health and diseases.
During their internships, students may encounter various problems such as communication problems
with healthcare professionals, patients/mothers/pregnant women and their relatives, and other interns,
professional conflict between nursing and midwifery, inadequate health care, disregard for interns, and
not having sufficient knowledge and experience. These problems demotivate students and negatively
affect the learning process. It is important for healthcare professionals and faculty to support students
more and serve as role models for them. It is recommended that the guidance nurse model be used
actively and that faculty members give routine feedback to the institution regarding the practice after
the internship.
Keywords: Nursing student, clinical learning, clinical practice, qualitative research, reflection,
reflective thinking.

        20TH CENTURY TURKISH PAINTING ART REFLECTS THE
              MOTHER GODDESS ARCHETYPE IN STYLE
                                                      Cihan TİKİT*

Abstract
From the beginning, humanity has tried to understand the universe and itself in all its states of being,
and has created myths, sometimes out of fear, sometimes out of the instinct to survive. Symbols, words,
rhythms, colours and all imitation actions created for myths and mythical actions thus express the first
traces of humanity in the universe, the first human feelings, thoughts and values that reflect the
consciousness and subconscious of the society in which they were created. Although all these mythical
elements undergo a formal transformation by adapting to the time with the new interpretations of the
societies and narrators, they continue to exist by preserving their essence. Carl Gustav Jung, one of the
founders of analytical psychology, calls this unchanging essence 'archetype'. Jung explains the
archetype as primordial symbols containing the remnants of all experiences of humanity. Myths and
archetypes, which give clues about the origins of thoughts, feelings and impulses, continue to exist in
the collective consciousness of all humanity through heredity and cultural transmission. Myths and
archetypes continue to be a source of inspiration for art, which is one of the important sources of culture
and cultural transmission. Among these myths, the Mother Goddess myth and therefore the Mother
archetype has become a universal image and has been included in artistic forms of expression from
past to present. The images of the Mother Goddess/Mother archetype, which transform without leaving
the basis of fertility, productivity and continuity, appear in every field of modern art. From the 20th
century to the present day, reflections of the mother archetype can be seen in the works of Cemal Tollu,
Mustafa Pilevneli, İbrahim Balaban, Özdemir Yemenicioğlu, Nevra Bozok, Tomur Atagök, Zerrin
Tuluğ, Can Göknil, Yeşim Tetik, Türkan Sılay Rador, Hüsamettin Koçan, Mevlüt Akyıldız and many
other artists who have shaped Turkish painting. This study, which deals with Turkish painting art in the
context of Turkish mythology, is descriptive research using the survey model. In the research, it is
aimed to determine the traces of the Mother Goddess archetype in mythical narratives that have existed
for thousands of years in 20th century Turkish painting art. In this direction, the twelve artists
mentioned above who dealt with the Mother Goddess archetype in 20th century Turkish painting and
their works, which were determined by purposeful sampling method, are analysed.
Keywords: Mythology, Turkish mythology, Turkish painting, Mother Goddess archetype.

    THEMATIC ANALYSIS OF THE HEALTH PERCEPTION AND
   ACCESS TO HEALTH SERVICES OF ROMA WOMEN: KOCAELİ
                       EXAMPLE
                    1. Çiğdem SUDAN*, 2. İrem ÇOBANOĞLU†, 3. Melike TAŞAR‡

Abstract
Roma, whose homeland is India, migrated to different parts of the world between the 5th and 11th
centuries. One of the countries they settled in as a result of their migration was Turkey. They took 3
different names according to the regions they settled in Turkey: Those who settled in the Eastern Black
Sea region are called Lom, those who settled in South Eastern and Eastern Anatolia are called Dom
and those who settled in the West are called Rom. It is known that this community, which lived as
nomadic in the past, is mostly settled in Turkey. The areas where they live are generally the suburbs on
the outskirts of the city. They maintain their own ethno-cultural structure in these neighborhoods where
they live together. Roma, who attract attention with their way of life, are subjected to various
discriminatory practices in the world and in Turkey, and experience violations of rights due to
discriminatory practices. Some of the discriminatory practices to which Roma are exposed include
access to employment, participation in education, access to health care and access to justice. While it
is known that Roma people all over the world are subjected to these discriminatory practices, it is
thought that Roma women may also experience other difficulties due to being Roma and being women.
The aim of this study, which was decided to be conducted because it was thought that Roma women
may experience different difficulties and discriminatory practices in health, which is one of the most
basic necessities in human life, is to analyze the health perceptions of Roma women living in Kocaeli
province, the economic, social and cultural difficulties they face in accessing their health rights
(discriminatory practices) to reveal the ways in which they make senserights. In order to learn about
the difficulties experienced by Roma women in the field of health, it is planned to seek answers to
questions such as "What is Roma women's perception of health and as a result of which experiences
has this perception been formed? In what ways do their experiences differ from the experiences of
other women? What kind of coping strategies have they developed in the face of their negative
experiences?" using in-depth interview technique and to analyze the interview data using Thematic
Analysis method.
Keywords: Women, Roma women, health, social policies.

     THE SEARCH FOR SUBJECTIVITY IN THE COUNTRYSIDE: A
         STORY OF EXISTENCE BETWEEN TWO ABSENCES
                                                Derya GÜLLÜK*

Abstract
Constructed on a political, social and cultural ground, women's identity has been addressed in a wide
spectrum in the world of literature. With the 21st century, women's identity, which has been discussed
on an increasingly "politicized" ground, has come to the forefront with feminist theory and postmodern
approaches. It is observed that women are questioned about their bodies, sexuality, motherhood roles,
marriages and their visibility in domestic and public life, while the distance between the image of
womanhood woven within the framework of cultural codes and the women who try to overcome them
and want to establish a consciousness of "self" is also emphasized. At this point, Seray Şahiner, who
started writing in the 2000s and who deals with the positioning of women in social life and the status
of womanhood in her narratives, chose her protagonist from a slum neighborhood in her novel Kul
(2007), for which she won the 2018 Orhan Kemal Novel Award, and in her novel Kul (2007), she dealt
with the evolution of a woman who makes a living by cleaning stairs into an "invisible" woman by no
longer being able to construct her own self when she cannot meet the roles of "mother" and "wife"
marked by social norms. Womanhood is again constructed within the framework of the norms
prescribed by society, and the woman who fails to meet these norms is unable to construct her own self
and becomes objectified under the gaze of society. In Kul, we do not see Mercan in active resistance
and she is almost purged from the center of her own life. Due to Mercan's inability to establish a family,
her house could not turn into a home, and the material destruction of the house in coordination with
the urban transformation, which is particularly visible in the novel, brought along its spiritual collapse.
Şahiner constructs the present of a woman whose past, childhood, longings and dreams we do not know
through two absences. There are two important absences in Mercan's life, two great emptinesses that
hurt her deeply; the absence of her husband, whom we do not know why he left her, and the absence
of a son who does not exist but whose existence she imagines. While these two absences occupy
Mercan's mind, we also read the wounding effect of not being able to be a wife and mother on her self
and her questioning her femininity. In this questioning, he will always look for shortcomings in himself,
list reasons and blur reality. Unable to get out of the patriarchal system of thought, unable to transcend
the boundaries when she could have been a perpetrator, Mercan is tossed about in the chaos of the city.
Constructed through the roles of wife and motherhood, womanhood seems to bury the woman's self
and identity under a pile of rubble when these two foundations on which it was built collapse.
Ultimately, in this study, we aim to read the extent to which women's existence has been blurred within
the patriarchal system through the possibilities of feminist criticism.
Keywords: Femininity, patriarchy, identity, province.

dgulluk@bandirma.edu.tr

 A RELATIONAL/INTERACTIONAL ANALYSIS OF GENDER-BASED
              OCCUPATIONAL SEGREGATION
                                                 Derya KESKİN*

Abstract
While women's labor force participation increases worldwide, gender-based occupational segregation
remains one of the most prominent and pervasive features of labor markets. Therefore, differences in
income, status and ultimately living standards between working women and men persist. Women still
tend towards jobs with lower prestige and incomes, while still being paid less than men for the same
work. Young women, who are encouraged to behave in accordance with gender roles from childhood
onwards, face these expectations during their education as well. Thus, regardless of their level of
education, women position themselves in the labor market in accordance with gendered expectations
to a significant extent. The main argument of this study, based on a relational sociological approach, is
that society is not made up of individuals but of the relationships between these individuals, and that
causal forces are often found not simply in objects and individuals but in the structures and
relationships they form. From the gender perspective, women's expectations and actions are shaped not
only by apparent structural conditions, but also by everyday relationships and interactions in which
gender plays an important role. Thus, gender is always an act, but not an act performed by a subject
that can be said to exist prior to the act. In other words, individuals tend to behave according to the
interaction order in relationships. Women and men do not need to consciously adhere to traditional
gender roles for career decisions to be influenced by gender beliefs. Interactions within relationships
determine women's and men's social, cultural, symbolic and ultimately economic capital in different
ways and often to the detriment of women. The aim of this study is to provide a conceptual and
theoretical discussion of how women's and men's perceptions and behaviors regarding their positioning
in the labor market are constantly influenced by dynamic processes in the relationships in which they
are part of. In this context, concepts such as “co-presence” and “interaction order”, “gender as an act”,
“self-expression” and “gender-based self-expression”, “social, cultural and symbolic capital” are used
to discuss gendered division of labor and gender-based occupational segregation and perceptions. In
addition, data from related studies are also utilized.
Keywords: Gendered division of labor, gender-based occupational segregation, gendered self-
expression, social, cultural and symbolic capital.

derya.keskin@kocaeli.edu.tr

   MIGRATION AND ITS EFFECTS ON WOMEN'S REPRODUCTIVE
                         HEALTH
                        1. Dilek BİNGÖL*, 2. Melike DİŞSİZ†, 3. Sevilay AYDIN‡

Abstract
Today, migration emerges as an international problem that affects individuals and society socially,
culturally and psychologically. Women are among the risk groups most affected by the migration
process. Although migration is traumatic for all individuals, women are more affected by this process
due to gender inequality. Among the effects of migration on women's health: There are many problems
such as STIs (sexually transmitted infections), abuse, early marriages, adolescent pregnancy, lack of
prenatal, birth and postnatal care, birth complications, low birth weight babies, and preterm birth. This
study was prepared to explain the effects of migration on women's health. Women who migrate from
their countries due to wars and other reasons have problems in adapting to a new life, changes in the
cultural environment and lack of social support, and in benefiting from health services, which is one
of the biggest negative effects of migration. In this study, studies examining the effects of migration
on women's health were evaluated. It is a known fact that wars and migrations around the world
increase women's health problems. The most common health problems in immigrant women include
infectious diseases, vaginal infections, inability to benefit from family planning services, spontaneous
miscarriages, multiple and short-term pregnancies, low number of births in hospitals, high number of
births at home, and irregular menstrual bleeding. When looking at the effects of migration on women's
daily lives and health, it can be said that it generally negatively affects their health and quality of life.
Nurses have important responsibilities in protecting and improving the health of immigrant women.
Nurses should accept that immigrants are risky groups, have information about the economic status of
the women they serve, family structure, traditional practices, beliefs, communication methods, roles in
the family and society, and cultural values, and ensure that immigrants have access to health services.
In line with this information, an individualized transcultural nursing care approach should be applied
without judging or questioning the woman.
Keywords: Migration, women, nurse.

      ORGANIZATIONAL SUPPORT AND BARRIERS IN WOMEN'S
                   LEADERSHIP JOURNEY
                                                   Dilek ÇINAR*

Abstract
The institutional support and barriers women face in their leadership journeys are at the center of
discussions on gender equality and diversity in business. While women leaders today are expected to
play an important role in business life and contribute to the success of businesses, they still face many
challenges. It is critical to provide organizational support mechanisms to support the success of women
leaders and improve the performance of businesses. These support mechanisms can be implemented
in various ways to support women leaders' career development, strengthen their leadership skills and
help them overcome barriers in the business world. Mentoring programs, training and development
opportunities, comprehensive support networks, and policies that focus on gender equality are
important tools to increase the success of women leaders. The various barriers that women leaders face
in the process of ascending to leadership positions should not be overlooked. Factors such as gender-
based discrimination, prejudices, societal expectations and organizational culture are important barriers
that make it difficult for women leaders to advance in business life. Institutional policies and cultural
changes are needed to overcome these obstacles and enable women leaders to realize their full
potential. This study aims to examine in detail the institutional support and barriers women face in
their leadership journeys and discuss various strategic approaches. It is important to address these
issues in depth to enable women leaders to achieve greater success in business and contribute to the
advancement of diversity and inclusion in organizations.
Keywords: Organizational Culture, organizational culture, barriers for women managers, women
leaders, gender-based discrimination.

       GENDER & POWER: THE DILEMMA OF INEQUAL
  OPPORTUNITIES IN EDUCATION, HEALTH AND EMPLOYMENT
                                               Diler Ezgi TARHAN*

Abstract
Gender inequality puts women and individuals with different fluid identities (sexual orientations) at a
disadvantage and exposes them to inequality of opportunity in education, health and employment. is a
problem. Since the steps and precautions that need to be taken to ensure equal opportunities in the
fields of education, health, employment and social services to all segments of the society cannot be
achieved without an objective measurement and analysis of gender perception in the society, this
declaration will primarily focus on women's home and work issues in the light of TÜİK 2023 data. and
the issues in which they are disadvantaged in their educational life will be analyzed, then the society's
awareness threshold on gender inequality will be discussed, and then what steps can be taken in this
regard in addition to the social policies implemented will be discussed. The inequality in question is a
violation of human rights, as it deprives women and LGBTI+ individuals who are marginalized in
society of their right to represent themselves in the public sphere, and is a result of power relations
organized with a patriarchal mentality. Therefore, inequality in education and employment not only
marginalizes and marginalizes women and LGBTI+ individuals in terms of reducing them to a
secondary status in society, imprisoning them in the private sphere, and condemning these segments to
live in inequality of opportunity; At the same time, it isolates these segments from the power, status
and decision-making bodies of the society in terms of social welfare and social development. Through
this line, discriminatory discourses and interventions caused by gender inequality in education, health
and employment, glass ceiling and child bride, etc. The gender perception of power relations that ignore
the differences of women and all other LGBTI+ individuals and establish a manipulative domination
over their lives will be criticized through concepts. The issue, which will be discussed in the light of
2023-2024 Turkey data, will be discussed in its relationship with social services and social policies,
especially in terms of employment, education-training, promotion in business fields and access to
health services.
Keywords: Inequality of opportunity, gender inequality, social policy, education-training,
employment.

     FEMININE AGENCY AMIDST COLONIAL MORALITY: A CASE
               FROM EARLY 20TH CENTURY INDIA
                                                         DISHA*

Abstract
This paper critically reassesses the notion of modernities in colonial India through a meticulous
examination of sexual offences during that era, spotlighting a compelling 1911 case involving a wife's
infidelity and her husband's involvement in sodomy. Central to the analysis is the profound question
echoing through Indian society: which transgression, a wife's adultery or a husband's sodomy, carries
greater significance? Within the Indian context, the study unveils the pervasive impact of colonial rule,
revealing how the British legal system imposed Victorian morality on diverse sexual cultures. The
clash between rigid colonial laws and the fluidity of pre-colonial sexual norms becomes starkly evident,
particularly in the realm of adultery and sodomy laws intricately interwoven with marriage, morality,
and gender dynamics. The examination of Section 377 of the Indian Penal Code exemplifies colonial
influence on sexual norms, criminalising "unnatural" acts. Similarly, Section 497 of the Indian Penal
Code perpetuates gender bias, treating women as property of their husbands. These laws, serving
British colonial rulers and local elites, significantly shaped power dynamics and intimate relationships
within marriages. The paper delves into the enduring impact of colonial-era sexual legislation on
contemporary perspectives on sexuality in India, emphasising the persisting conundrum of women's
roles. By exploring the historical roots of gender injustice, the analysis contributes to a broader
discourse on gender and justice, shedding light on how colonial impositions continue to influence
contemporary perspectives on equality in India.
Keywords: Adultery, gender injustice, sexual morality, sodomy, status of women, Victorian morality.

  EDUCATIONAL PROBLEMS FACED BY GIRLS IN RURAL AREAS:
                  TURKEY SOUTHEAST
                              1. Duygu SAĞLAM GÜVENÇ*, 2. Dilek ÇINAR†

Abstract
Turkey's education system plays a critical role in development and social justice. However, education
in rural areas faces significant problems such as gender discrimination. Especially in underdeveloped
regions such as Southeastern Anatolia, girls' right to education is frequently violated and they do not
benefit from education sufficiently. The fact that girls' right to education is ignored in the Southeastern
Anatolia region of Turkey and that they do not benefit from education at the desired levels reveals a
serious problem. Generally, in these regions, girls are married off at an early age, which leads to their
right to education being ignored and disregarded. When we look at the demographic characteristics of
rural areas, it is seen that they have a predominantly agricultural income source. Inadequate
infrastructure for schools and educational institutions, transportation problems, the fact that educational
institutions are located far away from the local community, and the demographic differences between
these communities restrict girls' access to education. It is a fact that in rural areas, traditional family
education or the cultural factor is very heavy and effective. The attitudes and behaviors of the
hierarchical and dominant regional culture towards girls reduce school attendance rates and even lead
to risks such as not receiving education at all. Economic factors should also be emphasized. Since the
income of the population living in rural areas is generally based on agriculture, the need for labor is
higher. Therefore, the need for labor force to work in agriculture is met by employing girls. Economic
inadequacies naturally also affect the meeting of educational expenses. All these factors cause families
to have a negative perspective on girls' education. The aim of this study is to reveal the problems faced
by girls in rural communities living in the Southeastern Anatolia region of Turkey in terms of education.
This research is a literature review. Within the framework of the research, it is aimed to compile the
studies in the literature, to develop an interpretative approach in line with the findings and results of
these studies and to catch a new perspective.
Keywords: Gender equality, rural development, education strategies.

              EFFECTS OF CLIMATE CHANGE ON PREGNANCY
                                    1. Ebrar HUT*, 2. Gülseren DAĞLAR†

Abstract
This compilation aims to draw attention to global climate change, its effects on pregnancy, and
strategies to mitigate its impacts. It is prepared using sources accessed through PubMed, Google
Scholar, and Yöktez search engines by searching for the keywords "climate change and pregnancy".
Climate change is a global public health issue that leads to excessive increases in temperatures, rising
sea levels, and more frequent natural disasters, threatening the lives of humans, animals, and plants.
Women are among the groups who are most affected by climate change. Pregnancy is a period
characterized by physiological and psychological changes, with a narrow margin between health and
illness for both the pregnant woman and the fetus. Due to the physiological changes during pregnancy,
pregnant women can be particularly vulnerable to environmental changes, especially climate change.
Additionally, pregnant women bear health risks associated with climate change, such as preterm birth,
increased morbidity and mortality, infection, dehydration, and inadequate nutrition. Studies have found
that exposure to high temperatures and air pollution contributes to gestational diabetes, hypertension,
preterm birth, premature membrane rupture, low birth weight, stillbirth, and congenital anomalies. Due
to all these risks, the need for health services for pregnant women increases. In this context, it is
necessary and important for women, especially pregnant women, to adapt to climate change, which
threatens the health of pregnant women and fetuses. Understanding the health problems that women
may experience due to climate change, conducting research, and taking preventive measures will
contribute to the maintenance of maternal and fetal health. Health professionals, especially midwives,
have a significant responsibility in adapting to climate change. Increasing midwives' awareness of
reducing factors contributing to climate change, enhancing adaptation, and resilience is crucial for
addressing the healthcare needs of vulnerable groups. Midwives should address health problems caused
by climate change during pregnancy monitoring and conduct research on the effects of climate change
on women's reproductive health using their roles as researchers. Maternal health should be evaluated
within the framework of climate adaptation strategies, and education and counseling should be
provided to individuals, families, and communities.
Keywords: Climate change, pregnancy, midwife.

* Master’s Student, Sivas Cumhuriyet University, Midwifery, Türkiye, ORCID: 0000-0003-1785-9199, email:

                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

          WOMEN EMPLOYMENT IN FINANCIAL TECHNOLOGY
                       INSTITUTIONS
                                                 Ece ERBUĞ*

Abstract
In line with the requirements of the digital age, traditional banking and finance practices are being
replaced by Financial Technology (FinTech) organizations all over the world. While it is accepted that
they are more egalitarian than traditional methods in terms of user experience, it is also claimed that
FinTech organizations increase women's employment. However, existing research shows that while the
employment figures in these organizations are in favor of men, the number of women, especially in
management positions, is quite low. Moreover, given that a significant portion of the organizations in
this sector are "start-ups", it is inevitable that FinTech founders are predominantly male. Economic and
social barriers to women's entrepreneurship make women especially discouraged and powerless to
enter this field. Considering women's social distance from technology and finance, and the normative
values attributed to entrepreneurship being masculine values, the relationship between this picture in
the FinTech ecosystem and gender becomes understandable. There have been many studies on women's
employment in the financial sector in Turkey, but there are very few scientific studies on FinTech
organizations, which are relatively new structures. Whether FinTech organizations offer equal
opportunities in the context of gender equality and whether they contribute positively to women's
employment has recently been a subject of debate. Both global and country-based data can be found
mainly in the reports of various private sector and non-governmental organizations. The purpose of
this study is to examine gender inequality in FinTech organizations in the context of employment. To
this end, a secondary literature review was conducted through scientific publications and reports, and
it was determined that despite the discourse of digitalization providing equalizing opportunities, the
rates of female employment and entrepreneurs in FinTech organizations remain at a very low level
compared to men.
Keywords: Financial Technologies (FinTech), women employment, women entrepreneurship, start-
up.

    GENDER EQUALITY, MASCULINITY AND PEACEBUILDING IN
             POST- CONFLICT ACEH, INDONESIA
                       1. Eka SRIMULYANI*, 2. SAMSIDAR†, 3. Cut Dewi UTHOR‡

Abstract
There has been a complex and complicated intersection among several issues regarding the concept of
masculinities and gender equality in a society like Aceh, Indonesia. The society has been known as
among a stronghold Muslim community in Indonesia, that has typical patriarchal traditions with some
matrifocal practices. Aceh had experienced a long-armed conflict (almost three decades) since 1976
which ended in 2005 after the signing of Helsinki peace Memorandum of Understanding. This armed
conflict has a serious impact on the society, politically as well as socially. The cases of violence, human
right abuses have been reported as the part of the history of the conflicts, some survived and a number
were also killed. The cases of violence occurred has an impact still on the society. with the support of
Sasakawa Peace Foundation and Integral Knowledge of Asia, a survey combined with some key
informant interviews and focused group discussion were conducted in some affected areas (Aceh
Besar, Aceh Tengah and Bener Meriah, Pidie and Pidie Jaya) in 2022 to observe how masculinity
intersected with other issues of violence and the challenge for promoting equality in the society. The
study also uncovers unsolved cases of violence, un-treated mental health issues among those survived
from the conflict both men and women. The complex masculinity problems that also intersected with
gender equality issues are discussed in this paper, there are some specific and contextual social
structure that influence the concept of masculinity and gender equality principles within a society both
conceptually and practically.
Keywords: Gender equality, masculinity, post conflict, Aceh Indonesia.

    INTERGENERATIONAL CONFLICT ON THE HEADSCARF AND
    RELIGIOUSNESS: THE STRUGGLE FOR 'UNVEILING' OF THE
                     NEW GENERATION
                                                    Elif ÖNER*

Abstract
While the era of ‘struggling’ to wear the headscarf is over in Turkey, a new generation of women of
religious background has started to ‘struggle’ over the headscarf again, this time for different reasons.
This paper focuses on young women’s conflicts with their families in the struggle to remove the
headscarf. Today, beyond the traditional representation but in parallel with the demand for freedom to
wear the headscarf, a discourse that emphasizes individual choices and freedom is emerging. In light
of the phenomenon of ‘unveiling’, young women are trying to redefine the headscarf and religiosity
for themselves in the global and digital world order. In contrast to the previous generation's insistence
on maintaining the ‘essentiality’ of the headscarf, the new generation of women instead advocates for
autonomy and liberation. In this context, women who want to remove their headscarves have started
to express their desire and process of unveiling on the online platform You Will Not Walk Alone. The
platform consists of letters shared anonymously and these letters describe women's relationships with
the headscarf they have worn or continue to wear against their will and the reactions, pressure, and
violence family members have subjected them to continue to veil. The letters point to a clash of
intergenerational perceptions and values between the young women who are forced to cover and the
parent or parents who forced them to cover. Within the scope of the study, 81 letters published on the
website yalnizyurumeyeceksin.com between 17.09.2023 and 26.01.2024 were analyzed. Through
content analysis, this study aims to understand the differences in the perspectives of the new generation
of women on the headscarf and religiosity and the reasons for these differences. In doing so, the study
questions whether the previous generation’s understanding of the headscarf and religiosity can retreat
from its ‘bully’ position depicted in the letters, and discusses the overlapping and diverging points of
the struggles for veiling and unveiling. In this respect, the study provides an analysis comparing the
headscarf and women’s religiosity from the perspectives of different generations.
Keywords: Headscarf, intergenerational conflict, You Will Not Walk Alone, unveiling, family
pressure, religiosity.

 WOMEN'S ACADEMIC JOURNEY: ANALYSIS AND COMPARISONS
              WITH BIBLIOMETRIC DATA
                          1. Elif TEKİN*, 2. Nagihan KARTAL†, 3. Nida ORUÇ‡

Abstract
Over time, many studies examining the presence of female academics in the academic field, gender
roles and discrimination have become the focus. In this context, issues such as increasing the self-
confidence of female academics, helping them balance their family and work lives, and strengthening
mentoring and support networks are prominent topics in addressing gender roles. The research
conducted in this context; it offers an in-depth analysis focusing on topics such as the concepts
emphasized by the authors in studies in different databases regarding the female workforce in the
academy, the relationships of the concepts with each other, trending words over the years, research
rates on authors by country and the annual increase percentages of the studies. Bibliometric analysis
was used through the R programming language to examine the studies between 1992 and 2023 in
Scopus and Web of Science (WoS) databases. Advanced searches were conducted in both databases
using keywords related to women and academia. While studies in both databases showed a slow
increasing trend between 2008 and 2018, there was a significant acceleration from 2018 to 2023. It is
observed that the biggest difference occurs in word clouds; it is seen that in both different clouds, the
focus is on words related to the field of health, which are superior in number of research. The trends
that have come to the fore in recent years are gender inequality and gender discrimination. In the
articles included in the bibliometric analysis, the efforts made for greater representation of women in
the academic field and the progress in gender equality are also emphasized, and it is observed in both
databases that concepts such as gender inequality, gender discrimination and gender equality have
come to the fore especially in recent years. These studies can constitute an important resource for
understanding and improving gender roles and the current situation of female academics.
Keywords: Female academics, bibliometric analysis, gender equality, gender.

        WOMEN'S POVERTY IN TURKEY: CAUSES, EFFECTS AND
                 STRATEGIES FOR COMBATING IT
                      1. Emine AVCU*, 2. Nilgün AVCI†, 3. Meryem METİNOĞLU‡

Abstract
In its most basic sense, it is a state of “poverty”, being “deprived” of the basic material means to meet
one's needs. In the most general sense, it is the inability to benefit from the goods and services required
to maintain a minimum level of living due to lack of income and the sense of social deprivation that
this situation brings with it. The issue of women's poverty has been included in the literature since the
1970s. The increase in women's poverty led to the concept of “Feminization of Poverty” and this
concept was first used by Diane Perarce. The “feminization of poverty” draws attention to the fact that
women are more likely to face poverty compared to men, that women's poverty is more serious than
men's poverty, and that poverty among women is increasing over time compared to poverty among
men. According to the World Bank 2020 report, there are more than one billion people living in extreme
poverty in the world and 70% of these poor people are women. Six out of every 10 of the poorest
people in the world are women. Although women make up more than 50% of the world's population,
they own only 1% of the world's assets. Women's poverty is a multidimensional concept and it is
insufficient to evaluate the problem only in its economic dimension. Because it also has social, political
and cultural dimensions. Deprivation in all these dimensions leads to the perpetuation of poverty.
Women experience poverty more in terms of education, health, occupation and participation in
decisions. Women are deprived of educational opportunities, do not have a profession, work in informal
sectors, are excluded from the scope of social security, are unprotected, do not benefit from the health
sector at an adequate level, experience more problems such as health risks, infectious diseases, chronic
diseases, and often have to continue their lives under the responsibility of being a wife and mother, and
poverty is seen as an inevitable result for women.
Keywords: Women, poor, poverty, struggle, strategy, impact.

  SOCIALIST ECOFEMINISM PERSPECTIVE IN CONTEMPORARY
                ART: WOMEN AND NATURE
                                               Emine GÜNDÜZ*

Abstract
The Industrial Revolution is considered one of the most transformative processes in history, and the
changes that took place during this period have played a significant role in shaping the modern world.
It has had profound impacts on the social, economic and environmental spheres, providing a critical
foundation for understanding many of the major issues of today's world. Solving such problems
requires a more just and sustainable society. In this context, important approaches such as ecofeminism
come into play. Socialist ecofeminism, a sub-branch of ecofeminism, critically examines the effects of
capitalism on the environment and on women and offers holistic solutions to these problems. This
approach aims to build a more sustainable future by promoting both environmental justice and gender
equality. Mary Mellor, one of the key figures of socialist ecofeminism, united socialism, feminism and
ecology in her 1993 book "Breaking the Boundaries: Toward a Feminist, Green Socialism" in 1993,
which combines socialism, feminism and ecology. In this book, Mellor focuses on gender,
environmental and economic justice and proposes a radical approach to these issues. She addresses the
domination of nature and women by capitalism and patriarchy and emphasizes that society must
achieve economic and ecological balance. Spirituality plays an important role in this movement,
because socialist ecofeminism goes beyond the social and economic levels to emphasize people's
emotional and spiritual connection with nature. This approach treats nature not only as a resource, but
also as an entity to be lived in integrity with. The meeting of the theory of socialist ecofeminism and
contemporary art, research on the works of artists in art history and the reflections of this theory in the
art world will be discussed. The presentation will present the significance and impact of socialist
ecofeminism in the art world from the perspective of socialist ecofeminism, along with the results of
the analysis and research conducted with the visuals of the artworks. This combination has raised
awareness of social and ecological issues and has enabled art to be used as a powerful tool of expression
and intervention.
Keywords: Ecofeminism, women, nature, art.

    EVALUATION OF POSTGRADUATE THESES ON GENDER AND
     GENDER INEQUALITY IN SPORT: YÖKTEZ AND PROQUEST
                        EXAMPLE
                    1. Emre BELLİ*, 2. Özlem Ece BAŞOĞLU†, 3. Şükran DERTLİ‡

Abstract
The aim of this study is to reveal the general profile of postgraduate theses in national and international
literature on gender and gender inequality in sport. At this point, postgraduate theses accessed through
ProQuestTM and Yöktez databases were evaluated. In this context, in the advanced scanning section
of the Yöktez database, firstly ‘sport and gender’ and secondly ‘sport and gender equality’ keywords
were used. In the first screening, 100 postgraduate theses and 22 postgraduate theses were reached in
the second screening. When the postgraduate theses were examined, it was determined that only 7 of
the 22 postgraduate theses obtained as a result of the second screening were not included in the first
screening, and a total of 107 studies were evaluated. In the same way, a total of 106 postgraduate theses
were reached as a result of advanced scanning in the form of (TS=(gender inequality in sport*)) AND
TS=(sport* social gender*) in title, abstract and keywords in the advanced scanning section of
ProQuestTM database. For these reasons, in this study, the data obtained from a total of 207 thesis
studies in the national and international literature in Yöktez and ProQuestTM databases were examined
by bibliometric analysis method. The status of postgraduate theses in Yöktez related to gender and
gender inequality in sport was analysed by using SPSS and word cloud programmes in terms of
‘academic level’, ‘gender’, ‘year’, ‘title’, ‘university-institute-major discipline’, ‘language of
publication’, ‘supervisor title’, ‘subject and number of subjects’, ‘keywords and number of keywords’,
‘number of pages’ and ‘access status’ parameters. In addition, the status of the postgraduate theses in
ProQuestTM was examined in VOSviewer programmes by performing bibliometric matching, citation
and co-citation analyses with other data obtained from Web of Science programmes with the
parameters of ‘academic level’, ‘year’, ‘language of publication’, ‘subject’, ‘university’ and ‘country-
region’. In this context, it was found that 78 of 107 postgraduate theses on gender and gender inequality
in sport in Yöktez were master's theses, 27 were doctoral theses and 2 were proficiency in art theses,
while 78 of 106 postgraduate theses in ProQuestTM were doctoral theses and 31 were master's theses.
However, it was determined that the postgraduate theses in Yöktez were prepared between 2005-2023
and the postgraduate theses in ProQuestTM were prepared between 1989-2023. It was found that the
publication languages of graduate theses were mostly Turkish in Yöktez and English in ProQuestTM.
It is suggested that bibliometric analyses should be conducted with data from different databases for
researchers who plan to study on the subject.
Keywords: Yöktez, ProQuest, sport, gender, gender inequality, bibliometrics.

  PLACE OF WOMEN IN SOCIETY IN THE 2ND CONSTITUTIONAL
    ERA IN THE HISTORICAL PROCESS AND IN THE LIGHT OF
                   İKTİSADİYAT MAGAZINE
                                                 Emrehan YÜCEL*

Abstract
One of the indicators of the level of development of a society is undoubtedly related to whether the
men and women who make up that society have equal rights. Although the Turks have undergone some
changes in this sense throughout the historical process, they have generally been a society where
women are respected and considered equal to men. Research conducted for the periods before the
adoption of Islam has shown that in the patriarchal family structure of the Turks, the father does not
have absolute authority and women and children also have a say in family life. Apart from this, it is
seen that women also have a say in state administration. Although this situation continued to a large
extent after the adoption of Islam, with the Great Seljuk period, the status of women in social life began
to change, men were brought to the fore and women were pushed into the background. This
understanding, which also spread to the Ottoman Empire, continued in the Tanzimat Reform Era, and
women regained some of their rights thanks to the innovations brought by the Tanzimat Edict. With
these rights, especially given within the modernization efforts of the Ottoman Empire, women were
able to receive education, become civil servants, and have a say in inheritance and divorce issues. II.
In line with the modernization movements that gained momentum with the Constitutional Monarchy,
Turkish women began to make their voices heard and exist in social life. It can be seen in the light of
the news published in "İktisadiyat Mecmuası", one of the important media organs of the period;
Markets were established for women and where women were employed, special trade courses were
given to women, women were trained in crafts such as tailoring, and even institutions such as the
"Kadınları Çalıştırma Cemiyet-i İslamiyesi " and the "İstihlak-ı Milli Kadınlar Cemiyeti" were
established. Through all these activities and established institutions, Turkish women have begun to
take their place in social life, especially through economic activities. The main purpose of this study is
to use the document analysis method, one of the qualitative research methods, and II. By discussing
the place of Turkish women in social life and the transformations experienced at this point in the
historical process until the Constitutional Monarchy Period, II. In the light of the evaluations of
"İktisadiyat Mucmuası", one of the important magazines of the Constitutional Monarchy Period, as
well as by using the press of the period, archive documents and copyrighted and research works,
information is given about the above-mentioned activities and organizations that enabled women to
take part in social and economic life during the Constitutional Monarchy Period.
Keywords: Woman, İktisadiyat Mecmuası, II. Constitutional era, social life, economic life.

                                                                                             Turkish Presentation




* Master's Student, Kırşehir Ahi Evran University, History, Türkiye, ORCID: 0009-0000-1498-7334, email:

emrehanycl@gmail.com

 WOMEN EDUCATION LEADER’S AGENCY AND EMPOWERMENT
          IN ENGLISH-RELATED DEPARTMENTS
                                                   Eser ÖRDEM*

Abstract
This phenomenological study explores the agency, empowerment, and narrative identity of five women
administrators who lead English language departments. Narrative identity theory suggests that
individuals form their identities based on the narratives they construct about their lives. This theoretical
approach, developed by psychologists such as Dan P. McAdams, posits that people create personal
narratives that imbue their experiences with meaning and continuity over time. These stories are not
static; they evolve as individuals encounter new experiences and challenges. Narrative identity theory
emphasizes the role of storytelling in how individuals see themselves and how they present themselves
to others. In the context of leadership, narrative identity can reveal how leaders perceive their roles,
make sense of their experiences, and articulate their values and goals, ultimately shaping their actions
and interactions within their professional domains. By employing thematic analysis, the research aims
to delve into the subjective experiences of these leaders to understand the dynamics of their
professional roles and personal identities within academic environments. The study focuses on how
these women construct their identities and exercise agency in their leadership positions, highlighting
the empowerment processes that support or challenge their professional trajectories. This investigation
into narrative identity offers insights into how personal histories, cultural contexts, and institutional
structures interplay in shaping their leadership styles and decisions. The findings aim to contribute to
the broader discourse on gender and leadership in academia, providing nuanced understandings of the
complexities faced by women in leadership roles.
Keywords: Narrative identity, women, empowerment, agency.

0001-9529-4045, email: eserordem@gmail.com

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

   EXPLOITATION OF WOMEN’S LABOR WITHIN THE CYCLE OF
                     REPRODUCTION
                                                Esin YALÇINTAŞ*

Abstract
According to Marxist theory, the concepts of production, reproduction and social reproduction
constitute the foundations of the capitalist system. Karl Marx attributes the continuation of the capitalist
system to the constant renewal of the production process and the continuation of the relations between
labor and capital by adapting to changing conditions. In this context, the famous thinker calls this
process the "reproduction" cycle. In feminist literature, the exploitation of women's labor emerges as
a fundamental issue at this point. The aim of this study is to make visible women and domestic labor,
which represent the non-material elements of the reproduction cycle, and to emphasize their place in
the reproduction process. The reproduction process that ensures the sustainability of the capitalist
system includes two main elements. The first element is the phase of renewal and development of the
means of production and production inputs (including the education system and cultural values). This
is often associated with male labor and has economic value. Secondly, it is the process of making the
workforce ready for production every day and producing new generations. At this point, women's labor
gains importance. According to Marxist theory, the labor undertaken by women in housework does not
turn into a material value in patriarchal capitalism, and this leads to the exploitation of women. The
scope of this study is women's labor in the process of reproduction and unpaid under the patriarchal
conditions of global capitalism. In the study, a literature review was conducted on the subject and local
and national data were used. Particularly in feminist literature, internationally published articles on
women's labor were used. According to Friedrich Engels, the realization of equality between men and
women will be possible by transforming housework into a social industry. This is a transformation that
can be achieved by increasing the visibility of women's labor in household work and ensuring their
economic value is recognized.
Keywords: Reproduction, women's labor, exploitation of women's labor.

                                                                                            Turkish Presentation

email: esinyalcintas@hotmail.com

        THE EFFECT OF DISASTERS ON WOMEN'S HEALTH AND
                      NURSING APPROACH
                               1. Esra GÖKDAĞ BALCI*, 2. Melike DİŞSİZ†

Abstract
Disasters are defined as earthquakes, floods, storms, and biological and chemical accidents that cause
loss of life and property for people, adversely affect society in physical, psychological, and economic
aspects, can be of human and technological origin and are generally unpredictable. The International
Disasters and Emergencies Database (EM-DAT) states that more than 26,000 disasters have occurred
in the world since 1900. Although the effects caused by disasters negatively affect all individuals,
gender-based inequalities affect women and children more, depending on social expectations, places
of social status, and biological characteristics. In disaster management, nurses have important roles
and responsibilities in maintaining and protecting women's health under all conditions. These are; pre-
disaster risk assessment, post-disaster situation assessment, emergency interventions at the time of
disaster, manager, researcher, educator, and caregiver roles in the fields of implementation of infection
control mechanisms. The purpose of this review; To examine the impact of disasters on women's health
and to raise the awareness of health professionals on the subject.In line with the studies conducted, it
has been determined that women are exposed to unwanted or unplanned pregnancies and reproductive
and sexually transmitted diseases due to sexual violence and poverty. After the disaster, serious
increases were found in lower genital tract infections, pelvic inflammatory disease, and menstrual
disorders. In this process, it has been reported that the use of modern birth control methods has
decreased and that unaffordable family planning services have increased. It has been determined that
women face various health and breastfeeding problems during pregnancy, childbirth, and postpartum
periods due to inadequate access to reproductive health services and materials. It has been found that
pregnancy complications such as premature birth, hypertension, mental problems, and respiratory
diseases are more common in pregnant women. It has been reported that the mortality rates of women
are higher than men during disasters in developing and undeveloped countries. It has been determined
that this rate difference is due to the problems experienced during pregnancy and breastfeeding periods.
In the World Disasters Report, it was reported that women were forced to have sexual intercourse to
meet their needs in disaster situations.It has been determined that women are at greater risk of sexual
and reproductive health problems during disaster periods due to situations such as forced marriage,
sexual violence, domestic violence, sexual abuse, and psychosocial problems at an early age. Since
women have more risk in disaster periods, it is important to integrate the roles and responsibilities of
women's health nurses specific to the field within the scope of disaster management steps.
Keywords: Disaster, nurse, women's health.

3915, email: melike.dissiz@sbu.edu.tr

                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                           Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

         WOMEN'S EMPLOYMENT ISSUE AND INTERNATIONAL
                   SOLUTION SUGGESTIONS
                                 1. Esra YILMAZ*, 2. Nurcan ÇAĞLAYAN†

Abstract
The global issue of women's employment is an issue related to the low participation of women in the
workforce and the persistence of inequalities around the world. Women are generally less employed
than men, receive lower wages, and do not benefit from promotion opportunities adequately. In the
study, the global women's employment problem, international mechanisms for this problem and
solutions at the national level were examined in the light of current evidence and literature.
Policymakers, employers and society as a whole need to work together to achieve gender equality and
encourage women's participation in the workforce. Women's access to educational opportunities should
be increased, support mechanisms should be provided to help them balance family responsibilities and
business life, and gender-based discrimination should be combated. An effective way to address the
problem of women's underrepresentation in the workforce is to implement gender quotas. These quotas,
which mandate gender diversity, aim to eliminate systemic barriers that prevent women from accessing
employment opportunities. In addition, international regulations play an important role in shaping the
structure of women's employment at the global level. Organizations such as the United Nations have
introduced conventions and agreements advocating gender equality in the workplace. The allocation
of public contracts can also have a significant impact when it comes to providing women with equal
opportunities for employment. Governments and other public institutions should promote gender
equality by awarding contracts to companies that demonstrate a commitment to diversity and gender
balance in their workforce. This encourages companies to comply with fair employment practices and
sends a strong message about the value of women's contribution to the economy. The problem of
women's employment at the global level is a multifaceted problem that requires a comprehensive and
coordinated approach. Societies can contribute to gender equality in the workforce by utilizing
mechanisms such as gender quotas, international regulations and public contracts. It is imperative for
cross-sectoral stakeholders to work together to create a more inclusive and equitable future for women
in the labor market.
Keywords: Women's employment, metaphor, international regulations, gender quotas, public
contracts.

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

  EVALUATION OF THE PROBLEMS FACED BY FEMALE SCHOOL
  ADMINISTRATORS IN THE CONTEXT OF GENDER INEQUALITY
                                                F. Simge YAVUZ*

Abstract
Masculine perspectives of societies cause some problems in women's business life as well as in their
social life. Although women are more involved in working life today than in the past, their numbers in
managerial positions are much less than men. As in other professions, the low rate of women in
education administrators is noteworthy. This situation is seen as a reflection of gender inequality in
societies on business life. This situation is affected by the fact that social expectations and roles
assigned to women are different from those of men. The fact that women's gender roles focus more on
motherhood and housework causes a limitation in the context of management. Inequalities pose a major
obstacle to the development of countries. According to research, the high level of welfare in societies
where equality between men and women is achieved shows that this situation directly affects social
progress. In this context, the aim of the research is to examine the problems faced by female school
administrators in the context of gender inequality. For this purpose, the research was conducted with
the phenomenology pattern, one of the qualitative research designs. The study group of the research
consists of 10 female school principals working at different levels in Çatalca and Büyükçekmece
districts of Istanbul. Easy sampling method was used when creating the study group. A semi-structured
interview form consisting of 3 questions was used to obtain research data. Interview questions were
asked to the school principals in the study group and voice recordings were made, and all of these
recordings were transferred to electronic media. The transferred data were examined using the content
analysis method. According to the data obtained from the interviews, the most common problems
experienced by the participants can be listed as work-family conflict, lack of support from superiors,
long working hours, male-dominated perspectives and discourses, excess responsibilities. It is clearly
seen that the basis of these problems is the effect of gender roles imposed on women. It has been
observed that placing family and child responsibilities on women causes female managers to be unable
to balance work and family life and to experience difficulties.
Keywords: Female administrator, gender, inequality, education administration.

simge.sensoy@gmail.com
