         MUSLIM WOMEN AND PHILANTHROPIC MOVEMENTS IN
              INDONESIA: AISYIYAH AND MUSLIMAT NU
       1. Fatimah ZUHRAH*, 2. KUSTINI†, 3. WARNIS‡, 4. Siti ATIEQOH§, 5. YUMASDALENI**

Abstract
This study explores the intimate relationship between Muslim women (Muslimah) and philanthropy in
Indonesia. With a female-majority population, Indonesia presents a unique landscape where women
hold social power and significant potential as donors and philanthropic actors. The research focuses on
the philanthropic movements of Muslim women's organizations, Aisyiyah (Muhammadiyah) and
Muslimat Nahdatul Ulama (NU). Both organizations operate autonomously, contributing significantly
to religious, social, educational, and economic spheres. Aisyiyah's Islamic movement emphasizes
"da'wah amar ma'ruf nahi munkar" (promoting good and forbidding evil), while Muslimat NU aims to
build a prosperous society based on Islamic principles and Indonesian national identity. The study
investigates the philanthropic efforts of these organizations towards prosperity in Indonesia and the
challenges they face. Employing a qualitative approach through document analysis, the research
reveals Aisyiyah's core foundation in philanthropy. All activities and funding stem from and benefit
members and society at large including a widespread network from regional to village levels, diverse
programs covering education, health, and economic empowerment, substantial assets like land
endowments, social welfare institutions, and the role of philanthropy in women's empowerment
through training and mentoring. Notably, Aisyiyah supports hundreds of educational and healthcare
institutions. Muslimat NU's philanthropic endeavors include social and health services, education, and
skills development. In the field of social and welfare, the NU Muslim Welfare Foundation was formed
to donate to orphanages, established hospitals, daycare centers, elderly homes, and established female
dormitories with programs on toddler development, immunization, and skill improvement for family
welfare. In terms of tangible philanthropy, the organization supports hundreds of kindergartens,
elementary schools, hospitals, and institutions. The research identifies cultural norms as a key
challenge. Despite recognizing the importance of women's participation, societal perceptions regarding
female leadership positions require further negotiation.
Keywords: Muslim women, philanthopy, Muslimat NU, Aisyiah Muhammadiyah, Indonesia.

SOCIALIST FEMINIST VOICE REGARDING WOMEN’S CAREER IN
               CARYL CHURCHILL’S PLAYS
                                     Fatma Aylin BAYRAKÇEKEN AKIN*

Abstract
Born in 1938, as one of the prominent post-war British playwrights and canons of women’s theatre,
Caryl Churchill is an advocator of women’s rights and reflects her socialist feminist perspective in her
plays exploring liberation of women which can only be achieved by building a career to put an end to
both economic and cultural sources of women’s oppression in a male dominated world. The paper will
discuss the challenges working women face in history and in the contemporary business life and
society. The focus of the paper will be on her Obie award winner 1982 play called “Top Girls” which
deals with women from different historical times and today, comparing and contrasting how they lose
their humanity to attain power in a patriarchal world order. In line with women from a variety of
professions, Churchill draws attention to the hegemony of male directors and actors in theatre and
deliberately makes use of female cast even for male characters to increase job opportunities for female
actors. The protagonist Marlene who abandoned her home, family and daughter to be a successful
career woman celebrates her promotion in a surrealist scene with historical, iconic and fictional women
with great stature. The aim, is to present how Churchill reflects, the price women have to pay for the
sake of being successful professionally while sacrificing motherhood, family, private life and thus
becoming iron ladies and losing feminine qualities. Paradoxically, the period the play was published
coincides with Thatcher’s right wing politics which favors the wealthy minority women despising the
less fortunate, resulting in a capitalist success. The method of delivery will be an overview of socialist
feminism, illustrations from the play and the playwright’s conclusion: While criticizing capitalism and
patriarchy, Churchill advocates social and economic justice in line with gender, class and power,
pointing out to the fact that true liberation of women can only be achieved by solidarity and collective
struggle.
Keywords: Socialist feminism, equality, suffering.

                 SEXUAL HEALTH IN WOMEN WITH DIABETES
                                1. Fatma ÖZKAL *, 2. Öznur ADADIOĞLU †

Abstract
Diabetes is a chronic metabolic disease characterized by elevated blood glucose levels due to
deficiency, ineffectiveness, or a combination of both of the insulin hormone. According to 2021 data,
approximately 537 million adults aged 20-79 are grappling with diabetes worldwide. This number is
projected to reach 643 million by 2030 and 783 million by 2045. Diabetes, a serious global health
issue, leads to significant complications such as retinopathy, end-stage kidney failure, coronary artery
disease, stroke, and lower limb amputations, thereby forming a substantial disease burden. Diabetes
and its associated complications significantly reduce individuals' quality of life. One of the less-
discussed complications that adversely affect quality of life is sexual health, which is equally prevalent
in both genders. In diabetic women, dyspareunia may occur due to decreased hydration of the vaginal
mucous membranes or increased vaginal infections due to hyperglycemia, decreased genital arousal
may result from vascular damage and neuropathy, or a reduction in sexual desire may occur due to the
burden of the disease causing stress or depression. The prevalence rates of sexual dysfunction in pre-
and post-menopausal women with type 1 diabetes range from 27% to 71%, while in women with type
2 diabetes, it ranges from 42% to 53%. A recent meta-analysis has shown that sexual dysfunction is
more prevalent in women with any type of diabetes, with a risk 2.02 times higher compared to the
control group. In one study, it is noted that diabetic women experience vaginal infections (77.9%),
dyspareunia (51.2%), and general orgasm problems (57.4%). In another study, the prevalence of sexual
dysfunction in diabetic women was reported as 58.95% according to the random effects model. In
conclusion, sexual dysfunction, one of the overlooked complications of diabetes, adversely affects the
quality of life of diabetic individuals and can lead to intrafamilial problems and conflicts. Therefore,
more research should be conducted on this issue, and special attention should be paid to including
education and informative content for diabetic individuals.
Keywords: Diabetes, women, sexual health.

     THE RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN WOMEN AND SUSTAINABLE
           DEVELOPMENT: BIBLIOMETRIC ANALYSIS
                                        Ferda NAKIPOĞLU ÖZSOY*

Abstract
The United Nations has called for action for a sustainable future by the end of 2030, consisting of a
total of 17 main headings and including sustainable development goals. The aim of the study is to
reveal the current trends in women's studies in the literature, especially under the headings of gender
equality, quality education, end to poverty, decent work and economic growth, among these
interconnected goals. On the other hand, it is seen that the literature focuses on bibliometric analysis
of gender equality, especially in line with sustainable development goals. For this reason, in the study,
basic theoretical and empirical studies were examined using the keywords sustainable development,
poverty, gender, women and education, and an attempt was made to present a summary of the existing
article using bibliometric analysis with quantitative data and to identify the gaps that emerged in the
literature on the concepts. In this regard, various published studies in the Web of Science database
covering the years 1994-2024 were examined. When we look at the distribution of 146 studies
according to the years they were published, it was determined that the most were in 2020 (15 studies),
2019 (13 studies), 2021 and 2023 (12 studies). The publication type is mainly article (117) and from
the perspective of Sustainable Development Goals, the most published main titles out of 17 main titles
are No Poverty (64 studies), Good Health and Well Being (42 studies), Reduced Inequality (27 studies),
Industry Innovation and Infrastructure (21 studies) and Zero Hunger and Gender Equality (18 studies).
While it was determined that the most working areas were in the field of economics (19) and public,
environment and occupational health (18), according to the keyword analysis, the most used keywords
are poverty with 20 repetitions, gender, and entrepreneurship with 9 repetitions, and women, education,
and sustainable development with 6 repetitions.
Keywords: Poverty, gender, women, education, sustainable development.

nakipoglu@gantep.edu.tr

 AN ANALYSIS OF INTEGRATION IN EDUCATION FROM A SOCIO-
 CULTURAL PERSPECTIVE: THE CASE OF THE ROMA CHILDREN
              1. Feyza KARAGÖZ*, 2. Deniz TOMBAKOĞLU†, 3. Rukiye GÖNÜLTAŞ‡

Abstract
With a population of 10 to 12 million, the Roma are described as 'Europe's largest ethnic minority', and
in Turkey they are also known as an ethnic group living in serious social, cultural and economic
disadvantages. In the field of education in particular, while school offers many opportunities for Roma
children to avoid being a pariah as the next generation of society, to better integrate into society, to
discover personal development and talents, to improve health and well-being, to combat
discrimination, and to participate and lead in society, the system seems to be lacking in integrating
Roma children into education. According to studies, the low enrollment rates of Roma from basic
education onwards and the problem of exclusion have a negative impact on their educational life. The
difficulties experienced by students of Roma origin in the educational process have been highlighted
as an important problem area in academic studies. Despite the availability of free education, it is
observed that Roma children, even if they attend school, face various obstacles due to economic
conditions that prevent them from meeting their other needs, problems of integration with teachers and
peers, lack of role models and widespread prejudices, and as a result, their academic achievement
decreases. Research shows that Roma children's decision to continue school after primary school is
often determined by their own preferences. This suggests that when they reach high school age, Roma
children are more likely to pursue a career in labor rather than education. This study focuses on the
Roma in Kocaeli, a group little known in the literature. The aim of the study is to identify the barriers
to educational participation of Kocaeli Roma, who are seen as a disadvantaged ethnic group, in line
with the views of teachers and to develop solutions for the problems they face. Through semistructured
interviews, the qualitative research aims to engage with and understand the views of teachers in schools
with a high density of Roma students in Kocaeli. This study also aims to develop solutions by
examining the existing literature, postgraduate theses and scientific articles published abroad.
Keywords: Roma children, integration, education, disadvantaged groups, teacher views.

FEMALE AVATARS OF ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: AN ANALYSIS
             IN THE CONTEXT OF GENDER
                                1. Fikriye ÇELİK*, 2. Ömer AYDINLIOĞLU†

Abstract
Gender, which refers to the gender roles imposed on the individual by society rather than the gender
roles that the individual is born with, seems to have carried the female identity constructed in daily life
to the virtual environment. The identity construction practice in question posits women in a way that
depends on their real life conditions and characteristics. This practice, the first examples of which we
have recently witnessed in the business world, has revealed itself in the form of female characters
designed using artificial intelligence technology. These virtual characters, which are completely
artificial intelligence products, perform real human activities. It is seen that these female avatars, which
copy real human behaviours, display a profile suitable for the beauty criteria of modern societies. These
avatars, which stand out with their appeal to the eye and pleasure-orientation, do not construct a new
female identity, but mediate the reproduction of the existing gender perception. In this study, content
analysis was used to examine avatars produced by various organisations as advertising and marketing
tools in the context of existing gender norms. In the study based on the detailed analysis of the female
profile drawn in the virtual world, Instagram phenomena Aitana Lopez and Virtual Alin and Idea, the
female avatar of Evidea, were included in the research by purposive sampling. The study is extremely
important in terms of revealing that avatars, which emerge as a result of identity creation in the virtual
world and are accepted as candidates to replace real people, are produced in accordance with social
norms and gender patterns. The fact that the gender theme, which has a widespread appearance in the
literature, is addressed through a unique and up-to-date sample within the scope of this research makes
a significant contribution to the research. As a result of the study, it was found that virtual female
characters, which are portrayed as objects of spectacle and objects of pleasure with a profit-oriented
approach, comply with the stereotypes of women produced in the real world and are used as a means
of persuading the consumer.
Keywords: Communication studies, artificial intelligence, gender, avatar, influencer, stereotype.

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

         TRANS ACTIVISM FOR BODILY LIBERATION IN JORDY
           ROSENBERG'S CONFESSIONS OF THE FOX (2018)
                                                 Furkan TOZAN*

Abstract
The civil rights movements of the twentieth century yielded some positive results in various aspects of
minority rights, status, well-being, and protection and have continued well into the twenty-first century
with increased political vigor and participation. Apart from struggles for equal legal recognition in such
areas as systems of education, healthcare, and law the civil rights movements have cemented their
communities into more connected, informed, and conscious political bodies. Enjoying unique
privileges often denied to other areas of representation literature offers historically ostracized groups a
means of self-expression that is not as easily sequestrable by governmental or cultural concentrations
of authority which might seek to undermine such groups' efforts for the betterment of their status.
Engagements in literature with issues regarding in particular LGBTQ+ people are closely indicative of
this commensal relationship between literature and disadvantaged/vulnerable groups. Jordy
Rosenberg's 2018 historiographic metafictional novel Confessions of the Fox is a contemporary
example of trans representation in literature based on an eighteenth-century English thief and serial
jailbreaker Jack Sheppard who captured the popular imagination and caused a favourable cultural
hysteria. The public reception of Jack Sheppard as a hero and—upon his eventual execution at the age
of 22—as a martyr can be best understood in terms of the overwhelming sympathy for his youthfully
ambitious escapade to rise above the poverty he was born into. His short but turbulently eventful life
has been adapted into a multitude of media since before his execution extending into the twenty-first
century with further adaptations in film and fiction. Jordy Rosenberg utilizes this historical figure as
well as the near-manic public endorsement as the formative material for his protagonist in Confessions
of the Fox. Reimagined as a transgender man Rosenberg's Jack Sheppard embodies both the historicity
of the real Sheppard's struggles and the fictionalized struggles of a transgender man who tries to come
to terms with his gender identity. The novel unites the two experiences—and the two versions of
Sheppard—in a somatographical account of being a transgender man, ultimately showcasing the
intricacies of the embodied experience of confinement in and liberation from gaols of the state, the
body, and the mind.
Keywords: Historiographic metafiction, literary somatography, postmodern novel, transgender,
LGBTQ+, body politics.

furkantozan@topkapi.edu.tr

                           Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

   PHOENIXES IN CONFLICT, DISASTERS AND PEACE BUILDING
                                                Füsun ÖZERDEM*

Abstract
Discriminatory gender norms and social structures are the basis of gender inequality and explain why
women are made more vulnerable than men in many areas of life. In times of crisis, when family and
social structures and institutions are disrupted or destroyed, gender norms may be affected and gender
inequality increases. The struggle for gender equality is more prevalent in both developed and
developing countries, but reports published by various institutions indicate that women and girls are
affected worse than men when countries struggle with crises. Climate change and the socioeconomic
effects of the Covid-19 pandemic, added to the effects of conflicts and natural disasters, further deepen
gender inequalities, especially against women and girls. Sexual violence is another disturbing trend
noted in United Nations (UN) reports. The UN Conflict-Related Sexual Violence 2023 Report notes
that sexual violence continues to lead to both internal and cross-border displacement, with displaced
and refugee women and girls facing higher risks. At least one in five female refugees has experienced
sexual violence and its effects, including trauma, stigma, poverty, poor health and unwanted pregnancy.
Although it is stated that the actual figures are “probably much higher” due to the difficulty of
monitoring data and under-reporting of incidents, the report also highlights that “sexual violence is
used as a war tactic.” There are many predictions that ensuring greater gender equality and including
more women in leadership positions will reduce the likelihood of violent conflicts and will also greatly
increase the possibility of peaceful resolution of existing conflicts. Research shows that countries with
greater gender equality are more likely to resolve conflicts without violence and less likely to use
military force to resolve international disputes. Concerted efforts to increase the number of women in
conflict prevention, mediation and resolution efforts include UN Security Council Resolution 1325,
adopted in October 2000, ensuring that women can improve outcomes in conflict prevention and
peacebuilding. While this study reveals the situation of women affected by conflicts and disasters based
on internationally accepted reports despite data limitations, it will also try to explain the effectiveness
of women and girls rising from the ashes to prevent or stop conflict and subsequently build long-lasting
peace, despite all difficulties.
Keywords: Women in conflicts, women's role in peacebuilding, UN practices.

                                                                                               Turkish Presentation




* Prof. Dr., Muğla Sıtkı Koçman University, Political Science and International Relations, Türkiye, ORCID: 0000-0002-

8204-8635, email: fusunozerdem@mu.edu.tr


                                                           74
                      International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                         Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

      THE BANALITY OF NATIONALIST POLITICAL DISCOURSE:
       DUALITY OF MASCULINE HERO AND FEMININE ENEMY
                                                Gizem ORÇİN*

Abstract
The gendered public sphere, which was transferred to the modern world as the legacy of the ancient
world, still exists in many institutional mechanisms. This historical form of inequality, shifting from
politics to law, the media and many other fields, is hidden in male discourse. Despite the politics of the
modern world justifying this unequal and sexist structure, politics remains far from being inclusive for
women. In this sense, the policy-making process, based on male political personalities, can be defined
as a field of narrow inclusivity and lacking solutions to women’s problems. Although politicians often
produce policies about women, families and children with sexist speeches, they cannot prevent the
continuation of issues in this area. The long-term goals of politics are still based on the male-dominated
form of discourse that has become inherent in the nationalist movement. Especially, the male
establishment of nationalist ideology is often neglected in studies that analyze the nationalist movement
and discourse in Turkey’s political agenda. The aim of this study is to enhance the questioning that is
absent in the literature by focusing on sexist themes in the political discourse of nationalism. This study
deals with the sexist language and discourse of the nationalist ideology, which presents a sharp, clear
and linear example of the male-dominated positioning of the political discourse. To that effect, the
discourse of nationalist ideology will be analyzed through its objectives in 2023, which is a decisive
electoral period for Turkish politics. The sample of the study is Türkgün newspaper, in which the
nationalist ideology is heavily dominant. This study will examine the sexist discourse mechanism of
news in Türkgün, which is limited to the 2023 discourses of nationalist political actors. The sexist
discourse of nationalism, which is a political structure that produces sexist distinctions in the political
arena, will be analyzed within the framework of Ruth Wodak’s discourse analysis method. The study
shows that in the 2023 ideal of the nationalist ideology, sexist ideals are preserved and identities other
than national ideals are heavily criticized. As a result of the study, some discourse patterns that have
become commonplace in nationalist political discourse were revealed. These include the identification
of the political subject, the actor and hero of the nationalist movement, with masculine elements, and
the oppositional and critical subject, as the enemy and feminine.
Keywords: Nationalism, nationalist discourse, gender, masculine hero, feminine enemy.

                                                                                         Turkish Presentation




* Dr., Independent Researcher, Türkiye, ORCID: 0000-0002-2740-7236, email: gizemorcin@gmail.com




                                                       75
                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

   WOMEN IN ARCHITECTURE: DO WE KNOW WHO THEY ARE?
                1. Gizem SEYMEN*, 2. Şölen KÖSEOĞLU†, 3. Semiha İSMAİLOĞLU‡

Abstract
As a result of women's struggle for equal rights, women began to be accepted to architecture schools
for the first time in many countries between the late 1800s and the early 1900s. The first studies on
women architects were conducted in the USA in the 1970s, and later, in the 1990s, many books were
published in the USA and England that deepened research on the subject. Initially, studies were
conducted only on women architects in English-speaking countries, but in the 2000s, studies began to
be conducted on women in other countries as well. Considering all these efforts and the thousands of
years of history of the field, despite the very new studies in the field, educational curricula in
architecture schools preserve their old structures. Both the media and architectural education do not
include women as much as they do men. Research shows that there are more women academics than
men in architecture schools in Turkey today. Despite this, when we look at the authors of the books
taught in schools, it is evident that the overwhelming majority belong to male authors. In addition, in
many architecture schools in Turkey, the number of female students has been higher than the number
of male students for decades. For these reasons, the aim of the study is to investigate the recognition
of women architects among undergraduate architecture students. For this purpose, within the scope of
the study, the survey method was determined, and a survey form containing open-ended questions was
created. A total of 80 students, 20 students from each level, participated in the study, which was
conducted among undergraduate students of Erzurum Atatürk University, Faculty of Architecture and
Design, Department of Architecture. The survey was conducted electronically via Google Forms due
to its ease of access and application. In the form, students were asked to give their gender, class
information, the names of architects that come to mind when "women architect" is mentioned, and an
example of a women architect-building pairing. It was seen in the study that almost all of the students
included a single name: Zaha Hadid. Students knew foreign architects more than Turkish architects.
As a result of the study, awareness of women architects was discussed among students studying
architecture, and evaluations were made based on the names that emerged.
Keywords: Women in architecture, gender, feminism, recognition, representation.

                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                           Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

     SWEDEN’S FEMINIST FOREIGN POLICY: IMPLICATIONS ON
                 INTERNATIONAL POLITICS
                                                      Gökhan AK*

Abstract
International politics are getting worse and worse by each year since main jurisdictional documents
agreed by many States are ignored in what way or another. It is due to the fact that great Powers such
as USA, UK, EU, China, France try to conduct international relations in the direction of their own
policies using their main political ideologies like liberalism, communism and so on. Therefore, many
countries in the globe began not to believe or rely on the fair and virtious verdicts and implications of
international politics in the last 20-30 years of time. However, Sweden as a state that adopted social
democracy in governmental political regime nearly for a century is a unique example not only for this
long-lasting state ideology type, but also interestingly unique as well since in 2015, Sweden’s foreign
affairs minister boldly acclaimed that the state had a feminist foreign policy, with rights, representation,
and resources at its core. Feminism is a movement consisting of various ideologies, social movements
and mass organizations aimed at eliminating inequalities in order to recognize the rights of women and
protect these rights. However, the implications of this political ideology on international politics in the
case of Sweden will be very exciting to examine since the symbiotic relationship between masculinity
and militarism and with militarism and the state. This androcentric view of international politics does
not adequately address the ways in which women’s lives affect and are affected by foreign policy
decisions. This study will outline some preliminary answers to these questions by discussing the
conceptual basis for evaluating various elements of a feminist foreign policy, arguing for an
intersectional, global approach to feminist foreign policy in the context of Swedish case. We will
conclude this exploratory discussion with some preliminary recommendations about how to improve
such a conceptualization and assessment. Main possible outcome of this study will become that
contemplating feminism in foreign politics will act as an “oasis” for a more secure international
relations. This study is mainly adopted the qualitative method of research, and hence will exploit
document analysis, content analysis and hermeneutic as scientific research methods.
Keywords: Sweden, feminism, foreign policy, international politics.

     ANALYSIS OF POSTGRADUATE THESES WRITTEN ON THE
    RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN CINEMA AND WOMEN IN TURKEY
                                               Gökhan GÜLTEKİN*

Abstract
Academic studies on the relationship between cinema and women started in the mid-1970s. Laura
Mulvey, who is considered the pioneer of these studies, discussed how women are represented in
cinema and developed the "gaze" theory in her work titled Visual Pleasure and Narrative Cinema. As
a matter of fact, many studies on the relationship between women and cinema in the following years
focused on how women are represented in cinema, citing Mulvey as an example. In her work, Mulvey
argued that the masculine and voyeuristic gaze comes in three forms: The camera's gaze, the gaze of
the film characters and the gaze of the audience. Thus, she also discussed how social roles are
reproduced and how the masculine view is imposed on the audience. However, the research on the
relationship between cinema and women, which started to gain momentum with Mulvey, has not gone
beyond revealing the representation of women in cinema. For example, it is very difficult to come
across reception studies that include audience perceptions of women's representation. Whereas the
"viewer's gaze" emphasized by Mulvey should have actually paved the way for such a field. Similarly,
it is almost impossible to come across a phenomenological research on women in the cinema industry
as actress, directors, make-up artists, etc. It can be argued that, especially in Turkey, there is still a
shallow perspective in studies on the relationship between cinema and women. Therefore, depending
on all these problems, the aim of this research is to examine the postgraduate theses written in Turkey
on the relationship between women and cinema, to discuss the deficiencies and to make some
suggestions. For this purpose, document analysis was used in the study. 18 theses found as a result of
the search conducted on the website of the National Thesis Center with the keywords "cinema and
women", "women in cinema", "women in films" and "films and women" were included in the research.
No studies such as reception analysis, interviews, experiments, etc. have been found regarding how
women represented in cinema are perceived. As a result, during the postgraduate period, which is
perhaps the first steps of the academic process, the direction of studies on the relationship between
cinema and women needs to be changed and different perspectives should be supported.
Keywords: Cinema and woman, postgraduate theses, document analysis.

                                                                                              Turkish Presentation

cinegultekin@gmail.com

A STUDY ON İSMAİL GASPIRALI'S ÂLEM-İ NİSVAN MAGAZINE IN
        THE CONTEXT OF THE JADIDISM MOVEMENT
                          1. Gökmen KANTAR*, 2. Gülberk Tuğba DEMİRBAY†

Abstract
In ancient Turks, unlike the social structures and cultures of the period, women were held in high
esteem both within the family, in state organizations and at all levels of society; He was in a position
of respect and importance. As a result of a nomadic life, constantly changing geographical conditions
and a number of different cultural interactions, the value seen by women in society has moved away
from the level in the old Turkish states. However, in Turkish culture, the view that women have always
been an indispensable element of the family and therefore a building block in the development of the
nation and the transmission of culture has never changed. While the long process that prepared World
War I in the modern period will now take place gradually; There was confusion in every country and
nation. The Turks, who succeeded in establishing many states throughout history, faced the collapse of
these states and their empires that ruled the continents when looking at this success from another
perspective. For this reason, Turkish intellectuals living both within the Ottoman Empire, the most
important representative of the Turkish-Islamic world of the modern period, and in other countries,
produced different ideas to seek a solution to this situation. This climate of thought aims to awaken
national consciousness among the Turks living under the rule of Tsarist Russia; It also found its
response in the representatives of the Jadidism Movement. The Jadidism movement, pioneered by
İsmail Gaspiralı, started as an education reform and later continued to grow with its economic, political
and social effects. Gaspirali puts forward the principle of unity in language, idea and work; She
published the Tercüman newspaper and, in addition, the Âlem-i Nisvan magazine for women. This
magazine, which aims to enable Turkish women to be conscious, educated individuals with national
values, continued its publication life with an innovative perspective in order to raise awareness beyond
the traditional roles attributed to women. In this study, by examining the publications of Âlem-i Nisvan
magazine, the first women's magazine published in the Turkish-Islamic world, to make women's
identity visible in Turkish culture; The importance of the magazine in the Turkish World of the period
will be emphasized.
Keywords: Turkish culture, Jadidism, İsmail Gaspiralı, Âlem-i Nisvan, women.

      DETERMINATION OF GENDER EQUALITY ATTITUDES OF
     TEKİRDAĞ NAMIK KEMAL UNIVERSITY ACADEMIC STAFF
                          1. Gökmen KANTAR*, 2. Nurcan YURTTAŞ DURAK†

Abstract
This study aims to investigate the attitudes of academic staff at the university regarding gender roles
and the factors influencing these attitudes. One of the primary objectives of this study is to draw
attention to gender inequality against women in all stages of social life, particularly in academia, and
to foster awareness of gender equality. Despite extensive research and activism over the years, gender
equality remains an ongoing issue, making this study potentially impactful in advancing social gains
in this area. Numerous universities and related institutions have conducted studies and devised action
plans on gender equality. However, these efforts have primarily focused on academic staff, leaving a
gap in understanding the attitudes of academics who shape the future direction in this regard. Therefore,
this study is expected to fill this void in the literature. The main goal of this research is to identify the
perception of gender among academic staff at Namık Kemal University in Tekirdağ and to generate
solutions based on the findings obtained. Analyzing the concepts of gender and sexual orientation as
analytical categories within the research framework will help in understanding the role, effects, and
determinants of gender in social relationships. Establishing an understanding of gender equality is
crucial for addressing inequalities reflected in socio-economic and political indicators. Especially, there
is a pressing need for efforts to improve the social and economic status of women. This research aims
to lay the groundwork for addressing gender equality by first identifying the current situation and
perception of gender, followed by proposing steps for action based on this assessment. The descriptive
research will encompass academic staff serving during the 2023-2024 academic year at Namık Kemal
University in Tekirdağ. The sample will consist of academics selected through non-probabilistic
sampling who voluntarily participate in the study. Data collection will involve the use of a "Descriptive
Information Form" and "Gender Equality Scale." Statistical analyses, including mean, standard
deviation, percentile distributions, independent samples t-test, and one-way ANOVA, will be
conducted to evaluate the data and derive concrete recommendations for addressing gender equality
gaps and issues.
Keywords: Social gender, gender equality, gender inequality, social gender perspective.

  ROMA WOMEN'S ACCESS TO EMPLOYMENT: ANALYSING THE
        EXPERIENCES OF İSTANBUL AND KOCAELİ
                      1. Göksen YAVUZ*, 2. Peri ORUN†, 3. Semanur ERDOĞAN‡

Abstract
T It is evident that women encounter a multitude of challenges in the contemporary era. Traditional
social norms, the association of domestic work with the private sphere, child and elderly care
responsibilities, and gender-based discrimination are among the factors that contribute to women's
marginalisation in the labour market. Due to factors such as education and ethnicity, individuals in this
group have limited access to employment opportunities and face difficulties in securing their sources
of income. Women workers face challenges in the labour market due to factors such as care work,
social norms and gender inequality. The absence of intersectional planning in the development of
women's employment is an important factor in the creation of more vulnerable female-dominated
groups and the subsequent marginalisation of their specific situation. The labour market position of
women in Roma society is particularly vulnerable and exemplifies the most extreme forms of precarity
and insecurity. A review of the literature on Roma women's access to livelihoods reveals a dearth of
studies addressing ethnic-based discrimination. This study aims to explore Roma women's experiences
of discrimination in the employment sector. A qualitative approach will be employed to gain insight
into the challenges Roma women face in the workplace and identify the underlying causes of these
challenges. The study will examine Roma women's experiences in the labour market, including
recruitment, sector preferences and the impact of ethnic discrimination on their careers. Furthermore,
the study will assess the impact of public policies on Roma women's employment, including the
prevalence of underlying prejudices, the impact of educational qualifications on employment
opportunities, and the sectors in which Roma women are employed. Roma communities are dispersed
throughout Turkey, with the majority residing in urban areas. The cities of Istanbul and Kocaeli are
notable for their high population density. These cities have been observed to exhibit a high degree of
industrialisation, which has led to Roma communities assuming various roles in the manufacturing
sector. The research covers Roma communities in Kocaeli and Istanbul, where respondents are
currently residing, have a history of active participation in the labour market, or are unable to access
public services due to restrictions.
Keywords: Gender, intersectionality, employment, Roma, segregationism, women.

  WOMEN IN THE CYCLE OF DRUG ADDICTION AND VIOLENCE:
                THE IMPACT OF GENDER
                            1. Günseli DOĞAN*, 2. Aslıhan Ardıç ÇOBANER †

Abstract
Structural factors and inequalities arising from gender roles are effective in the initiation of substance
use, substance use patterns and treatment between men and women. At many stages of the substance
addiction process, a connection is established between the violence and trauma experienced by women
throughout their lives and substance use. In the literature, it is emphasized that the effects of neglect,
abuse and violence experienced by many women who use substances in their personal histories have
an impact on their addictions. In fact, it is noted that substance use often serves as the best coping
mechanism available to individuals. Additionally, these studies emphasize the aspect of violence
resulting from gender roles. However, scientific studies on substance-addicted women in Turkiye are
limited. This research aims to understand the experiences of violence (the types of violence they
encounter and the impact of this violence on their addiction processes) that substance user/substance
addict women in Turkey encounter before and during the substance addiction process. The study was
designed according to the phenomenological (interpretive) approach, which is one of the qualitative
research methods. The participants of the research were determined by the snowball technique, one of
the purposeful sampling methods, and included 13 women over the age of 18 who reside in Ankara
and Mersin provinces and have a history of substance addiction, and one hundred women in alcohol
and substance addiction treatment centers (AMATEM) and Green Crescent Counseling Centers
(YEDAM) in these provinces. It was conducted through face-to-face and online in-depth interviews.
The data were analyzed with the inductive thematic analysis technique, proceeding through thematic
coding and classifications, using the MAXQDA Analytics Pro (24.1.0) program. While gender roles
create obstacles for women to exist in social life, they open a more restrictive space for them in social
life, and this perspective can create various grievances for women. In other words, gender behaviors
that are deemed inappropriate for women by society lead to violence, accusation, exclusion or
condemnation against women. In addition, substance use, which is often deemed inappropriate for
women, may occur due to the violence and negativities experienced by the woman using substances.
Keywords: Drug addiction, violence, women, gender.

THE JOURNEY OF WOMEN LEADERS IN THE BANKING SECTOR:
              OBSTACLES AND SOLUTIONS
                                      1. Hande DURU*, 2. Dilek ÇINAR†

Abstract
The banking sector, which has an important place in the skilled labor market, is an area of intense
competition, long working hours and frequent overtime. Although the sector is tried to be taken under
control with various sanctions, the regulations are not sufficient. Another important problem is that the
difficulties faced by women in this sector are remarkable. The sector is the lifeblood of economic
activities by covering various financial services. However, the difficulties faced by women in this
sector should not be ignored. The obstacles women face in their advancement to bank management
positions are often associated with gender-based discrimination and career differentiation. Invisible
barriers, referred to as the "glass ceiling", limit women's advancement to senior management positions
and these barriers can reduce women's motivation and hinder their career development. Work-family
conflict, inequality of opportunity and internal barriers have an important place among the individual
and organizational factors faced by women managers. Women's roles and responsibilities within the
family can create barriers to their career endeavors. It is also common for women to face gender-based
discrimination in the workplace and for their career development to be restricted. Organizational
culture and policies also affect women's advancement to managerial positions. Traditionally male-
oriented cultures may hinder women's advancement and women may have to work harder to establish
themselves within the organization. In addition, discrimination against women in the hiring process
and career development can also limit women's career opportunities. This study provides a detailed
analysis of the problems faced by women managers in the banking sector through a literature review
and discusses solutions. The challenges faced by women in the process of rising to leadership positions
are discussed with an emphasis on gender bias in organizational culture, lack of mentoring, inequality
of opportunity and internal barriers. It also discusses proposed solutions and policies to address these
issues. In this way, it is aimed to raise awareness about gender equality and women's access to
leadership opportunities in the banking sector and to contribute to the creation of a fair working
environment.
Keywords: Organizational culture, corporate culture, glass ceiling syndrome, gender discrimination,
career barriers.

THE JOURNEY OF WOMEN LEADERS IN THE BANKING SECTOR:
              OBSTACLES AND SOLUTIONS
                                1. Hanım GÜLER*, 2. Beyza Nur DOLAR†

Abstract
Although education holds the potential to overcome geographical, socio-economic, and gender
inequalities, these opportunities are not equally accessible to everyone. Providing education in various
geographical regions of Turkey presents special challenges for female teachers, as well as occasional
opportunities. This study aims to explore the experiences of female teachers practicing their profession
in challenging geographical conditions. The research is based on in-depth analysis of data collected
through semi-structured interviews. This method allows participants to express their perspectives,
experiences, and challenges in their own words, thereby providing a roadmap for potential
improvement efforts. During the research process, semi-structured interviews will be conducted with
female teachers identified using snowball sampling method. These interviews aim to deeply understand
the challenges faced by teachers, such as geographical isolation, limited access to resources, gender
norms, and professional development opportunities in the areas where they work. The content analysis
of the data will provide rich information about the challenges faced by teachers, their coping strategies,
professional resilience, and roles within the community. Additionally, recommendations will be
developed for the development of policies and practices that promote equal opportunities in education.
This study will provide valuable insights into how education policies and practices can be optimized
within the framework of challenging geographical conditions and gender equality. Ultimately, by
leveraging the experiences of female teachers, this study will highlight the challenges, advantages, or
disadvantages in developing strategies to increase educational equity in Turkey and other regions
facing similar geographical challenges. This study represents an important step towards increasing
educational equity and creating a more inclusive and fair education system by considering the
experiences of female teachers. It will shed light on future research and initiatives on the subject.
Keywords: Challenging geography, female teacher, equality, gender.

 THE ROLE OF WOMEN LEADERS IN CRISIS MANAGEMENT: AN
 EXAMINATION IN THE CONTEXT OF HOSPITAL MANAGEMENT
                                    1. Hatice Seda KUZ*, 2. Dilek ÇINAR†

Abstract
Hospitals are institutions serving in a constantly changing and complex environment in the health
sector. However, it is inevitable to encounter unexpected crises in this environment. Crisis situations
play a critical role in the sustainability of healthcare services by testing the leadership abilities, strategic
management skills and communication competences of hospital managers. In recent years, there has
been a growing interest in the potential and impact of women leaders in crisis management. The
potential and approach of women leaders in crisis management is important in this context. The role
of women leaders in crisis management is receiving increasing attention. Especially in the health sector,
leadership competencies and strategic approaches of women leaders in crisis situations are analyzed.
In this context, the importance of research focusing on the role of women leaders in crisis management
in the field of hospital management is increasing day by day. It is important to understand the impact
of women leaders' responses to crisis situations on organizational outcomes. The achievements and
strategic approaches of women leaders in crisis management have the potential to increase the quality
of healthcare services and improve hospital management processes. Therefore, research focusing on
the role of women leaders in crisis management can help identify effective leadership practices in the
healthcare sector and better prepare for future crisis situations. Therefore, research in this area is
becoming increasingly important. The purpose of this study is to examine the role of women leaders
in crisis management in the context of hospital management and to contribute to the literature. It is
aimed to examine in detail how women leaders approach crisis situations, their leadership styles,
communication strategies and strategic decision-making processes. In the continuation of the study, it
is planned to examine the researches in the literature to better understand the role of women leaders in
crisis management and to identify the gaps in this field and to provide suggestions for future research.
This study is expected to contribute to the understanding of the role of women leaders in crisis
management in the health sector and the development of hospital management practices.
Keywords: Strategic management, crisis management, hospital management, women leaders.

 CONTRIBUTION OF PUBLIC EDUCATION CENTER TO WOMEN'S
  EMPLOYMENT: THE CASE OF MARDIN PUBLIC EDUCATION
                       CENTER
                                     1. Havva YAĞCI*, 2. Esra ÖZBAY†

Abstract
The aim of this study is to examine the impact of public education centers on women's employment.
The trainings provided in public education centers should provide women with sufficient skills and
abilities to encourage them to participate in business life. However, it is seen that very few of the
trainings provided in these centers are aimed at meeting the needs of the market. There are various
studies in the literature on the effects of public education centers on women's employment. However,
there is no research on the contribution of Public Education Centers in Mardin province to women's
employment. The study group of this research consists of 16 female trainees selected by simple random
method from among 60 trainees in Artuklu Public Education Center in Mardin city center in 2023-
2024 through 6 different courses (cookery, hairdressing, handicrafts, clothing, child development,
furnishing). Interview technique, one of the qualitative research methods, was used in the study. Open-
ended questions were used in the interviews. The data obtained were analyzed by descriptive analysis
method. As a result of the research, the trainees stated that the trainers at Mardin Artuklu HEM have
sufficient knowledge and equipment. It is noteworthy that a significant portion of the trainees came to
these courses for socializing rather than acquiring a profession. They think that they will use the skills
they have acquired in the courses mostly to meet their household needs. Very few of the trainees plan
to sell the products they produce with the skills they have acquired in these courses to their spouses,
friends and neighbors. It is noteworthy that the trainees who think of working in a job after the course
are mostly trained in cookery. It has been determined that the trainings given in public education centers
should be organized according to market needs.
Keywords: Course, trainee, public education, employment, vocational training.

       THE PROBLEM OF WOMEN WORKING DAILY IN THE
    AGRICULTURAL SECTOR: THE CASE OF KAHRAMANMARAŞ
                                     1. Havva YAĞCI*, 2. Vildan KEKİL†

Abstract
Women working in the agricultural sector have various problems. Most of these women work in their
own businesses as unpaid family workers. The risk of poverty is higher for women living in rural areas.
Women who do not have land belonging to their family go to daily work in fields and gardens to
contribute to the family budget. Women who work in these jobs temporarily, unregistered and without
any social security, are exposed to various dangers. In this study; The working conditions and living
conditions of female agricultural workers who go to daily work in Çağlayancerit district of
Kahramanmaraş province were examined. Within the scope of the research, face-to-face interviews
were conducted with 20 women working in fields and gardens. Additionally, a survey was administered
to 80 women. According to the data obtained in the research, it is seen that temporary agricultural
worker women work in unregistered jobs without social security. It has been determined that these
women, who are daily seasonal agricultural workers, go to work in surrounding provinces and districts
such as Malatya, Adıyaman, Gaziantep and Kahramanmaraş. Although most of the women working in
casual jobs have a low level of education, there are also a few high school graduates and university
graduates. The fact that women with high school and university education also work in these jobs
prevents families from sending their daughters to school. Agricultural worker women hit the roads at
midnight to go to work in nearby provinces and travel with unsuitable shuttle vehicles. They work long
hours in fields and gardens under conditions that are inadequate in terms of hygiene and occupational
safety. Within the scope of the research, it was determined that women working in daily agricultural
work deal with intermediaries instead of employers. These people, who help women find jobs, receive
a significant portion of the wages as a share. Women are at risk of having a work accident. It has been
determined that there are no necessary inspections and regulations regarding people working in the
daily agricultural sector. It has been determined that the majority of women who go to daily work are
far from meeting their basic needs. It is also seen that these women working in fields and gardens are
unaware of occupational safety, insurance and other issues. It is necessary to take steps to improve the
working conditions of these women who face deep poverty and to register them.
Keywords: Seasonal female agricultural worker, wage, unregistered employment, job security,
poverty.

 ANALYSIS OF PHYSICAL IMAGE PREFERENCES PRESENTED BY
      FEMALE POLITICIANS BASED ON DRESS CODES
                                                 Hilal TAŞKIN*

Abstract
The characteristics of individuals operating in the field of politics are conveyed through political
communication studies, and it is aimed that political parties and their aims influence the targeted
audiences. It is seen that the personality traits, physical characteristics and discourses of political
leaders gain importance in communication studies with the transformation from party image to
candidate image. Within the image studies on physical elements, it is seen that issues such as clothing
model, color of clothing, accessories, hairstyle, shoes used are shaped around ideologies, personal
characteristics and determined intentions. In this study, the physical images of Yuliya Timoshenko,
Angela Merkel, Kamala Harris and Meral Akşener from the universe of female leaders were examined
on the basis of a sample consisting of eight outfit combinations. While seeking answers to three basic
questions about the study, the boundaries of which were determined through purposeful sampling, it
was aimed to determine the dressing codes and messages of the leaders in their images. In a semiotic
study, it was determined that the messages given through colors were predominant in the physical
images of female leaders. It has been determined that while the colors used are generally perceived to
create trust and express the limits of dominance, the clothing models allow the feminine and masculine
messages to be understood. While it was understood that the messages that the leaders emphasized
with the colors of their clothes were strengthened with their hairstyle and colors, it was observed that
the continuity in the adopted images could be interpreted as a stable and solid stance. While it was
determined that the situation of creating integrity in the images of the leaders within the framework of
their own preferences varied, it was seen that many common uses and messages were used when
compared to each other. It is believed that this study, which was carried out within the scope of political
communication, is rare and valuable because it is thought to serve the deficiency in the field identified
in the literature.

Keywords: Political communication, image of leader, physical image, dress codes, female politicians.

                                                                                            Turkish Presentation

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

 INVESTIGATION OF ACCOMMODATION PROBLEMS IN TURKEY:
            SECURITY QUEST OF ROMA WOMEN
                                1. Hilal YILDIRIM*, 2. Şevval BAHTİYAR†

Abstract
In Turkey, Roma people constitute one of the minority groups facing prejudice, discrimination, and
exclusion from a wide segment of society. They encounter disadvantages in accessing fundamental
rights such as education, healthcare, and housing. Roma women, in addition to their Roma identity,
experience this process from a different perspective due to their gender. Societal prejudices,
discrimination, early marriage, and limited education contribute to the impoverishment of Roma
women. This study aims to address accommodation problem of Roma women from social, cultural,
and economic perspectives, focusing on security and private space. The research relies on literature
review and in-depth interviews to explore factors contributing to accommodation problems, security
risks in various settings, encountered challenges, and their impact on daily lives. Additionally, it
examines how societal and cultural biases, along with masculinist power dynamics, affect Roma
women, with a focus on understanding experiences in Istanbul and proposing solutions from a gender
equality perspective. Evaluating strategies to protect and empower their private spaces is crucial for
improving the daily lives of Roma women. Furthermore, this study sheds light on the psychological
and social impacts of the challenges Roma women face in their daily lives. It will delve into the societal
and cultural prejudices and discrimination experienced by Roma women, analyzing them through the
lens of Pierre Bourdieu's concept of "masculine domination of space" and R. W. Connell's definition
of "hegemonic masculinity." By comprehensively understanding the experiences of Roma women in
Istanbul and contributing to solutions from a gender equality perspective, this study aims to enhance
their daily lives and empower them to protect and strengthen their private spaces.

Keywords: Roma women, accommodation rights, private space, security, hegemonic masculinity.

  COMMON STRATEGIES OF WOMEN ENTREPRENEURS AND
MANAGERS IN TURKEY IN COMBATING GENDER INEQUALITY: A
                     CASE STUDY
      1. Hümeyra TAŞÇIOĞLU*, 2. Burçak BAŞAK YİĞİT†, 3. Mustafa Abdül Metin DİNÇER‡

Abstract
This study focuses on how successful entrepreneurs and women managers overcome the challenges
arising from gender inequality in their careers and looks for a common pattern among the solutions
they find. In Turkey, women earn 15.6% less than men doing the same job; men earn 100 TL while
women earn 84.40 TL. While informal work increases the gender pay gap to 24.2%, this gap is 11.5%
for formal workers. This wage gap may become more pronounced as the career progresses. In this
framework, the research investigates whether there is a common pattern among the solutions produced
by successful entrepreneurial and executive women, and if so, "how" and "in what direction" this
pattern develops. Researchers think that the inferences that can be obtained from this process will
provide important tools to overcome the problems related to gender inequality. In this framework, the
study will address the effects of gender-based discrimination in the recruitment processes, promotions,
wage policies, and access to managerial positions for entrepreneurial and managerial women who have
succeeded in their careers. Based on the experiences of women entrepreneurs and managers from
different sectors, the study will examine in depth the prevalence of gender inequality in the business
world and how they deal with these inequalities. The case study method will be used as a research
strategy, and four women entrepreneurs and four women managers from different sectors will be
selected using theoretical sampling logic. Semi-structured interviews will be conducted with the
women who constitute the cases of the study. The results obtained from the interviews will be analyzed
using the qualitative content analysis method through codes and categories created in light of the data
obtained from both the literature and the field. This method aims to contribute to policies and practices
aimed at solving gender inequality by providing direct information about the challenges and coping
strategies of women entrepreneurs and managers from their own voices.

Keywords: Gender inequality, successful women entrepreneurs and managers, solution strategies, case
study.

      DETERMINATION OF DISTRESS LEVELS IN PREGNANT
   PREGNANTS APPLYING TO EMERGENCY OBSTETRIC CLINIC:
                    ŞIRNAK EXAMPLE
                                 1. İbrahim UZAN*, 2. Sevda KARAKAŞ†

Abstract
This study was conducted to determine the level of distress in pregnant women who applied to the
emergency obstetrics clinic. The study was conducted in the pregnant emergency clinic of Şırnak State
Hospital, Maternity and Children's Hospital, between May 2023 and July 2023. In calculating the
sample size of the study with a known sample size, the number of samples was calculated as prevalence
with a 5% error level and a 95% confidence interval, and 300 pregnant women were included in the
sample. Introductory Information Form and Tilburg Pregnancy Distress Scale (TGDS) were used in
pregnant women. The data were evaluated using numbers, percentages and statistical tests in the SPSS
20.0 program. T test, analysis of variance and post-hoc tests were used to evaluate the data. In our
study, it was found that the difference between educational status and TGDS total and negative affect
subscale score averages was statistically significant. It was determined that the average TGDS score
of women who had problems in their previous pregnancies was higher than those who did not. As a
result, it was determined that the level of distress was high in pregnant women who applied to the
emergency obstetrics clinic. It has been determined that as the spouse support and income level of
pregnant women increase, feelings of anxiety and uneasiness during pregnancy decrease and are
affected by many factors. Evaluating women not only physiologically but also psychologically during
pregnancy is of great importance for both mother and fetus/baby health.

Keywords: Emergency obstetrics, pregnant, distress

       INSTITUTIONS WHERE WOMEN VICTIMS OF GENDER
     INEQUALITY CAN SEEK HELP IN CASES OF VIOLENCE AND
                     OTHER PROBLEMS
                                  1. İclal ÇAĞLAR*, 2. Ayşegül DÖNMEZ†

Abstract
Women who have been victimized as a result of all kinds of violence against women, disempowerment
of women, gender inequality, social impositions and expectations continue to be victimized in our
country as in many parts of the world from past to present. Although studies on gender inequality in
Turkey have increased in recent years, sufficient progress has still not been achieved. The low
employment and representation rates of women are a concrete indicator of inequality in this field.
Women face gender inequality from the moment they are born. They are blamed within the family and
in society for the gender of their babies being girls. Sometimes this situation can result in the violation
of the babies' right to life. In the workplace, women are often subjected to mobbing and have to deal
with inequalities of opportunity in the workplace. In addition, social roles and responsibilities are
imposed on women at home, which is another aspect of gender inequality. Violence against women
should be addressed as a societal issue, not just an individual problem, as it has far-reaching negative
impacts, particularly on women and children. Support systems need to be in place to prevent gender-
based violence, empower women and improve their health, and preventive services need to be planned
and accessible. Violence is rooted in social inequality and situations such as domestic violence can
seriously affect individuals' mental health. Various strategies such as raising social awareness,
strengthening women's economic independence and making effective legal arrangements are important
in combating violence against women. However, this struggle is a process that requires the participation
not only of the state but also of the whole society. A comprehensive and coordinated approach will be
more effective in preventing violence against women and strengthening women's rights. In this context,
midwives play an important role in all reproductive health services, including birth and postnatal
processes, and play a key role in women's access to preventive health services. Midwives can support
women by recognizing the signs of gender inequality while providing health services to women and
provide assistance when necessary. At this stage, midwives have an important role in combating gender
inequality. This review, which is based on the literature, aims to share information about the institutions
where women who are victims of gender inequality can get help for violence and other problems.
Keywords: Violence against women, women's organization, women's NGO, social assistance,
midwifery

       EVIDENCE-BASED PRACTICES IN LABOR AND DELIVERY
                                    1. İclal ÇAĞLAR*, 2. Çiler YEYĞEL†

Abstract
Labor is one of the most critical periods in a woman's life and is extremely important for the health of
mother and baby. However, unnecessary interventions and wrong practices during labor can harm the
health of mother and baby. Midwives actively support pregnant women during labor and postpartum
period. Increasing the number of births performed by midwives will increase the use of evidence-based
practices. Therefore, the importance of evidence-based practices in childbirth is increasingly
emphasized. The aim of this review is to examine the importance and impact of evidence-based
practices in labor and delivery. It is aimed to encourage health professionals to make the right decisions,
to reduce unnecessary interventions, to increase the comfortable birth experience of expectant mothers,
to ensure cost-effectiveness, to prioritize patient expectations, to improve the quality of health care and
to provide standard care. In line with this aim, it is based on examining the current status of evidence-
based practices in labor and delivery through a literature review. In addition, international and national
standards of care, clinical guidelines and similar resources used to increase the effectiveness of
interventions in the labor process are evaluated in detail. The focus is on the role of health professionals
in the use of evidence-based practices in labor, reducing unnecessary interventions, and positive
feelings and views towards labor. The review emphasizes that the dissemination and adoption of
evidence-based practices in labor is of great importance for maternal and infant health. In order to
increase evidence-based practices in delivery rooms, it is recommended that all health professionals
involved in childbirth and especially midwives involved in all stages of childbirth should participate
in in-service training programs covering evidence-based practices, be aware of current developments
and conduct more research. In this context, strengthening midwifery practice should include expanding
the roles of midwives and strengthening practices that support natural birth. This approach will allow
the birth process to be based on women's preferences, needs and satisfaction.
Keywords: Labor, evidence-based practices, midwife.

          PHYSICAL CRIMES AGAINST WOMEN IN UŞAK IN 1930
                                                  İlker BAYRAM*

Abstract
Nowadays, not a day goes by without breaking news about a crime against women. While the news in
question is seen in the country, such news is also seen abroad. In order to prevent these crimes before
they happen, important practices are being carried out both in Turkey and around the world. Attempts
are made to take precautions, sometimes by ensuring the deterrent effectiveness of punishments and
sometimes by providing training. For this purpose, KADES was established within the Ministry of
Internal Affairs in Türkiye. Purpose of this is to enable women to notify the state's law enforcement
agencies when they detect any crime against them. In this way, it is aimed to prevent crimes against
women before they are committed. Of course, such a practice was not possible in Turkey 94 years ago.
When the Decision Cards of the Uşak High Criminal Court of 1930 were examined, it was seen that
45 separate crimes against women were tried. It was understood that 42 of them involved sexual crimes.
It was determined that 29 are for unmarried people and 13 are for married people. It was observed that
3 of the crimes committed against women in Uşak in 1930 were physical crimes. In this study, only
physical crimes against women will be examined. It was understood that out of 3 separate physical
crimes against women in Uşak in 1930, 2 were the crime of forcibly abducting a woman without her
consent, and 1 was the crime of strangulation of a married woman by her husband. Nowadays, it is
seen that the crime of murder of a woman by her husband, among the crimes committed against women,
is more common than in the past. In this study, the contents of the physical crimes committed against
women in Uşak in 1930 and the penalties given as a result of the trial will be conveyed, taking into
account the principle of protection of personal data.
Keywords: Crimes against women, Uşak, 1930, history of the republic of Türkiye.

                                                                                              Turkish Presentation

7709, email: ibayram09@hotmail.com

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV

  THE IMPORTANCE OF FEMINIST PEDAGOGY TO PROPAGATE A
                   FEMINIST AGENCY
                                                 İlknur MEŞE*

Abstract
Feminist education is about feminist theory, from which we can get help on what to teach, and feminist
pedagogy, which shows us the road map of how to teach. When we understand feminism as a social
movement and a movement of thought that aims to end sexism, sexist exploitation and oppression,
following bell hooks' definition, we define feminist pedagogy as a variety of teaching approaches or
strategies that help to reveal the causes and consequences of this exploitation and oppression. In
relation to this, feminist pedagogy aims to organise teacher-student relations, student-student relations
and the habitat of classrooms in an egalitarian, democratic, critical and solidarist framework. Since it
aims to create a transformation to overcome sexism, racism, classism and other destructive hatreds, it
explores ways to produce a new praxis that integrates theory and practice, to establish the link between
the classroom and social change, and to teach students activism. Feminist pedagogy is the practice of
developing another form of social relations, another language, subjectivity and agency, even dignity in
students, between teachers and students and among students themselves. In this sense, feminist
pedagogy has a strong regulatory aspect. The physical, historical, cultural and pedagogical
characteristics of the place of education, its possibilities and limitations, the pedagogical characteristics
and pedagogical predispositions of the students are resources that shape feminist pedagogy. Each
university, department, lecturers and students have their own local characteristics and issues that
determine the framework and applicability of feminist pedagogy. Feminist pedagogy is therefore
context-dependent and pluralistic. In this paper, I will draw a general framework for feminist pedagogy
and discuss how feminist pedagogy, which has applicability not only in the social sciences but also in
the classrooms of other disciplines, can help us develop a feminist agency to stand against sexist
violence, exploitation and oppression in Turkey today, drawing on both feminist theory and our own
experiences.

Keywords: Feminism, feminist pedagogy, feminist agency.

                                                                                           Turkish Presentation

                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV

     UNDERSTANDING VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN: POSSIBLE
                    ECONOMIC CAUSES
                                              İsmail Hakkı İŞCAN*

Abstract
Academic studies on violence against women are of significant value in terms of understanding the
causes of violence against women and the measures to be taken to prevent violence. However, studies
on the subject, especially those that are integrated with emotional content, may exhibit “prejudice” and
“classical parallels”. For example, suggesting that violence is only directed against women or that
violence against women is parallel to economic underdevelopment and/or low levels of education.
While such hard-to-resist propositions may be true within the constraints of history, country and social
difference, they may leave us incapable of identifying, understanding and preventing the root causes
of violence. Worse still, it may also distract us from what is needed in terms of the decisions to be taken
in efforts to prevent violence. In today's conditions where national and international sensitivities have
become extremely sensitive, violence has still not been prevented. The dominant view insists on the
idea that many of the factors cited among the causes of violence against women will be eliminated
when women gain economic and/or legal independence and gain power, and that the problem can only
be solved in this way. Likewise, it is hoped or aimed that violence will end with global concepts/slogans
such as “gender equality” developed at the international level in this direction. However, the fact that
violence is still continuing and increasing reveals that it is much more important to reinforce the “social
interaction” and “social acceptance” that such initiatives will bring in order to prevent violence with
both legal and sociological structure. International and uniform analyses that are arrived at without
taking into account such country or regional differences within the same country show that different
perspectives on the problem are needed, given the existence of figures on ongoing violence. In this
respect, perhaps the most important effort of field studies should be to force a differentiation from such
a classical view. This study aims to identify the economic causes of violence. In line with this effort,
quantitative data obtained from various international organizations and studies conducted in the field
will be evaluated. In addition, the study aims to contribute to the production of solution-oriented results
about the problem by trying to bring different and perhaps unanswered questions to the agenda. It is
understood that the most important way to prevent violence is not only to ensure that women gain
power through economic, political and legal rights granted to women with various concepts/slogans
such as “gender equality”, but also to establish legal, institutional and sociological mechanisms that
will ensure the “social absorption” of this power.
Keywords: Violence, women, economy.

ismailhakki.iscan@bilecik.edu.tr

GENDER DISPARITIES IN INFORMATION AND COMMUNICATION
  TECHNOLOGY COMPETENCIES AND USAGE AMONG STEM
               STUDENTS: A PILOT STUDY
     1. Johanna NAVARRO-ESPINOSA*, 2. David Muñoz RODRIGUEZ†, 3. Alberto J. PEREA
    MORENO‡, 4. Miguel CASTILLO RODRIGUEZ§, 5. Pilar MARTINEZ-JIMENEZ**, 6. Pilar
                              APARICIO-MARTINEZ††

Abstract
In the context of modern education, the integration of information and communication technologies
(ICTs) is a pivotal factor in shaping students' academic trajectories, particularly in science, technology,
engineering, and mathematics (STEM). However, despite the strides made in STEM education,
persistent disparities in ICT competencies, usage, and representation based on gender continue to exist.
This study is of paramount importance as it seeks to determine the representation, competencies, and
use according to gender among STEM students, thereby highlighting a significant and urgent issue
with real-world implications. A descriptive analysis of STEM students was conducted between January
and February 2024. The data was obtained via QR code using a 28-item survey. The survey included
age, gender, bachelor's, use of ICTs, competencies, and the types most used for study purposes. The
students for two faculties were asked to voluntarily fill out the survey regarding their competencies
and usage of ICTs. The results (N=61) indicated a low representation of females (18%) compared with
males (50%), which was also shown by the frequency of engineers (86,9%). The level of competence
was similar in both groups, female and male. Meanwhile, the relevance of ICTs in the learning process
was highly evaluated by male students (52%), compared to females (18,18%). There were no
significant differences regarding the usage of ICTs, including Moodle, tablets, social media, and virtual
laboratories (p>0,05). The results indicated that females have less representation in STEM
competencies and usages. Nonetheless, the results regarding the gender differences were not significant
based on the miniature representation of young women. This research illuminates the intricate
dynamics of the gender gap in ICT competencies and usage among STEM students. It highlights that
there continues to be a gap in the representation and participation of women in STEM, highlighting the
need for policy interventions and educational initiatives. Ultimately, closing the gender gap in ICT
competencies is a pivotal step towards advancing societal progress and innovation in the digital age.
Keywords: STEM, gender, competencies, ICTs.
