"WHEN I RESISTED HIM, I DIDN’T KNOW WHAT HE’S GOING TO
DO TO ME": JEWISH RESISTANCE TO SEXUALIZED VIOLENCE IN
 NAZI FORCED LABOUR, CONCENTRATION, AND DEATH CAMPS
                                            Kästle VAN DER MEER*

Abstract
Despite the recent rise in research concerning sexualized violence in the Holocaust, virtually no studies
exist concerning the ways in which those who experienced sexualized violence in Nazi camps resisted
such abuse. That so little has been written about this topic means that many questions are left
unanswered. For example, who resisted such violence? How did they do so? What factors impacted
one’s ability to resist? What punishments did prisoners experience if they resisted a camp authority
figure? In an attempt to answer some of these questions, my research looks at Jewish experiences of
sexualized violence by Nazis in forced labour camps, concentration camps, and death camps and
investigates how such violence was resisted. An analysis of survivor testimony from the USC Shoah
Foundation Visual History Archive shows that sexualized violence was resisted by women in the camps
vigorously and in a variety of ways, the result of prisoners utilizing both their agency and luck. This
study demonstrates that resistance did not always end in death; it was possible to resist sexualized
violence and survive. This is critical, because in the face of genocide, survival was the ultimate act of
defiance. Indeed, survivors’ own testimonies seem to suggest that those who resisted often went
unpunished for resisting, even while perpetrators themselves faced consequences. Yet, even if attempts
at resistance were unsuccessful or resulted in one’s death, they challenged the power structure that the
camp system relied on, exemplifying the importance of individual resistance in the survival strategies
of prisoners.
Keywords: Sexualized violence, resistance, Nazi, holocaust, testimony, survivor.

                                                                                             English Presentation




* Sessional Lecturer, University of Victoria, Canada, ORCID: 0009-0008-3616-7191, email: kastlev@uvic.ca

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

       EVALUATION OF THE RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN BODY
     DISSATISFACTION, DIET QUALITY AND EATING DISORDER
                    TENDENCY IN WOMEN
                                 1. Kezban ŞAHİN*, 2. Hacı Ömer YILMAZ†

Abstract
Body dissatisfaction is a negative body perception and is characterised by a discrepancy between
individuals' actual body and their perception of their ideal body. Body dissatisfaction is more common
especially in women and is associated with many negative health outcomes. In this study, it was aimed
to examine the relationship between body dissatisfaction, diet quality and eating disorders tendencies
of women. The data of the study were collected through an online questionnaire (Google Forms). The
questionnaire form prepared by the researchers consists of sociodemographic information,
anthropometric measurements, Stunkard Body Rating Scale, Mediterranean Diet Adherence Scale
(MEDAS; Mediterranean Diet Adherence Screener) and REZZY Eating Disorders Scale. The research
data were analysed with SPSS 25.0. The significance level was accepted as p<0.05 for all statistics.
203 adult women participated in the study. The mean age of the women participating in the study was
27.0±8.0 years, median BMI was 22.04 kg/m2 (15.43-39.06 kg/m2), MEDAS score was 7 (2-11) and
REZZY score was 1 (0-4). Body dissatisfaction was found in 20.2% of the women. The median BMI
[24.92 kg/m2 (16.85-39.06 kg/m2)] and REZZY scores ([2 (0-4)] of women with body dissatisfaction
were significantly higher than the BMI [21.48 kg/m2 (15.43-32.45 kg/m2)] and REZZY score [1 (0-
3)] of women who were satisfied with their bodies (p<0.001). However, no significant difference was
found between the two groups in terms of MEDAS score (p>0.05). Body dissatisfaction was
significantly positively associated with REZZY score (r=0.249; p<0.001) and BMI (r=0.409; p<0.001),
but negatively associated with MEDAS score, but this relationship was not statistically significant (r=-
0.022; p>0.05). In conclusion, body dissatisfaction is associated with increased BMI level and
increased risk of developing eating disorders in adult women, but not with diet quality. Therefore,
increasing women's body satisfaction level and destroying negative body perception may be an
important strategy in preventing both obesity and eating disorders. In line with the findings obtained,
it is thought that a multidisciplinary team in which dietitians and psychologists take an active role may
contribute to the detection of obesity and eating disorders in women before they develop.
Keywords: Body dissatisfaction, obesity, eating disorder, Mediterranean diet, women.

ksahin@bandirma.edu.tr

haciyilmaz@bandirma.edu.tr

                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

     THE RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN SMARTPHONE ADDICTION,
       HEALTHY DIET LITERACY AND OBESITY IN WOMEN
                 1. Kezban ŞAHİN*, 2. Rümeysa GERBOĞA†, 3. Hacı Ömer YILMAZ‡

Abstract
Obesity is one of the health problems with increasing prevalence day by day. With the developing
technology, the rate and duration of smartphone use for various purposes such as obtaining information
and communication are increasing steadily. This study was planned and conducted to determine the
relationship between women's smartphone addiction status, healthy diet literacy and obesity status. The
questionnaire form developed by the researchers included socio-demographic information about the
participants, smartphone usage habits, Smartphone Addiction Scale-Short Form and Digital Healthy
Diet Literacy Scale. The research data were evaluated with the computer-assisted software programme
SPSS 25.0 and p<0.05 was accepted as significance level. In the study, data of 260 women were
analysed. The average age of the participants was 32.21±12.39 years and the average body mass index
(BMI) was 23.85±4.50 kg/m2, 20.0% were overweight and 13.1% were obese. Analysing the
smartphone addiction status of the participants, 37.7% (98 people) were found to be addicted to
smartphones. The mean BMI of those who were addicted to smartphones was 23.16±4.87 kg/m2, while
the mean BMI of those who were not addicted to smartphones was 24.26±4.22 kg/m2 (t=1.914,
p>0.05). Similar to the average BMI values, the digital healthy diet literacy scores of those who were
addicted to smartphones were lower than those who were not addicted to smartphones (21.47±10.36
and 24.61±11.24, respectively, p<0.05). Although not statistically significant, negative correlation was
found between smartphone addiction and digital healthy diet literacy (r=-0.044, p>0.05). In addition,
a negative significant correlation was found between smartphone addiction and mean BMI values of
the participants (r=-0.131, p<0.05). As a result, it was determined that women's smartphone addiction
status may affect both their healthy nutrition literacy and obesity status. It is thought that following the
scientific and accuracy aspects of social media accounts and information-sourced sharing sites
followed especially for the use of internet-connected smartphones, consulting health professionals
before applying the information obtained in daily life may prevent the occurrence of other health
problems, especially obesity, and more research is needed in this regard.
Keywords: Smartphone, obesity, diet literacy, women.

     THE EDUCATION OF YOUNG MUSLIM WOMEN IN DZHEBEL
                        NOWADAYS
                                                  Kristina KIROVA*

Abstract
In the regions with a religious mixed population, diverse groups of people live together united by the
common territory they inhabit. Bulgaria is a country where, in historical reasons, Christians and
Muslims have peacefully lived together for centuries. The scope of this research focuses on the process
of education of Muslim women in Bulgaria, and more specifically to those, who live in the city of
Dzhebel nowadays. The aim of this study is to examine the change in lifestyle and daily practices of
Muslim women in Dzhebel, in the context of their religious and secular education latterly. The research
makes the attempt to explore the reasons that influence the socio-cultural choices of Muslim women
to be educated or not to be. It is interesting to see how Islam affect women's choice for education and
which education – secular or religious – is more attractive for Muslim women in Dzhebel today and
why. The main approach in this study is conducted anthropological research in the city of Dzhebel. In
a result a certain empirical information in a specific settlement in Bulgaria, concerning the education
of some Muslim women has been collected and interpreted. The sociological method used in the
research is pursued structured interviews with Muslim women, who have lived in Dzhebel. Through
this qualitative method and the field research this study tries to explore the attitude and outlooks of
Muslim woman towards religious and secular education. Thereupon the study attempts to analyze to
what extent the education of Muslim women, who live and work in Dzhebel, makes them to live their
lives in the society better and in harmony with the values of Islam at the same time.
Keywords: Islam, muslim women, education.

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

     A STRONG CHALLENGE TO HEGEMONY IN ART: CHANTAL
               AKERMAN’S FEMINIST CINEMA
                                            M. Özer ÖZKANTAR*

Abstract
Cinema is an art with multifaceted narratives, incorporating many different sub-genres, and capable of
addressing social and social transformations within its own framework. However, in addition to being
an art, cinema is also considered a commercial commodity since it is seen as a means of entertainment
for people, and this situation can lead to the formation of powerful cinema industries such as
Hollywood Cinema and these structures can act with certain cinema clichés. Therefore, this process
both paves the way for monopolisation in cinema and lots of films shot within Hollywood act with a
hegemonic masculinity, confining the roles of femininity and masculinity to certain boundaries and
many films are created within the framework of these rules. However, feminist cinema has literally
rebelled against this situation and has embarked on a struggle to present narratives about women in
accordance with the essence and realities of women's identity. At this point, the main concern of this
study is to reveal the understanding of cinema of Chantal Akerman, who is considered as one of the
most important representatives of feminist cinema, and to reveal the ways in which the director
challenges classical narratives by comparing mainstream masculine cinema with Akerman's cinema.
In addition, the other aims of this study are to emphasise how the director positions the female identity
in the film atmosphere and to ascertain the codes related to gender. In this direction, Akerman's Jeanne
Dielman, 23, quai du Commerce, 1080 Bruxelles (1975) was selected through purposeful sampling
method, the related film was divided into some categories, compared with Hollywood cinema and
analyzed through descriptive analysis method. As a result, it has been observed that the director has
turned conventional cinema narratives upside down, deconstructed them, transformed clichéd images
and seriously challenged hegemonic cinema narratives by introducing contexts that bring the essence
of female identity to the fore.
Keywords: Feminism, feminist cinema, Chantal Akerman, Jeanne Dielman.

                      International Congress on Women in A Global World IV

      GENDER APARTHEID IMPACT IN AFGHANISTAN AND THE
       POSSIBILITY TO BECOME AN INTERNATIONAL CRIME
                                       Marla KARELLY ALENCAR*

Abstract
When the Taliban took over Afghanistan in 2021, the situation of women in the country became
hazardous. Therefore, this article focuses on the gender apartheid system in the country, aiming to
illuminate its intricate manifestations, conceptual framework, and profound impacts on the lives of
Afghan women. The overall objective is to investigate the condition of women in Afghanistan, focusing
on the gender apartheid system, and to analyze its relevance as an international crime, aiming to
contribute to the rights of Afghan women and girls. By undertaking a qualified analysis, the study is
committed to enlighten the gravity of this systemic injustice and its implications. Gender apartheid is
a serious violation of human rights, and its characterization as an international crime can facilitate the
implementation of effective measures, the protection of women, and the accountability of perpetrators.
Methodologically, the research utilizes a rigorous review of existing academic literature, legal statutes,
and reports from international organizations. Through this meticulous examination, the article aims to
offer a comprehensive understanding of the gender apartheid regime, its underlying mechanisms, and
its widespread consequences for Afghan women and girls. Moreover, the research endeavours to
delineate actionable steps for the protection of women and the accountability of perpetrators. This study
seeks to encourage global efforts towards implementing effective measures to safeguard the rights and
dignity of Afghan women. By elucidating its nuances and advocating for its recognition as an
international crime, the study aspires to encourage meaningful change and promote an equitable society
for Afghan women and girls.
Keywords: Gender apartheid, human rights, Taliban, women rights.

FACTORS AFFECTING WOMEN'S HEALTH AND MIDWIVES' ROLE
                                1. Maryam AZARNIA*, 2. Ayşegül DÖNMEZ†

Abstract
Women's health is an important field shaped by the complex interaction of biological, social, and
environmental factors. Understanding these factors determines women's lifelong well-being and
directly influences societal welfare. Women's health is a multifaceted process that encompasses various
stages of life and holds a prioritized position in healthcare compared to other segments of society.
Determinants of women's health, as described in the literature, include various factors shaping women's
well-being on a global scale, such as sexual and reproductive health, gender equality, and the
importance of healthcare delivery. Additionally, the significance of social factors like education,
migration, employment, and violence in influencing women's health is emphasized. This becomes even
more crucial in developing countries like Turkey, where women constitute half of the total population.
Women's health spans various stages of life and is a priority issue in healthcare compared to other
segments of society. Among the factors influencing women's health, there is a complex relationship
between biological determinants, socio-economic status, culture, and access to healthcare delivery.
Even though pregnancy, childbirth, and postpartum processes can pose health risks despite being
healthy, priority should be given to women's health. Access to family planning and contraception
methods are important initiatives in preventing fertility-related issues in women's health. Physical and
mental health during adulthood are crucial for the healthy progression of children's growth and
development and concern not only women's health but also the well-being of mothers, children, and
society as a whole. This review, based on literature, aims to share information regarding the role of
midwives in women's health by emphasizing the health and social dimensions of factors affecting
women's health. Examining the various dimensions of factors influencing women's health can
contribute to evidence-based interventions and policies that affect public health and empower women
for healthier, higher-quality lives.
Keywords: Woman health, health factors, social factors, reproductive health, midwife.

          EVIDENCE-BASED PRACTICES IN PRENATAL TESTING
                                  1. Maryam AZARNIA*, 2. Çiler YEYĞEL†

Abstract
The use of evidence-based practices in prenatal testing encompasses evidence-based approaches in
routine pregnancy monitoring and fetal assessment, providing comprehensive evaluation throughout
pregnancy. Therefore, the utilization of evidence-based practices in prenatal testing is crucial for
achieving optimal maternal and fetal outcomes. This study aims to highlight the significant
contributions of evidence-based developments in prenatal testing. Additionally, it seeks to provide a
comprehensive overview of evidence-based practices in prenatal testing and emphasize their critical
role in ensuring optimal maternal and fetal outcomes. Integration of evidence-based interventions such
as ultrasound examinations, amniocentesis, and chorionic villus sampling should be analyzed to
enhance the quality and effectiveness of antenatal care. Evaluation methods for fetal assessment,
including techniques such as ultrasound, should be considered to contribute to the early detection of
potential issues during the prenatal period based on accurate and scientifically grounded information
about fetal development. Utilizing evidence-based practices for comprehensive maternal assessment
and monitoring maternal health will support pregnancy well-being. Challenges and barriers to the use
of evidence-based practices in prenatal testing are evident. This study underscores the critical role of
evidence-based developments in prenatal testing for achieving optimal maternal and fetal outcomes
and advocates for their adoption in routine care. Among the notable findings of prenatal testing, high-
quality evidence supports second-trimester amniocentesis as the preferred procedure for testing from
the 15th week of pregnancy onwards. When a test is necessary before the 15th week of pregnancy, low
to moderate quality evidence suggests that depending on the outcome of interest, transabdominal
chorionic villus sampling could be considered as the preferred procedure. Therefore, further research
is essential to address the challenges and barriers to the implementation of evidence-based
interventions in prenatal testing.
Keywords: Prenatal test, safe pregnancy, pregnancy follow-ups, evidence-based midwifery care.

     OCCUPATIONAL BURNOUT: A QUALITATIVE RESEARCH ON
                    WOMEN ACADEMICS
                        1. Mehmet DİNÇ*, 2. Elmas GEZER†, 3. Sibel NALBANT‡

Abstract
This study aimed to examine the experiences of female academics regarding how the
problems/challenges they experience in their working lives affect their occupational burnout. In the
study, a qualitative research was conducted using the phenomenological pattern method. The sample
of the research consists of 6 female academics working at a state university in Turkey. Semi-structured
individual interview method was used and 13 open-ended questions were asked to the participants,
created by three academics who are experts in the field. The data obtained from the interviews were
analyzed using the thematic analysis method. In the research, five main themes were obtained to
explain the occupational burnout of female academics. Among these themes, the theme is "difficulties
experienced"; It points out that female academics face prejudices and access problems. In the second
theme, "factors that cause burnout"; It has been revealed that there are problems regarding gender roles,
workload, being ignored, and the cost of living. In the third theme, "factors that facilitate being positive
and happy"; Opinions were obtained regarding improving the working environments of female
academics, economic improvements, alleviating the workload, and positive attitudes of
administrators/managers. In the fourth theme, "being a working woman", opinions were obtained about
the advantages and disadvantages of being a working woman. In the fifth theme called
"Recommendations"; Opinions were reached on keeping home and work separate and keeping
motivation high. It is seen that occupational burnout is higher in environments where the number of
academics is low, the number of female and male academics is not equal and working conditions are
not good. In addition, the social role of women, economic reasons and workload related to the title also
cause occupational burnout. Making the working environment suitable, lightening the workload,
creating fair working conditions with an egalitarian approach in the workplace and equalizing the
number of women and men in the working environment can be recommended as effective solutions
against occupational burnout.
Keywords: Woman, academician, occupational burnout.

     EXPERIENCES OF SINGLE PARENT MOTHERS REGARDING
            THEIR CHILDREN’S EDUCATIONAL LIFE
                                  1. Melek LAL*, 2. Yasemin Şule SARI†

Abstract
Family; the community, which consists of spouses, children, siblings and people from the same lineage
who are connected by mutual emotional, legal and blood ties, is the social structure that is accepted as
the smallest unit. The family group consisting of mother, father and unmarried children living in the
same house is called the basic family. Within the concept of nuclear family, a structure consisting of a
single parent and a dependent child has become more common today. This situation is an indispensable
process of a developing and changing society. A single-parent family structure is a family structure that
is largely carried out by a single person and where responsibility is usually assigned to only one parent.
Its formation depends on the death of either of the parents, divorce, extra-marital union and other
factors. This study aims to understand the experiences of single parent mothers affected by any of the
mentioned factors in the educational process of their children and to present these experiences from a
woman's perspective in the context of equal opportunities in education. In addition, the experiences of
single-parent families, especially mothers, during their children's educational processes and the
positive and negative sides of these experiences are the focus of this study. In the light of the
transformations in the family structure, the effects of being a single parent on the educational life of
children are examined through economic, social and legal difficulties and coping strategies with these
difficulties. Based on qualitative research methods, the data obtained through semi-structured
interviews aim to provide a deep understanding and presentation of the school experiences of single-
parent mothers and their children. The results of the research will emphasize the need to make
education policies and practices more sensitive to the needs of children living with a single parent. In
addition to all these, the study also suggests that more work should be done in the field of equal
opportunities in education and that the voices of single-parent families should be heard more, and it is
supported by the examples given.
Keywords: Single parent, mother, broken family, child, education.

        ANALYSIS OF THE MOVIE IRON JAWED ANGELS IN THE
                 CONTEXT OF FEMINIST THEORY
                                           Melih Burak ÖZDEMİR*

Abstract
This article aims to analyze the film 'Iron Jawed Angels' directed by Katja von Garnier through the
basic concepts and principles of feminist therapy. This study was conducted with the belief that film
analysis studies based on theories are limited in Turkey and that the film can be used as a therapeutic
element by different disciplines and contribute to the field. Descriptive analysis and document analysis
techniques, which are qualitative research methods, were used in the study. The subject of women is a
phenomenon that involves many disciplines such as economics, politics, psychology and sociology.
The film depicts the impact of the patriarchal structure on social life and the difficulties experienced
by women trapped in this structure. It may be possible to see the movie as a rebellion against the male-
dominated world and to reach outputs that support the feminist approach through the social message
intended to be given in the movie. The use of films by different disciplines and their evaluation through
theories can be useful for prospective psychologists, psychological counselors and sociologists during
their undergraduate education. Making women visible in social life, seeing men and women equally as
free citizens and making feminism a philosophy of life can make the world a more livable place. At
this point, it can be said that women's movements that stand behind their ideas and do not submit to
the masculine order are pleasing for us to look to the future with hope. In this context, it can be
interpreted that films can be effectively utilized in making theoretical knowledge concrete and used by
different disciplines. Although gender discrimination seems to be a universal problem for women, it
can be said that it is not a problem that only concerns women today, but is a negativity that affects the
whole of social life, and it is important to continue the struggle from this perspective in order to analyze
the consequences of gender discrimination well.
Keywords: Psychology, sociology, feminist, woman, cinema.

ADDRESSING VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN IN THE CONTEXT OF
            RELATIONAL CULTURAL THEORY
                                                Melike YALÇIN*

Abstract
Relational cultural theory is a postmodern feminist theory. It explains that women's basic
developmental needs are different from men's and that these needs are just as important as those in
dominant male-dominated models. The importance of the quality of their relationships with other
people in women's psychological development is emphasized. By incorporating relational cultural
theory into violence against women programs, a more comprehensive and effective support system can
be provided for women exposed to violence. Relational cultural theory focuses on building long-term
empowerment and resilience. However, it is also increasingly recognized for its potential to bring about
meaningful and sustainable change in cases of violence against women. This theory, with its emphasis
on empathy, mutual empowerment and collaboration, provides an important basis for supporting
women who have experienced violence. Research demonstrates the impact of relational cultural theory
in increasing the resilience and empowerment of women survivors of violence. It has a transformative
role in supporting women survivors of violence by enabling understanding of relational dynamics and
the impact of social power structures. In this framework, by incorporating the principles and techniques
of relational cultural theory into intervention programs for women exposed to violence, support will
be provided for women to heal and establish healthy relationships. In this study, the foundations of
relational cultural theory and its applications and explanations in the context of violence against women
are given. The implications for women's empowerment and participation are explained, as well as what
needs to be considered in practice.

Keywords: Women, violence against women, relational cultural theory.

                                                                                           Turkish Presentation




* Asst. Prof., Afyon Kocatepe University, Social Work, Türkiye, ORCID: 000-0002-2312-276X, email: myalcin@aku.edu.tr

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

    CONDITIONAL INCLUSION AND DOUBLE IDENTITY IN FADIA
             FAQIR’S THE CRY OF THE DOVE (2007)
                                             Merve Ceren SÜTER*

Abstract
One of the prominent figures among contemporary postcolonial writers, Fadia Faqir is a British
novelist with Jordanian roots. In her third novel The Cry of the Dove (2007), Faqir deals with the
identity issues of an asylum seeker, who flees her country in fear of becoming an honour killing victim.
As an immigrant herself, Faqir captures and conveys the struggles of adapting to a new society and
culture through Salma, who is the protagonist of this non-linear narrative. Salma is an Arab Bedouin
woman, and as a woman in a Middle Eastern country, she has no control over her own life. However,
she falls in love secretly and gets pregnant out of wedlock, spends eight years in prison to escape the
death sentence of her family and tribe and meanwhile, she loses her daughter to the authorities. With
the help of a nun, she flees from her own country and starts a new life with a new identity in Britain.
Being an Arab Muslim woman in a white society, she faces all kinds of racism and degradation while
she is on a quest to build a new life for herself as Sally Asher. This paper aims to analyse Salma’s
journey as the Other and her conditional inclusion to the Western society, wearing the lenses of
postcolonial theory, specifically taking after Edward Said’s Orientalism and his ideas on the Other.
Being a potential victim of honour killing, Salma struggles to adjust to life in Britain, and being the
Other, her inclusion in British society is conditional. Hence, her conformity to these conditions brings
about a situation of a double identity. At first, she is this oppressed Middle Eastern woman who is
controlled by the man of her family and tribe and then she immigrates and becomes the Other in the
British society. As she comes and goes between Salma and Sally, trying to adjust to the British way of
life, we follow her journey in struggling to assert her own identity as the Other and the unwanted
foreigner in Britain.
Keywords: Conditional inclusion, women, double identity, the Other, honor killing, asylum seekers

0597-6656, email: mervecereneroglu@gmail.com

   AFTER THE FEBRUARY 6 KAHRAMANMARAŞ EARTHQUAKE,
         STUDIES ON WOMEN: A SYSTEMATIC REVIEW
                                           Merve Deniz PAK GÜRE*

Abstract
In this study, it was aimed to evaluate the results of the studies on women following the February 6,
2023 Kahramanmaraş-centered earthquakes. Google Scholar, Dergipark, Asos Index, SOBIAD,
Pubmed, and ULAKBIM TR Index databases were searched using the Turkish and English equivalents
of the keywords 'woman', 'earthquake', 'disaster', 'February 6', 'Kahramanmaraş earthquake' and 'Hatay
earthquake'. In the first step of the analysis using the PRISMA (The Preferred Reporting Items for
Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analysis) protocol, 305 studies were found. The inclusion criteria were
that the study was an article, published between 2023 and 2024, and the full text could be accessed.
Case reports, presentations, book/book chapters, proceedings, postgraduate theses, systematic reviews
were excluded. After assessment according to these criteria, inappropriate and duplicate studies were
eliminated and 11 articles were analyzed within the scope of the study. These included 3 research
studies and 8 reviews. The topics of these articles are discussed under three headings: women's sexual
and reproductive health in disasters, the impact of disasters on women's psychosocial needs and the
roles of professionals working with women in disasters. Studies on women's sexual and reproductive
health in disasters reveal that gynecological diagnosis and treatment services are disrupted, prenatal,
intrapartum and postnatal care is inadequate, and studies on family planning and contraception should
be increased. Studies focusing on the psychosocial needs of women report that gender inequality
deepens after earthquakes, care responsibilities increase, social support systems weaken, and post-
traumatic stress disorder, depression and burnout are more common in women. Within the scope of the
roles of professionals working with women in disasters, there are studies on the defense of women's
rights and the provision of women-friendly healthcare services. In conclusion, prioritizing
comprehensive reproductive health services, strengthening social support systems and implementing
gender-sensitive disaster response strategies can be recommended.
Keywords: Woman, women's health, February 6 earthquake, Kahramanmaraş earthquake, disaster

                                                                                           Turkish Presentation




* Assoc. Prof. Dr., Başkent University, Social Work, Türkiye, ORCID: 0000-0001-7060-3729, email: mdpak@baskent.edu.tr




                                                        111
                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

      CHANGING FORMS OF ORGANIZATION OF WOMEN WITH
     DIGITAL ACTIVISM: EXPERIENCES WITH TAKING OFF THE
                         HEADSCARF
                                                  Merve KAYA*

Abstract
The headscarf used by women has been the subject of different struggles in every era from the past to
the present. This study examines the experiences of women who have taken off their headscarves and
have been oppressed for doing so, through the online letters they have sent to the platform You Will
not Walk Alone; it aims to discuss what kind of space the platform creates for women, what meaning
it has and, above all, what kind of opportunities for activism it creates. Women have changed the
dimension of activism with the interaction they have achieved through technological developments in
social media and have taken organizing to a new dimension by allowing the concept of "digital
activism" into our lives. As a result of this change, women have started to create different platforms on
social media where they can explain themselves and their lives and integrate themselves. It is important
to create digital activism to increase the visibility of women who have been subjected to family and
religious "male domination" all their lives, to explain their struggle to take off the headscarf
individually and to make their voices heard. This new activism, which has been mobilized and gained
momentum through the removal of the headscarf, is now handled in a different dimension than previous
activisms. In this context, there are various reasons why the study is being discussed through digital
activism. It is about creating platforms where women can communicate the domination they are
subjected to without being tied to a hierarchy. It's about creating a communication network with women
who have the same problems as they do. It is about creating a space for online activism where women
can express their solidarity. Using the technique of document analysis, which corresponds to the
qualitative research methods of the data obtained on the basis of the study, it was investigated how the
letters sent to the platform You Will Not Walk Alone affected the solidarity process of women who
wanted to take off their headscarves and what kind of activism opportunity it created. The universe of
the study consists of letters on the website "yalnizyurumeden.com", a social sharing platform. First, a
descriptive analysis of 81 anonymous letters shared on the platform's website between 17.09.2023 and
26.01.2024 was carried out and selected using the targeted sampling technique. In the data
subsequently obtained, the meaning of the platform, the way it creates digital solidarity and the way
women try to overcome their loneliness in the "othering" process they experience because they go
beyond what is "normal" in society by writing on the platform were examined in more detail through
content analysis and the results made available.
Keywords: Digital activism, headscarf removal experiences, you will not walk alone platform.

                                                                                           Turkish Presentation

                                                        112
                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

 WOMEN'S POLITICAL PARTICIPATION AND QUOTA PRACTICES
                      IN EUROPE
                                               Merve ÖZDEMİR*

Abstract
In its most general definition, political participation is defined as the power of the governed to influence
the decisions and practices of the state. Political participation in countries; It depends on various
variables such as gender, education, profession, age, culture and family. Political participation cannot
be directly associated with voting behavior. Various features such as citizens participating in
discussions on political and administrative issues, having basic knowledge about politics, and being
able to objectively approach the information they obtain from the media are important for political
participation. It is seen that certain debates regarding gender equality continue since ancient Greece
and continue today. Although there is a difference in terms of gender equality compared to the past, it
is seen that this issue is not fully achieved in many areas today. One of these areas is the issue of taking
part in politics. It is observed that especially women's participation in the political field is lower than
men. As in many countries of the world, women's political participation in Turkey has a lower rate than
men. From this perspective, the fact that women's representation in politics is less in number than men
brings about discussions on the democracy problem. One of the most important factors that create this
situation is that women have a formal representation in politics. To prevent this, parties can create
gender equality in politics by implementing quota activities, ensure that women are pioneers in
meetings, and ensure that parties make women as visible as men in media campaigns. Quota practices
to be implemented for this purpose will make it easier for women to gain more space in the political
arena and for women to have a say in management. In this study, the concept of quota application will
be explained and the practices of the parties in this sense will be included. In addition, in the method
part of the study, female representation rates and quota practices in Europe will be evaluated and
examined in terms of gender equality. For this purpose, although it is seen in the study that European
countries are ahead in quota applications compared to some countries, it is seen that they cannot fully
ensure equality between men and women in politics.
Keywords: Political participation, parties, quota applications, women's representation, media.

merve.ozdemir@bozok.edu.tr

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

      GENDER INEQUALITY IN THE CONSTRUCTION INDUSTRY;
                    WOMEN ARCHITECTS
                                  1. Merve TUTKUN*, 2. Murat TUTKUN†

Abstract
Women have undertaken various roles and responsibilities in society from past to present. While these
roles, which change and diversify under the influence of traditions, cultural norms, economic structure
and social environment, sometimes bring to the fore areas of action where women are more active,
they sometimes bring about discrimination and inequality of opportunities against women, and
therefore their separation from action areas. This understanding, in which men and women are
evaluated separately, has brought about a significant separation, especially in professional acquisition
and business life, and certain sectors have been socially accepted as areas where women cannot take
on a role. In this context, the construction sector, which is generally accepted as a business line in
which men predominantly work, was evaluated within the scope of the study. Engineering and
architectural services, which stand out as professions that require qualified education within the
construction sector, were examined within the scope of the study in the context of women's duties as a
business line, their opportunities to use their potential and the difficulties they experience in the sector.
According to the data published by the Union of Chambers of Turkish Engineers and Architects
(TMMOB), it is known that the total number of engineers and architects in 24 professional chambers
affiliated with TMMOB is 677 thousand 242 people in 2024. Among these professional chambers, it
is also stated in TMMOB data that the chamber with the highest number of female members is the
Chamber of Architects. The number of architects affiliated with the Chamber of Architects reached 80
thousand 789 people according to 2024 data. It is known that among these members, 38 thousand 12
people are women and 42 thousand 777 people are men. With the influence of the traditional belief
system regarding women's social roles, the fact that the architectural professional organization consists
of women at a high rate of 47% within the construction sector, which is generally accepted in the social
framework as a business line in which men are predominantly employed, is an important example in
the context of examining the potential of women in business life. In this context, the difficulties
experienced by women who work within the architectural professional organization in office work and
also in construction site / field work, both during their education periods and in business life, have been
analyzed analytically within the scope of statistical data based on survey studies, as well as theoretical
and conceptual evaluations in the context of gender equality / inequality.

Keywords: Architecture, Women, Gender.

THE MOST EVIDENT EXAMPLE OF FEMALE POVERTY IN TERMS
        OF MATERNAL MORTALITY: AFGHANISTAN
                                                   Merve UYSAL*

Abstract
Poverty transcends being merely an economic issue; it is increasingly becoming a humanitarian and
social problem globally. This situation poses a significant barrier to individuals leading dignified lives
and accessing fundamental human rights. Defining poverty, like other humanitarian issues, can be
challenging. However, if poverty is acknowledged as a significant obstacle to accessing human rights,
it is essential to accurately determine its boundaries and understand its causes thoroughly to overcome
this problem. Furthermore, poverty affects women and men differently, necessitating the development
of gender-specific approaches in solution processes. While women's poverty negatively affects a
country's economic, social, and cultural development, it can be a bigger problem in underdeveloped
countries such as Afghanistan, reducing overall prosperity. Considering the situations in Afghanistan
in recent years, it is clear that its economic and social conditions are not at a desirable level. Women,
due to gender inequality and compounded by issues like poverty that further exacerbate health and
welfare problems, often lack the necessary resources and funding to access adequate health services.
Specifically, maternal mortality is directly related to the accessibility and quality of health services
worldwide. In developed countries, maternal mortality rates are typically low due to high-standard
health systems and accessible medical care. However, in low and middle-income countries, inadequate
health infrastructure, lack of education, and socioeconomic factors often result in higher rates. Poverty
can restrict access to health services, especially in rural areas, and increase the risk of maternal death.
Afghanistan's maternal mortality rate of 620 deaths per 100,000 live births, one of the highest in the
world, highlights the severity of the current problems. Therefore, this study aims first to discuss the
relationship between female poverty and maternal mortality rates and then to assess how conflict, war,
and political instability in Afghanistan affect maternal health services access and maternal mortality
rates using country-specific data. Findings indicate that in some settings in Afghanistan, women are
completely unable to access health services, preventable factors account for a significant portion of
maternal deaths or near-misses, and access has worsened since the change of power. Creating strong
and coherent international pressure on the Taliban to respect the rights of women and children to access
basic health services is critically important for the future of the Afghan population.

Keywords: Poverty, female poverty, maternal mortality rate, Afghanistan.

merve.uysal@final.edu.tr

 EFFECT OF LAUGHTER THERAPY IN WOMEN WITH MIGRAINE
                        1. Meryem ERDOĞAN*, 2. Yasemin ÖZYER GÜVENER†

Abstract
The prevalence of migraine is higher in women, especially in those of reproductive age.
Pharmacological options are the first that comes to mind in the treatment of migraine. This study aimed
to determine the effect of laughter therapy on women with migraine. This randomized controlled study
was carried out with 52 female students with migraine (control: 28, experimental: 24) studying in the
faculty of health sciences of a university. The sample was randomly divided into two groups using the
draw lots method. The experimental group received 20-30-minute laughter therapy once a week for 8
weeks. Data were collected before and after the therapy using an Individual Identification Form created
by the researchers, the Visual Analog Scale, and the Psychological Well-Being Scale. There was no
significant difference between the experimental and control groups in terms of the Psychological Well-
Being Scale (p>0.05). Eight participants in the experimental group had no headache or migraine attack
during the laughter therapy. Furthermore, there was a significant difference between the pre-therapy
and post-therapy pain severity in the experimental and control groups (p<0.05). Laughter therapy can
be effective in reducing pain and preventing migraine attacks in women with migraine. No study has
been found in the literature examining the effect of laughter therapy, one of the alternative treatment
methods, on migraine treatment in women. In our study, it is thought that migraine may reduce pain
severity. However, further studies with larger populations should be conducted. In this context, health
professionals, especially nurse researchers have the most important task. Nurses can both examine the
effect of laughter therapy on patients with migraine and apply this intervention, which has no side
effects, to patients with migraine.

Keywords: Laughter therapy, woman, migraine.

  VIOLENCE IN THE STAGES OF WOMEN'S LIFE: RISKS AND THE
                    ROLE OF THE NURSE
                      1. Meryem METİNOĞLU*, 2. Aysun PERİM KETENCİLER †

Abstract
When the life stages of women are evaluated in the context of sexual and reproductive health, they are
examined in six stages: neonatal, childhood, adolescence, sexual maturity, menopausal, and elderly.
Violence is a possible risk that women may encounter at any stage of their lives and is a public health
issue experienced or being experienced by many women. Therefore, the aim of this study is to identify
potential violence risks in important periods of a woman's life cycle and to specify the roles of nurses
in this regard. In the definition of violence, which is an interdisciplinary subject, actions based on
intentional or societal norms, such as physical or psychological harm, hindering personal development,
deprivation, etc., are included. Violence is a possible risk at any stage of women's lives. For example,
during adolescence, there is a possibility of experiencing domestic violence, while during sexual
maturity, there is an increased likelihood of encountering partner violence or caregiver violence during
elderly age. Individuals who are exposed to or witness violence at any stage of life are known to have
a higher risk of becoming individuals who apply or are exposed to violence in the future. As Dr. Fredric
Leboyer mentioned in his 1975 book "Birth Without Violence," violence can sometimes start in the
delivery room with the first unconscious slap on the baby's buttocks to initiate the first breath.
Furthermore, Leboyer argues unattractively that health care workers are directed to choose their
professional careers due to the effect of this information deeply embedded in the subconscious layers.
The impact of violence on human behavior may increase the number of individuals who tend to
normalize violence. Without breaking this cycle, it will be very difficult to create violence-free healthy
societies. It is important for nurses who serve in health care delivery from the prenatal period to every
stage of women's lives to be aware of possible violence risks, to know their role in prevention, healing,
and protection, and to work in complete readiness. Nurses, along with the disciplinary work disciplines
towards individuals, families, and society, are key professionals in addressing violence against women
at every stage of a woman's life.
Keywords: Violence, stages of women's lives, role of nurses.

    CLIMATE CRISIS AND WOMEN: INEQUALITIES, SOLIDARITY
                      AND SOLUTIONS
                                1. Mine KORKUT *, 2. Hatice Esra BAKIR†

Abstract
Climate change, which refers to the deviation of temperature and weather patterns around the world
from their natural cycles in long-term processes, affects basic living areas such as health, food security,
shelter, security and employment, and affects especially vulnerable groups even more negatively. In
this aspect, climate change; In addition to the subjects of climate and eco systems such as climatology
and atmospheric science, it has also begun to be included in the field of social sciences. Challenges
such as drought, food security and water shortage, which are among the most important globalized
problems of the climate crisis, further increase inequalities by deepening poverty and disadvantage,
especially among vulnerable groups. Women are among the groups most affected in this field in
economic, social, cultural and physical terms. Women who do not have the opportunity to access
education are at greater physical risk due to reasons such as turning to agriculture, water supply and
other nature-related works, and not having skills such as swimming in times of disaster. In addition,
they feel the effects of climate change more in terms of forced migration, maternal roles and women's
health. In disasters caused by the climate crisis, women are again at a disadvantage in accessing basic
needs such as health, water and food, as well as losing income; This situation causes women to stand
out as a prominent actor in climate change. The fact that women generally have livelihoods close to
agriculture and nature also plays a critical role in the measures that need to be taken to deal with the
climate crisis. The fact that women generally have livelihoods close to agriculture and nature also plays
a critical role in the measures that need to be taken to deal with the climate crisis. Women develop
innovative solutions in areas such as livelihoods, sustainable agricultural practices, water and energy
management. They strengthen their communities for their livelihoods by establishing local solidarity
networks. Moreover, women's training on the climate crisis not only enables them to combat climate
change in a more conscious, skilled and effective manner, but also improves their leadership skills and
solution-oriented approaches in the field. This article discusses the effects of the climate crisis on
women and the role of women's coping strategies in the context of this crisis. This article was created
by literature review method, based on the analysis of existing research findings. As a result, it is
emphasized that reducing gender inequalities and empowering women can increase the capacity to
cope with the climate crisis.

Keywords: Climate change, climate crisis, resilience, solidarity, vulnerable group, woman.

   SEXUAL HEALTH LITERACY AND STIGMA IN YOUNG WOMEN
                     1. Mukaddes Turan MİRAL*, 2. Beyza ŞAHİN†, 3. Buse ÇAKIN‡

Abstract
Fear of stigma can be a significant barrier to young women accessing accurate and reliable information
on sexual health. Increasing the level of sexual health literacy (SHL) can change the individual's
perception of stigmatization related to sexual health. However, no study evaluating SHL and
stigmatization together in young women was found in the existing literature. This study aimed to
determine the relationship between SHL and stigmatization in young women. This cross-sectional
descriptive and correlation-seeking study was conducted at a foundation university in Istanbul between
December 2023 and April 2024. The sample was composed of women between the ages of 18-25. Data;
The Participant Information Form was collected using the Sexual Health Literacy (SHL) Scale and the
Sexual and Reproductive Health Stigma in Young Women Scale (SRHSYWS). 432 women participated
in the study. 79.4% of the participants did not receive sexual health education. The largest group of
sexual health information sources were social media (32.9%), friends (32.2%) and school (31.7%). The
main obstacles they perceive in accessing sexual health information are not discussing sexuality
(57.2%), not being a reliable source of information (33.8%) and fear of stigma (26.6%). The SHL Scale
score of the participants is 53.85±10.94, and the score shown by the SRHSYWS is 6.62±3.95. There
was no significant relationship between the two scale scores (r= -.09, p> .05). In sexual health
education, the SHL Scale score (58.87±9.39) is higher and the SRHSYWS score (5.69±3.42) is lower.
Those who received information about sexual health from scientific publications (57.11±9.67), school
(56.46±12.09) and friends (55.49±9.90) had higher SHL Scale scores. Those who study in a field other
than health (7.41±3.99), have a sexual partner (7.64±4.22), do not receive sexual health education
(6.86±4.04), and those who get basic information about sexual health from social media (7.54±3.67)
and friends (7.65±4.22) have higher SRHSYWS scores. In conclusion; young women's SHL level and
sexual and reproductive health stigmatization level are moderate, and no significant relationship was
found between SHL and stigmatization. Sexual health education increases SHL and reduces the level
of stigmatization. The most important obstacle to accessing sexual health information is not discussing
sexuality.

Keywords: Sexual health, sexual health literacy, stigma, reproductive health, women

  THE MATERNAL EXPERIENCES OF SYRIAN MIGRANT WOMEN
            UNDER TEMPORARY PROTECTION
                                               Mukaddes YETER*

Abstract
Migration, an ongoing movement driven by factors such as wars, exiles, hunger, unemployment, and
economic hardships for centuries, is recognized as a significant social phenomenon that transforms the
entire social structure alongside the physical displacement. Individuals and communities, through
migration, not only relocate geographically but also reconstruct all the relationships encompassing
their individual and social lives. However, the changes and transformations experienced in the
aftermath of migration do not always yield positive outcomes; depending on the circumstances and
timing, they can give rise to various problems for both migrants and host countries. In 2011, due to the
war in Syria, people had to seek refuge in neighboring countries, primarily Turkey, in large numbers.
Turkey initially welcomed Syrian migrants as guests and pursued an open-door policy. Turkey,
establishing refugee admission in line with the 1951 Geneva Convention, attempted to address the legal
status of Syrian immigrants initially accepted as guests, aiming to find solutions through legal avenues
in the form of "temporary protection statute", with the continuation of the Syrian war. While the
phenomenon of migration itself presents a series of challenges, it exacerbates greater hardships for
socially disadvantaged groups such as women, the elderly, the disabled, and children during the
migration process. The changes experienced by women as immigrants, particularly stemming from
their roles as mothers, while raising their children in a foreign society, including adjustments related to
childcare, the child's education and adaptation to social life outside the home, significantly impact the
mothering experiences of immigrant mothers. Therefore, the necessity arises to evaluate women
separately during the migration process and to establish supportive policies accordingly. This study
focuses on the motherhood experiences of Syrian migrant mothers under temporary protection in our
country. The study aims to explore the types of problems encountered by migrant mothers, the methods
they employ to address these problems, whether a new form of motherhood emerges, and if so, to
identify the nature of this motherhood. The study population of this research consists of 25 Syrian
migrant mothers, and it was conducted using feminist methodology, including literature review,
fieldwork, data analysis, and interpretation. The research reveals that migrant mothers' statuses and
roles differ, changes in gender roles and economic conditions lead to physical and psychological
problems, language barriers prevent adequate contribution to their children's education, and they
exhibit a more reserved form of motherhood in social settings.
Keywords: Migration, migrant women, motherhood under temporary protection

mukaddes.ytr@gmail.com

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

                 FEMICIDES IN OTTOMAN EUROPE (1903-1908)
                                                 Mustafa KOÇ*

Abstract
Femicide refers to intentional killing of women (or girls) because they are women or girls. This study
focuses on the femicides that took place in the Ottoman Europe between 1903 and 1908 and searches
for answers to the questions "What are the main motivations of femicides? Who are the actors of
murders? Which tools were used in murders? How common were murders in rural and urban areas?
How did the Ottoman security services manage crises that resulted from femicides? What kind of
precautions did the Ottoman bureaucracy take against femicides?". Method of the study is case
analysis, and the main source is documents accessed through the Ottoman Archive. The chronology
starts with the Rebellion of İlinden and ends with the Young Turk Revolution. In addition to archival
documents, there are two period photographs of Ottoman women in the study. These photographs were
selected from Getty Research Institute Special Collections online catalogue and used with the
permission of the Institute. Unlike classical case analysis, the text was structured with the victims of
violence at the centre. For this reason, first of all the cases of violence against women in the Ottoman
empire which resulted in death were determined. The findings show that the main perpetrators of
femicide were bandits. Many women who helped the Muslim community or who were thought to have
betrayed (such as reporting) were killed at different times by bandits/gangs/committees. Apart from
bandits, we can see family members or friends as perpetrators of murder. There are various scenes of
murder such as house, field, forest, neighbourhood and riverside. Rifles, pistols and bombs were
frequently used as murder weapons. The only case of beating to death was between two women. The
murdered women were predominantly Muslim women. However, there was no distinction of identity
in femicides. It is possible to see many murdered Bulgarian, Greek and Vlach women in archival
documents. Especially the issue of mass femicides (three-four-five women killed at the same time) are
interesting among the findings. The highest number of murders confirmed is seven. The results show
that most of the murdered women did not know their murderers. In most of the murders, there were no
past relationships between the individuals. This result shows that –apart from few examples- women
were killed simply because they were women.
Keywords: Femicides, Balkans, violence in the Ottoman Empire.

  WOMEN'S ACCESS TO PUBLIC SPACE: A COMPARATIVE STUDY
              IN CONSTANTINE AND ROME
         1. Nawel ACHOUR-BOUAKKAZ*, 2. Ouafa SAIGHI†, 3. Daniela DE LEO‡, 4. Asma
                        BENBOUHEDJA§, 5. Oulfa Nouha KOURI**

Abstract
The purpose of this study is to investigate the access of women to public space in two cities:
Constantine in Algeria and Rome in Italy. The study is conducted with a quantitative and qualitative
research method. The first one is based on a survey with questionnaire with two groups of women who
live and work in Constantine (30) (Algeria) and Rome (20) (Italy) by observing their daily journeys
between the domestic sphere and the urban space with the aim of making them visible. The second one
was conducted with semi-structured interviews with ten women who share their experiences of living
in these two cities. Thus, considering with Bourdieu, the access to space as “a cultural act that consists
in drawing the line that produces a separate, delimited space” we observed that this line tends to
disappear. Indeed, the number of women who have access to the spaces of their city, takes on greater
thickness when we observe the quality of this access...governed, by a set of rules, laws and socio-
spatial constraints. In this study, women's access to space seems to be subject to the same social and
cultural dynamics in both cities, involving an investment in outdoor spaces in the search for a better
balance in the dialectics facing women, i.e. "tradition and modernity", "housewife and working
woman", etc. Nonetheless the observation, analysis and interpretation in a comparative approach
reveals: the reality of women's presence in urban space in these two cities on both shores of the
Mediterranean; the mechanisms of adaptation and/or avoidance that they put in place to guarantee their
right to the city in urban space.

Keywords: Women, access space, spatial practices, comparative approach, Constantine, Rome.

        THE WORK-LIFE STRUGGLE OF WOMEN SCIENTISTS IN
                         UZBEKISTAN
                                         Nigora MAMADAMINOVA*

Abstract
This study investigates the work-life balance experienced by women scientists in Uzbekistan, shedding
light on their unique challenges and coping mechanisms within the context of a developing nation. In
Uzbekistan, women typically marry in their early twenties and often reside in extended family
households where familial obligations are extensive. These responsibilities, compounded by societal
expectations of immediate motherhood and the cultural norm of multi-generational living
arrangements, pose significant challenges for women seeking to pursue careers and engage actively in
society. Despite these obstacles, the Uzbek government recognizes the importance of gender equality
and is actively promoting women's participation in higher education and scientific endeavors. The
academic literature on women in academy highlights the challenge of work-family conflict and work-
life balance as a significant barrier to women advancing into administrative positions. Informal gender
patterns in universities contribute to differential career outcomes for men and women in academia,
with senior colleagues often perceiving that women must choose between career and family due to the
'motherhood myth'. Utilizing a qualitative approach, semi-structured interviews were conducted with
20 women scientists across various fields and career stages in Uzbekistan. Thematic analysis revealed
multifaceted factors influencing work-life balance, including institutional support, societal
expectations, familial responsibilities, and personal aspirations. Findings indicate a complex interplay
between professional advancement and personal fulfillment, as participants navigate traditional gender
roles, cultural norms, and organizational structures. Furthermore, the study highlights the role of policy
interventions and organizational initiatives in fostering a more equitable and supportive work
environment for women scientists. By amplifying the voices of women scientists in Uzbekistan, this
research contributes to the broader discourse on gender equality, organizational diversity, and
sustainable development. Recommendations for stakeholders include the implementation of family-
friendly policies, mentorship programs, and cultural sensitization initiatives to enhance work-life
balance and promote gender inclusivity in the scientific workforce.
Keywords: Work-life balance, women scientists, Uzbekistan, gender equality

n.mamadamino@student.uw.edu.pl

                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

     THE CHANGES IN DIETARY CONSUMPTION FREQUENCY IN
              WOMEN DURING MENSTRUATION
                1. Nur Bahar KURU AKTÜRK*, 2. Aydan YILMAZ†, 3. Merve KOLCU‡

Abstract
This study was conducted to determine changes in dietary consumption frequency among women
during menstruation. This cross-sectional study was conducted with a preliminary sample of 109
women aged 18-45 years. For data collection, a Demographic Information Form prepared by the
researchers according to the literature and the Dietary Consumption Frequency Change Scale, which
was validated in Turkish by Başaran and Pekmezci Burut in 2022, were used. The data were evaluated
using mean, standard deviation, frequency, percentage, and dependent group t-test methods with the
Statistical Package for Social Sciences (SPSS) 25.0 Windows program, and a statistical significance
level of p<0.05 was accepted. Ethical approval for the study was obtained. It was determined that the
average age of the women participating in the research was 26.2±7.1, 61.5% were university graduates,
56% had an income equal to their expenses, and only 26.1% of the women were working. The
menstrual cycle of 54.1% of women lasts every 21-28 days and 56.1% lasts 6-9 days. It was reported
that 80.7% of women had an increased need for sweets during menstruation and 41.3% experienced
nausea. 56.0% eat at least two meals a day and 74.3% consume snacks. 93.6% of women stated that
there was no food they did not eat during menstruation. A significant difference was found in women's
intake of herbal teas, whole wheat bread, ayran-kefir, calcium-magnesium and zinc supplements,
probiotics and propolis during menstruation compared to before menstruation (p<0.05). In addition, no
significant difference was found in red meat, white meat, fish, eggs, salt and sugar intake compared to
pre-menstrual periods. Hormonal fluctuations related to the menstrual cycle can affect appetite control
and eating behavior and increase food consumption. Ensuring appetite control and paying attention to
the diet will make the process more comfortable. This study is a preliminary study and working with a
larger sample will increase the reliability of the results.

Keywords: Menstruation, nutrition, women's health.

  BIBLIOMETRIC ANALYSIS OF THESES ON BREASTFEEDING IN
            GRADUATE PROGRAMS (2018-2023)
                1. Nur Bahar KURU AKTÜRK*, 2. Beyzanur İŞBAY†, 3. Melike DİŞSİZ‡

Abstract
Breastfeeding is a very wide area in which many issues such as breastfeeding positions, breastfeeding
problems, evaluation of post-breastfeeding education processes, parents' breastfeeding attitude,
mother's breastfeeding success and self-efficacy, breastfeeding perception, breastfeeding comfort can
be studied, which can be affected by numerous factors, benefiting mother and baby. Many graduate
theses have been written on breastfeeding in Turkey, and this study has been planned to guide the
graduate theses to be done on this subject by examining the completed graduate theses, determining
the specialty area, number of samples, sample group and keyword distribution of the theses. This
research aims to determine postgraduate research trends by examining medical specialty, master's and
doctoral theses on breastfeeding-related issues between 2018 and 2023, and to create a chronological
bibliography of postgraduate theses on breastfeeding. Master's, doctoral and medical specialty theses
were scanned using the word 'Breastfeeding' between 01.04.2023 and 26.04.2023, and studies
conducted on and after 01.01.2018 were included in the research. All thesis studies published between
01.01.2018 and 15.09.2023 in the internet database called "National Thesis Center" of the Council of
Higher Education (YÖK) constitute the universe of the research. In this context, 277 theses were
included in the research. Of the 277 theses included in the research, 197 were determined as master's
theses, 31 doctoral theses and 49 medical specialty theses. The examined postgraduate theses were
reviewed and analyzed using the bibliographic analysis method in terms of features such as the year of
the thesis published, the type of the thesis, the number of pages, the gender of the author, the title of
the advisor, the number of samples, the sample group and keywords. In the bibliometric analysis,
answers were sought to a total of 8 questions within the scope of the research. In the research, most
postgraduate theses are; It was carried out at the University of Health Sciences, Ege University and
Atatürk University; It was determined that the language of 98.6% was Turkish, the method of 97.5%
was quantitative and the type of 23.4% was randomized controlled experimental type. Considering the
first three sample groups most used in research; 62.1% are mothers; It was determined that 16.2% were
pregnant women and 7.2% were women of reproductive age.
Keywords: Bibliographic Analysis, breastfeeding, thesis review.

 WOMEN’S POVERTY FROM A MACROECONOMIC PERSPECTIVE
                                               Nuran H. BELET*

Abstract
Following the transition to a settled lifestyle, social inequalities emerged, prompting efforts to address
them. Over time, various definitions and methodologies for measuring poverty have been developed,
reflecting shifts in its socio-economic impacts. Poverty is generally defined as the inability to meet
basic needs, resulting in substandard living conditions. Given the multifaceted nature and implications
of poverty, its classifications and measurement techniques vary accordingly. Geographical and cultural
differences among countries significantly influence the concept and processes of poverty. Since the
1960s, there has been a growing focus on the disparities between men and women, leading to increased
scrutiny of women's poverty. An analysis of the macroeconomic causes and consequences of poverty,
such as welfare loss and income distribution imbalances, reveals that women's poverty exacerbates
intersectional inequalities, further impeding macroeconomic and societal development. The
recognition of poverty's multidimensionality has been highlighted by the evolution of indices used for
its measurement. Nevertheless, the principal obstacles to accurately assessing poverty lie in the
complexities of data collection and management. The factors contributing to women's poverty include
limited economic participation, inadequate access to market-relevant skills and competencies, gender-
based stereotypes, and the absence of gender-sensitive budgeting. The prevalence of women's poverty
has been particularly evident since the implementation of neoliberal policies on a global scale
following 1980. The multidimensionality and significant disparities in welfare levels across countries
present a challenge to the formulation of universal poverty alleviation strategies. This study will
examine poverty from a macroeconomic perspective, with a particular focus on the impact of gender-
based macroeconomic policies on women's poverty. Furthermore, the study will address the challenges
associated with measuring poverty, particularly in relation to conceptual frameworks and calculation
methodologies. This paper examines the macroeconomic dimensions of poverty, with a particular focus
on gender disparities and the associated measurement challenges.
Keywords: Poverty, women's poverty, macro economy, gender.

nbelet@gmail.com

 WOMEN VICTIMS OF WAR AND THE ETHICS OF WAR: THE CASE
                       OF GAZA
                 1. Nurefşan ODABAŞ*, 2. Beyza Nur YILMAZ†, 3. Rümeysa PIRTICI‡

Abstract
The religious, political, cultural and economic conflicts that started in the region after the Ottoman
withdrawal and Israel's occupation policies towards these areas have led to many human rights
violations in the Palestinian territories until today. Many Palestinians have been displaced and forced
to live in refugee camps or migrate to different countries. On October 7, 2023, the Gaza operation
deepened the humanitarian crisis in Palestine. Gaza has been bombarded from the air. Hospitals,
schools, refugee camps, civilian settlements have been targeted and many civilians have lost their lives
due to Israeli bombardment. International press and media outlets have been covering the events.
According to the latest data, in Israel, 33.686 people, including 14.560 children and 9.582 women,
were killed in Gaza since October 7. Thousands of Palestinians, especially women and children, cannot
access food, water and medical supplies due to the denial of humanitarian aid to the region. 37 mothers
die every day from the attacks. This study has been prepared through in-depth research and qualitative
method. The data collection process of the research was carried out by examining the reports of
international institutions, news outlets and organizations, official statements and detailed research of
the relevant literature. As a sample for this study; in the Israeli attack on the Al-Ahli Hospital in Gaza
on October 17 resulted with 471 dead and 342 injured people. Women undergoing treatment for
childbirth were reported to be trapped under collapsed buildings, leading other pregnant women to seek
treatment in unhygienic places and wait for delivery. According to the ethics of warfare, the operation
in hospitals is a violation. There are also allegations of rape of captured Palestinian women and
children. It’s against the rules regarding the treatment of prisoners. It’s seen that the rules and conditions
were not met. In this study, the war crimes and human rights violations that have occurred in the Gaza
operation from October 7, 2023 to the present day will be revealed. The bio-psycho-social problems
experienced by women victims of war will be discussed.
Keywords: Gaza, Israel, Palestine, ethics of War, women.

      THE IMPORTANCE OF BREAST CONSERVING SURGERY IN
            BREAST CANCER TREATMENT FOR WOMEN
                                1. Nursema AKSÖZ*, 2. Gülseren DAĞLAR†

Abstract
This review was written to draw attention to the importance of breast conserving surgery (BCS) in the
treatment of breast cancer for women's body perception. The keywords were searched in PubMed,
Google Scholar and Yöktez search engines. Breast cancer is the most common type of cancer in women
and can result in death. One in every eight women will experience invasive breast cancer during her
lifetime. Although the incidence of breast cancer has been increasing worldwide over the years, there
has been a decrease in breast cancer-related deaths due to improvements in treatment. In the primary
treatment of breast cancer, mastectomy or BCS may be preferred depending on the location, size and
spread of the tumour. Mastectomy is the surgical removal of the entire breast tissue. In many societies,
a woman's breast is seen as a symbol of aesthetic appearance, sexuality, motherhood and feeding the
baby. Loss of breast with mastectomy is perceived as loss of femininity, fertility, attractiveness,
sexuality and sexual identity, and this may cause various psychosocial problems due to deterioration
in the woman's body image. BCS is ‘the safe removal of only the breast tumour from the breast while
preserving the breast tissue, leaving no cancer tissue behind’. It is noteworthy that in Türkiye, the rate
of BCS has increased from 25% to 39.3%, while the rate of mastectomy has decreased from 75% to
60.7%. In the literature review, it has been observed that BCS has a positive effect on women's life and
treatment process compared to total mastectomy, women are more advantageous in terms of body
perception and sexual functions, and they feel better. There are studies showing that body perception,
sexual satisfaction and self-esteem of women who have undergone BCS operation are higher compared
to mastectomy. Midwives, who are specialised in the protection and promotion of women's health, are
health professionals who provide quality service in the protection of women's breast health, prevention
of breast cancer, breast cancer treatment process and postoperative care of women, protection and
development of biopsychosocial health of women and their families. For this reason, it is very
important that midwives should definitely be included in the multidisciplinary team and take a more
active role in the care and treatment process of patients diagnosed with breast cancer.

Keywords: Midwifery, breast cancer, breast-conserving surgery.
