   EVALUATION OF STUDENTS' GENITAL HYGIENE BEHAVIORS
               1. Nurten Denizhan KIRCAN*, 2. Berna Kaya IBIS†, 3. Sezer Er GÜNERİ‡

Abstract
Genital infections caused by inadequate genital hygiene practices are an important reproductive health
problem that women in developed and developing countries face at any time of their lives. The study
was conducted to determine the genital hygiene behaviors of students. The population of the descriptive
and cross-sectional study consisted of all female students (n: 420) enrolled in Muğla Sıtkı Koçman
University Fethiye Faculty of Health Sciences between October and December 2019. No sample
selection was made in the study, and all female students who agreed to participate in the study and
completed the questionnaire forms completely were included in the sample (n: 238). Data were
collected by self-report method using a descriptive questionnaire form consisting of 12 questions
prepared by the researchers and the genital hygiene behaviors scale consisting of 23 Likert-type
questions developed by Karahan (2017) to evaluate women's genital hygiene behaviors. In the
evaluation of the data; numbers, percentages, mean, standard deviation were used. It was determined
that all of the students were single, 39.1% stayed in three-four-person rooms, 78.6% had a nuclear
family type, 53% had a primary school graduate mother, and 79.8% had social security. It was
determined that 21.8% of the students' current genital discharge characteristics were colored
(pathological), and 85.7% defined the meaning of genital discharge as a normal process. It was
determined that 21.0% of the students always and 42% sometimes used soap for cleaning the genital
area, 63.4% changed their underwear once a day, and 85.3% practiced front-to-back urination. The
mean genital hygiene score of the students was 50.07±4.56, the mean menstrual hygiene score was
35.48±3.37, the mean abnormal finding awareness score was 11.92±2.71 and it was determined that
they had inadequate genital hygiene behaviors. It was found that there was a statistically significant
difference between the mean scale scores of the students who were studying in the first grade, whose
mother's education level was primary school graduate, who had low income, who used soap in genital
cleaning, who did not change underwear every day and who had the habit of cleaning the genitals from
back to front (p<0.05). It was found that students' genital hygiene behaviors were inadequate.
Therefore, it is recommended that students should be trained to acquire correct genital hygiene
behaviors.
Keywords: Genital hygiene, genital infection, behavior, student.

    OPINIONS OF IRANIAN STUDENTS AT ERCIYES UNIVERSITY
         REGARDING GENDER DISCRIMINATION IN IRAN
                            1. Omid MOLLAZAEI*, 2. Tuğçe AYATA DOĞAN†

Abstract
Equality between men and women is a fundamental prerequisite for upholding human rights and
promoting social justice, as well as for fostering development and peace. Throughout history and across
diverse cultures, the relationship between men and women has predominantly been characterized by
patriarchal norms. Gender discrimination remains a prevalent issue worldwide, particularly in South
and East Asia. In Iran, the status of women in society has been the subject of increased scrutiny since
the Islamic revolution in 1979. Gender discrimination persists in Iran, where women are not accorded
the same rights as men and male superiority is often accepted. The aim of this research is to examine
the perspectives and experiences of Iranian students enrolled at Erciyes University regarding gender
discrimination. Specifically, the research seeks to illuminate women's thoughts and experiences
pertaining to gender equality. To achieve this, an in-depth analysis is conducted employing qualitative
research methods. The research involves conducting focused group interviews with 11 Iranian students
enrolled at Erciyes University. The qualitative research method is employed to delve deeply into
individuals' experiences, thoughts, and emotions. This method offers an effective means of
comprehending participants' viewpoints and elucidating their lived experiences. The findings of the
research indicate that Iranian students at Erciyes University possess a high level of awareness regarding
gender discrimination. Participants highlighted instances of discrimination faced by women in Iran,
such as in the realms of marriage, divorce, inheritance, custody, and travel. Moreover, a heightened
awareness was observed concerning the low social status of women and their limited access to equal
opportunities in the workplace. These findings underscore the pressing need for the Iranian government
to actively support women's rights and work toward achieving gender equality. By drawing attention
to the research outcomes, policymakers and society at large can be prompted to foster greater awareness
regarding women's rights and the imperative of gender equality. Ultimately, this research aims to shed
light on Iranian students' perspectives on gender discrimination in Iran, with the overarching goal of
fostering awareness and driving societal change in this domain.
Keywords: Gender discrimination, women's status, Iran, Erciyes University.

       PUBLIC SERVICE ANNOUNCEMENT ON THE THEME OF
      DISCRIMINATION AGAINST WOMEN: A CROSS-CULTURAL
                         APPROACH
                                1. Ömer AYDINLIOĞLU*, 2. Fikriye ÇELİK†

Abstract
Women and men differ in terms of their anatomical characteristics as well as the gender roles they
undertake in society. The discrimination that women are exposed to in both private and public spheres
stems from the norms and structure of the patriarchal society. Patriarchal society places women in a
submissive position and treats them as sexual objects. This approach sometimes gains visibility as a
theme in public service announcements aiming to create social change. A public service announcement
is a communication tool that has the role of changing existing behaviour and/or creating new behaviour.
It has the function of raising awareness in people about the social problems included in the scope of
the media content. In line with this function, public service announcements on discrimination against
women aired in Turkey and Ukraine were analysed. The aim of the research is to reveal through which
gender the theme of discrimination, which is prominent in the analysed public service announcements,
gains visibility. Evaluating the social and cultural perspective specific to the theme of discrimination
against women through the public service announcements of two different countries points to the
unique value of the study. Two public service announcements on the theme of discrimination against
women, selected from Turkey and Ukraine by purposive sampling method, were analysed through
semiotics. Both visual and linguistic indicator differences were found in the message production of the
examined public service announcements; it was understood that the use of appealing elements such as
humour, fear, sexuality, violence and music stemmed from the dominant social perspective depending
on the culture. As a result of the research, it was observed that while the theme of discrimination against
women in public service announcements aired in Turkey was addressed through the superiority of men
over women, public service announcements aired in Ukraine addressed the same theme through the
representation of a disadvantaged and inadequate man against women. The study has shown that
culture, which is the prominent factor in determining gender roles, is also an important determinant of
media content that directly targets behavioural patterns such as public service announcements.

Keywords: Communication studies, public service announcement, advertising research,
discrimination against women, semiotics.

    ANALYSIS OF DISCURSIVE CONSTRUCTION OF WOMEN IN
  PRIMARY SCHOOL TURKISH TEXTBOOKS WITHIN THE SCOPE
           OF DISCOURSE HISTORICAL APPROACH
                             1. Özgül BAŞARAN*, 2. Gülsüm Songül ERCAN†

Abstract
Textbooks, one of the most important tools used in education, provide a road map to teachers and
students to ensure that the curriculum is carried out within a certain framework. In addition to this basic
function, textbooks are an important socialization tool that transfers social, political, cultural, economic
norms and values of society to new generations. In this respect, they directly and/or indirectly transfer
cultural codes to students. Gender and gender roles constitute one of these codes which are transferred
to students, in explicit or implicit ways. Culturally, woman's social identity is mostly defined by their
role in marriage and family, while man's identity is associated with work, success and intelligence.
However, the gender equality movement globally has contributed a lot to challenge the said
stereotyping on all grounds. Raising gender equality awareness in educational institutions should be
one of the areas of this transformation and textbooks are supposed to have been designed to reflect this
change. Based on this view, the aim of our study is to reveal whether textbooks maintain the existing
traditional gender roles of women or support gender equality by analyzing the textbooks taught in
Turkish classes in the 2022-2023 academic year at primary school level, which can be considered to
be the period for students to recognize gender roles. For the analysis of the data, proposed within the
frame of Discourse-Historical Approach (DHA) (Wodak, 2001; Reisigl & Wodak, 2017), nomination
and predication strategies with their linguistic devices such as (i) membership categorization (deictics,
anthroponyms, etc.), (ii) tropes such as metaphors, metonymies and synecdoches, (iii) verbs and nouns
(iv) explicit predicates and predicative nouns/adjectives/pronouns have been investigated in the
textbooks. Results show that stereotypical gender roles can still be traced on the textbooks and the
details of findings will be presented at the conference.
Keywords: Gender, women, textbooks, discourse historical approach, nomination strategy

         CLIMATE CHANGE AND ITS EFFECTS ON PREGNANCY
                                              Özlem AKALPLER*

Abstract
According to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change, “Climate change” is
defined as a change in climate resulting from human activities that directly or indirectly disrupt the
composition of the global atmosphere, in addition to natural climate change observed over comparable
time periods. The World Health Organization (WHO) states that being aware of climate change and
being prepared for what will happen is important in reducing its negative health effects. According to
WHO, approximately 250 thousand new/additional deaths are expected annually due to climate change
between 2030 and 2050. The consequences of climate change are evaluated not only with its ethical
dimension but also with its economic cost. It is stated that climate change will be effective all over the
world but will mostly affect developing countries with low coping capacity, which are fragile and in
need of foreign aid. Women are vulnerable to climate change-induced food shortage due to increased
nutritional needs during menstruation, pregnancy and the postpartum period. Climate change also
causes early marriages due to migration and an increase in the problem of gender-based violence
among women. Failure to maintain some services during natural disasters negatively affects access to
sexual and reproductive health services. Exposure to air pollution, starting from the pre-pregnancy
period, can negatively affect the health of both the mother and the fetus. Vector-borne diseases are
important conditions that can cause vertical transmission from mother to fetus during the intrauterine
period and also affect the fetus. The warming of the earth's surface and more frequent heat waves due
to climate change may affect the severity or frequency of hot flashes experienced by women during
the menopausal period. There are many studies in the literature examining the effects of environmental
air pollution on fertility or reproductive health. Climate change can also negatively affect pregnancy
by affecting women's menstrual cycle patterns. It is important for all health professionals, especially
nurses, who play the role of patient advocates, to know the problems and solutions that women may
encounter in all stages of their lives and to raise women's awareness of these issues.
Keywords: Pregnancy, climate change, effect.

                                                                                            Turkish Presentation

ozlem.akalpler@neu.edu.tr.

                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV

            REFUGEE AND DISPLACED WOMEN AS VICTIMS OF
                  TRAFFICKING AND EXPLOITATION
                                                 Pablo BANCHIO*

Abstract
Trafficking, smuggling, sexual exploitation, and abuse of migrant women are complex phenomena that
pose significant challenges from a legal perspective. Migrant women can be exploited in a variety of
ways, including labour exploitation, trafficking, forced labour or domestic servitude. Also physical,
psychological, and sexual violence by partners, family members, employers or clients. However, one
of the main ways in which migrant women become victims of trafficking is through sexual exploitation,
prostitution or pornography. Often, these women begin their journey knowingly and willingly with
promises of work as childcare or accompanying the elderly, but end up being exploited due to various
circumstances, such as extortion, deception or forced debt. Many are forced to work to pay off debts
owed to traffickers, exposing them to inhumane trafficking conditions and the risk of exploitation even
after the debt is repaid. Sex trafficking is one of the most common forms of exploitation faced by
migrant women. It involves the prostitution of adults or children against their will, using force or other
coercive means. In many cases, women are forced to have sex with police officers as a form of payment
to facilitate their passage across borders. An alarming example of this phenomenon is the case of
Nigerian migrant women. According to the US State Department's Human Rights Report, thousands
of Nigerian women and girls arrived in Europe by sea, and most of them were sexually exploited by
criminal networks taking advantage of the chaos caused by the migration crisis. In conclusion, the
trafficking, exploitation, and abuse of migrant women are urgent problems that require a coordinated
international legal and social response. Addressing the underlying causes of these phenomena, such as
poverty, gender inequality and lack of economic opportunities, is essential to protect the human rights
of all migrants. Our proposal is the legal response to the human right not to migrate as a way to protect
the rights of all women and girls who wish to remain in their countries in order to freely develop their
life project there without being forced to make these desperate journeys to achieve it.

Keywords: Refugee and displaced women, smuggling and sexual exploitation, economic
vulnerability, human trafficking.

     WOMEN IN COMPUTER SCIENCE: FROM THE MANHATTAN
                 PROJECT TO THE PRESENT
                                             Pakize ERDOĞMUŞ*

Abstract
As in the fields of Science, Technology, Engineering and Mathematics (STEM), innovative ideas that
create value and make a difference in the field of computer science have become possible thanks to
strong women researchers working in the field of computer science. From the first woman Computer
Scientist, Ada Lovelace to today, there have been a lot of successful computer scientists like Grace
Murray Hopper. From mechanical computers to electronics, from digital computers to quantum
computers there have been numerous women computer scientists. Although the Manhattan Project is
not considered a pure computer science project, it is included in this study because many women
computers were involved in the project. The aim of this article is to present successful women in
Computer Science from the Manhattan project to today. For this purpose, successful women in this
field have been selected and various attributes such as area of expertise, contributions and
achievements and active time periods about women researchers have been collected. These features
have been examined from a computer science perspective and the results have been presented. As a
result, women in computer science bring diverse perspectives, equalize the gender biases. Women's
involvement in computer science ensures the technology products and solutions designed with diverse
user needs in mind, more safe and secure technologies for everyone. Especially today, AI technology
research for the benefit of humanity can be inclusive due to women.
Keywords: Woman, computers, computer science.

                                                                                              English Presentation




* Prof., Düzce University, Computer Engineering, Turkey, ORCID: 0000-0003-2172-5767, email:

pakizeerdogmus@duzce.edu.tr


                                                        135
                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

  GENDER INEQUALITY IN COMPUTER SCIENCE AND REASONS
                   AND SOLUTIONS?
                                             Pakize ERDOĞMUŞ*

Abstract
Although the gender ratio in humans, like most other species in the world, is approximately one to one,
the number of women in computer science is not proportionally equal, as it is in most professional
fields. Gender specific roles, educational discrimination for genders are important reasons of today
gender inequality in most of the profession as it is in computer science. In this study, it has been
researched the reason of the gender inequality in computer science and the solutions for making the
equal genders in computer science. With this aim, the articles listed from Web of Science, published
especially last decade have been evaluated, to present the big picture. According to the results, the main
reasons of the gender inequality in computer sciences are faculty environments, dominated by men,
low confidence of female students in undergraduate or graduate level because of the sexist comments,
stereotypes and adopted wrong educational applications, and biased course materials. The stereotypes
that men are smarter than women and “nerd culture” are principal factors for low self-confidence in
women. The factors to cause low confidence of female students include low female participation in
the faculty are faculty members beliefs, sexist comments, biased behaviors, and stereotypes related to
women. Many suggestions have been listed to solve gender inequality issue. But the fundamental
solutions are to educate families for future of global world and to clear the biases from educational
materials, social life practices and from the laws. Lastly to emphasize the importance of why women
must be in computer science will decline the inequality in computer science.
Keywords: Gender inequality, computer science, education.

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

  CROSS-CULTURAL CONTROVERSIES: UNRAVELLING ANTI-
FEMINIST DISCOURSE IN THE TRANSLATION OF PIERRE LOTI’S
                   DÉSENCHANTÉES
                                               Pelin TÜRKMEN*

Abstract
Les Désenchantées (1906) offers a dramatic exploration of the modern Ottoman harem, unearthing the
imprisonment of elite Muslim women in Istanbul. The novel dwells on women torn between East and
West clash and the limitations placed on them in the Ottoman Empire and their desire for liberation.
Although orientalist and modernist prose found in Pierre Loti’s works is widely recognized, the
translation journey of this partially autobiographic novel is yet a mystery in research related to the
field. This study analyzes the Turkish text, Mutsuz Kadınlar (1975), translated by Nahid Sırrı Örik,
who was a pasha and a member of the council of state during the reign of Abdülhamid II. The research
aims to uncover anti-feminist ideologies used by the translator within the text due to his field-specific
habitus. The study suggests that despite pro-feminist message of the original French text, the translator
was influenced by political contexts which he was embedded, adopted an anti-feminist and culturally
domesticated approach in Hamidian era., By using the Hallidayan model of discourse and House’s
method of register, the paper explores how these factors shaped the description of the women in modern
harem, emphasizing the symbolic implications of translation decisions. It addresses the socio-cultural
complexities affecting the narrative of Les Désenchantées. The purpose is to critically examine the
translator's role in sustaining prevailing notions of anti-feminism within the Ottoman context.
Paratexts, cultural connotations related to Harem and women characters are taken into consideration
to demonstrate how Harem was depicted in the novel’s Turkish translation (1972), the impact that
history and the political situation of the time exerted on the translator’s decisions.
Keywords: Feminist, ideology, translation studies

                                                                                            English Presentation




* Lecturer, İstanbul Topkapı University, Foreign Languages, Türkiye, ORCID: 0000-0001-5446-444X, email:

pelinturkmen@topkapi.edu.tr


                                                        137
                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                           Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

  PROBLEMS FACED BY ENTREPRENEURIAL WOMEN WHO ARE
          MOTHERS IN THE WORK LIFE PROCESS
                                    1. Perihan CİHANER*, 2. Dilek ÇINAR†

Abstract
The concept of mother is recognized as an emotional and immutable value that emerges with having a
child. Today, being a mother is a very difficult and restrictive responsibility within living conditions
and circumstances. Increasing economic difficulties cause the mother-child relationship to be shaped
or differentiated. For this reason, one of the main problems faced by women or women who are
mothers in their professional lives is the duty of motherhood and the responsibilities arising from this
duty. With the emerging female entrepreneurial population in Turkey, the number of women
entrepreneurs who are both mothers and in business life is increasing. It is clear that one of the main
challenges faced by mother entrepreneurs is the constraining effect of motherhood and its
responsibilities. The first priority for women and mothers is to keep family relationships in balance.
However, in today's conditions, the effort to balance work and family life often encounters serious
problems. The fast pace and risk factors in business life can pose additional challenges for women and
mother entrepreneurs. Gender discrimination against women is one of the main problems that
negatively affect the business performance of women entrepreneurs. Such issues affect women's
business success and hold them back. The process of returning to business life poses a significant
challenge for women entrepreneurs, especially during pregnancy and postpartum periods. Balancing
being a mother and a businesswoman is quite challenging. In addition, the constraints women
entrepreneurs face in accessing financial resources is also an important problem. This can lead to
women being left behind or discriminated against in business life. This study aims to compile and
analyze the findings in the literature by focusing on the challenges faced by women entrepreneurs who
are mothers in business life. It emphasizes the existence of gender discrimination in Turkey and the
difficulties faced by women entrepreneurs in accessing financial resources. In this context, the need to
develop policies and programs to support women entrepreneurs is emphasized. Considering that some
civil society organizations and public institutions support women entrepreneurs, it is emphasized that
this support should be increased. This study aims to contribute to the creation of a fairer business
environment.
Keywords: Women entrepreneurship, gender equality, barriers to entrepreneurship, work life
balance.

    KNOWLEDGE AND ATTITUDE TOWARDS CERVICAL CANCER
    AMONG REPRODUCTIVE AGE GROUP WOMEN IN LAHORE,
                       PAKISTAN
                                           Qaisar Khalid MAHMOOD*

Abstract
Pakistan has a population of 73.8 million women ages 15 and above who are at risk of developing
cervical cancer. Cervical cancer is one of the most commonly diagnosed malignancies among women,
after breast and colorectal cancer. It is estimated that each year, 5008 Pakistani women are diagnosed
with cervical cancer, and 3197 die from the disease. The prevalence of this cancer is increasing with
each passing year. One of the major causes of the high mortality rate due to cervical cancer is a lack of
awareness about the importance of screening and preventive measures. Therefore, there is a need to
assess knowledge and attitudes towards cervical cancer among reproductive-age women in Pakistan.
This study aims to assess the level of knowledge of the symptoms and prognosis of cervical cancer
among reproductive age Pakistani women. For this purpose, the researchers decided to conduct a
quantitative research study. Opting cross sectional survey design, a sample of 500 women were
randomly surveyed from Lahore. The tool of the data collection was self-administered close ended
questionnaire. The data was analyzed using SPSS. Descriptive analysis indicate that majority of the
people were living as nuclear family (66%) and had monthly family income of 100 thousand Pakistani
rupees. Less than half of the respondents recognized warning signs of cervical cancer. Moreover, the
majority of them were not confident enough to notice any symptoms of cervical cancer. They were not
aware of the screening tests needed to diagnose this type of cancer. They also did not know about the
vaccination for cervical cancer. This study recommends that an effective media campaign is needed to
educate the Pakistani people, particularly women, about the signs and risk factors of cervical cancer in
the country.
Keywords: Cervical cancer, closed signs, vaccination, survey, Pakistan.

qaisar.dgs@pu.edu.pk

   A COMPARATIVE ANALYSIS OF THE REPRESENTATION OF
 HEADSCARVED AND NON-HEADSCARVED WOMEN IN TURKISH
                      CINEMA
                                             Rojin Deren ULUTAŞ*

Abstract
The conflicts faced by headscarved women, the hierarchy within religiosity and the contact between
the secular and religious sectors have become more visible in Turkish cinema in the last 10 years. In
this perspective, the aim of this study is to understand the role and representation of headscarved
women and women without headscarves in Turkish cinema. For the research, the Turkish Netflix series
Bir Başkadır (Oya, 2020) and the movie Toz Bezi (Öztürk, 2015) were analysed and the research was
conducted qualitatively through content analysis. The reason for choosing these two films is that both
directors successfully reflect a visual analysis of women's roles on the screen. However, for this
research, going beyond movie analysis, 81 letters written between 17.09.2023-26.01.2024 on the
“Yalnız Yürümeyeceksin” platform, where women who want to remove their headscarves
anonymously describe the process of removing their headscarves, were analysed in order to question
the reality of representations in cinema. In this framework, the harmony, similarities and differences
between what is told in the letters and what is transferred to the cinema in the series Bir Başkadır and
the film Toz Bezi are compared. This research questioned the representation of women in Turkish
cinema through the concepts of “alienation”, “two poles”, “the other” and aimed to understand the
cinematography formed in this context. In addition, it discussed the theme of the contrasts between the
secular and religious sectors and the absolute intersection point that exists. The study also questions
the political atmosphere in Turkey within the framework of discrimination, contrasts and conflicts
within women. This study, which reflects and discusses the reality of production in cinema, can offer
different perspectives and new stories to scenario and screen studies. Comparing the letters of the
Yalnız Yürümeyeceksin platform with the stories reflected in the cinema can lead the scripts to an
independent change and transformation. This study can provide an increased reality practice for
filmmakers who want to understand and narrate women's norms in cinema.
Keywords: Bir Başkadır, Toz Bezi, Turkish Cinema, headscarf representation, two poles, alienation,
Yalnız Yürümeyeceksin Platform.

    OVERCOMING BARRIERS TOWARDS SOCIO-ECONOMIC
  EMPOWERMENT: THE WOMEN MARKET VENDORS IN ILIGAN
                       CITY
                           1. Rose LANGBID-RODA*, 2. Lyra Joy COLLANTES†

Abstract
Market vendors are an integral part of the urban economy. They provide necessary goods and services
where people gather to buy and sell various products. They operate in various settings but for the
purpose of this study, the setting is a traditional market in Iligan City, Philippines. This study looked
into the economic and social empowerment among women market vendors while exploring how
they’ve overcome the barriers towards economic and social empowerment. This is quali-quantitative
research and purposive sampling was employed among respondents for data gathering. A guided
questionnaire was prepared and 50 women market vendors were surveyed. As revealed, these women
vary when it comes to motivation as market vendors. The most common factor is income generation.
Being a market vendor provides them a source of income for their households. Second,
entrepreneurship. Many market vendors are entrepreneurs who want to start their own business.
Becoming a market vendor allows them to be their own boss, make independent decisions, and
potentially build a successful business. Lastly, is socio-economic empowerment. With financial
independence, it enhances their overall well-being and reduces dependency on others. They can support
their family, especially their children to pursue education but most importantly is family engagement.
They showed having more time for family togetherness and have control over their time. To conclude,
despite the economic and social empowerment that these women enjoyed, they too also experienced
barriers such as the difficulty in sustaining their business due to limited access to resources, competition
and their health. External factors that include economic fluctuation and local government policies to
name a few are also considered a challenge to these women.
Keywords: Women market vendors, income generation, barriers, socio-economic empowerment.

    INDONESIAN FEMALE MIGRANT WORKERS AND THEIR
 INVOLVEMENT IN VIOLENT EXTREMISM: THOUGHTS, TRENDS
     AND CHALLENGES FOR EMPOWERING THE WOMEN
                                               Rosita TANDOS*

Abstract
Some studies found the increase number of women involved in violent extremist and terrorist attacks,
even more pathetic by taking children into the actions (Bloom, 2010; IPAC 2017; Sjoberg & Gentry,
2011; Mansour-Ille 2019). Recently, specific group of female migrant workers were targeted by violent
extremist group as potentials of the women to be recruiters, sponsor, propogandist and attackers. This
study explores more this phenomenon emphasizing thoughts, trends and challenges for empowering
the women. The thoughts come from cultural - religious understandings and stories, while trends of
women shifting their roles into recruiters, fighters and bombers. Then, the challenges refer to use
gender analysis in efforts to strengthen women’s positive roles in keeping and maintaining peace. At
this point, Indonesia like other countries have experienced conflicts and terrorist attacks at local and
national levels level. Therefore, the governments focus to implement the policies and program for
conflict resolution, preventing and countering terrorism by spreading the spirit of religious moderation.
The study was applied mixed methods (quantitative and qualitative phases) and had former female
migrant workers (n=100) as participants of the study. Using a short survey, the study aimed to measure
levels of violent extremism and religious moderation of the female participants using close and open-
ended questions, followed by short interview to some participants. The significances of the study are
to enhance the discussions of Islam, women and gender, explore the imperative of inter-religious
understanding and cultural awareness, and recommendations for improving the effectiveness of
policies and programs for empowering women.
Keywords: Islam, gender, violent extremism, religious moderation.

 A FEMININE PERSPECTIVE IN GRAPHIC DESIGN REFLECTED IN
               TYPOGRAPHY: PAULA SCHER
                       1. Rumeysa Zeynep ARAÇLI DURSUN*, 2. Uğur BAKAN†

Abstract
Typography, the backbone of graphic design based on Gutenberg, gained a different perspective from
the modernist trends of the 20th century. As a result of the effects of the Industrial Revolution in the
19th century, artists who approached art and design with a questioning attitude began to search for
more aesthetic, experimental, and innovative designs. This period also brought about significant
developments in terms of graphic design and moved the design direction away from tradition. It has
also changed the course of development in graphic design, causing formal and contextual changes.
Artists also created their works by acting with a more aesthetic way of thinking. Paula Scher, who
emerged as one of these artists in the 90s and was referred to as "the most influential female graphic
designer on the planet" by Ellen Lupton, created a new design perception thanks to her impressive
typographic uses. Scher, who frequently emphasizes and includes the feminine perspective in her
designs, stands out with her colorful, borderless works that include architectural areas. Scher has
managed to attract attention by creating a revolutionary design perception by combining architecture
and graphic design. This unique perspective of Paula Scher re-evaluates the perception of design in
graphic design in an innovative way. The specific purpose of this research is to consider the change in
typography design, which has become a part of graphic design in the modern period due to the changes
experienced. In addition, it aims to carry out a detailed examination by focusing on the innovative
approaches of Paula Scher, who has become one of the pioneers of this field, to evaluate her
experimentally and aesthetically innovative approaches to typography design, and to examine her
contributions to the field. In the conclusion section, By examining Scher's unique typography designs
within the scope of the field, opinions are expressed within the scope of the information obtained in
the research and light of the designer's approaches and impressions.
Keywords: Paula Scher, typography, graphic design, woman, female graphic designer, modern
graphic design.

   THE ROLE AND IMPACT OF WOMEN LIVING UNDER MUSLIM
   LAWS (WLUML) NETWORK IN THE FIGHT AGAINST GENDER
             APARTHEID IN MUSLIM SOCIETIES
                                                Rüveyda ÇINAR*

Abstract
This research delves into the global struggle of the Women Living Under Muslim Laws (WLUML)
network against gender inequality and the gender apartheid imposed by patriarchal structures in the
Islamic world. The study examines WLUML's initiatives to define gender apartheid as a crime under
international law and its international campaigns, particularly addressing women's rights violations in
Afghanistan following the Taliban's return to power. Additionally, it focuses on the "No Violence in
Our Culture" campaign initiated by WLUML, which challenges claims that cultural norms, aligned
with religion, legitimize violence against women. This campaign is regarded as a significant step in
advocating for gender norms and women's rights. The research illustrates how WLUML has brought
about effective changes in legal regulations and societal perceptions through theoretical frameworks
and concrete examples. Furthermore, it will explore the Orthodox Islamic view of women and how
traditional fundamentalist discourses legitimize patriarchal religious legal systems. Texts within
Orthodox Islam, often interpreted by male scholars, support legal and social norms that restrict women
from taking active roles in social, economic, and political life. These interpretations reinforce religious
rulings that limit women's visibility in the public sphere and emphasize their roles within the
household. Moreover, Sharia law practices include legal regulations that grant women fewer rights
than men in matters such as inheritance, divorce, and custody, significantly restricting their
independence. These patriarchal interpretations are not only confined to strict adherence to religious
texts but are also reinforced by societal and cultural norms that hinder women's participation in
education and employment, confining them to domestic roles and positioning them as passive
individuals in society. WLUML's efforts shed light on the role of such interpretations in legitimizing
violence against women and gender discrimination, actively advocating for international campaigns to
challenge and change these structures. WLUML conducts campaigns that promote necessary legal and
social changes to break down these patriarchal structures and recognize women's human rights
worldwide, taking significant strides toward achieving gender equality.
Keywords: Gender equality, WLUML, gender apartheid, women's rights, feminist activism, Islam
and society

                                                                                             Turkish Presentation




* Res. Asst., Selçuk University, Philosophy and Religious Studies, Turkey, ORCID: 0000-0002-4026-2342, email:

ruveydacinar@selcuk.edu.tr

                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV

        THE ADVENTURE OF WOMEN’S RIGHT TO SURNAME IN
             TURKEY: THIS IS NOT A HAPPY ENDING YET
                                                   Seda ÖZKAN*

Abstract
The purpose of this paper is to evaluate the annulment of Article 187 of the Turkish Civil Code, which
regulates the surname of women, by the decision of the Constitutional Court dated 22/02/2023. For
this purpose, the paper will comparatively address the jurisprudence of the European Court of Human
Rights (ECtHR) and the Constitutional Court regarding woman’s right to surname, and the attitude of
the legislature towards the annulment decision of the Constitutional Court will be discussed. Until the
annulment decision of the Constitutional Court dated 22/02/2023, important judicial steps were passed
regarding the woman’s right to surname. The ECtHR, in decision of Ünal Tekeli v. Türkiye, ruled that
the woman’s right to surname was violated, and this decision became a turning point. Because this
decision is the first decision stating that the violation of rights arising from Article 187 of the Law
violates the European Convention on Human Rights (ECHR). In this decision, the ECtHR reveals that
the article regulating the surname of the woman violates the right to respect for private life guaranteed
by Article 8 of the ECHR and, accordingly, the prohibition of discrimination under Article 14 of the
ECHR. This decision has also been a guide in terms of constitutional complaints brought before the
Constitutional Court as of 23/09/2012. Finally, the decision of the Constitutional Court dated
22/02/2023 obliged the legislature to amend this article. As a result, the violation of the women’s right
to surname has not yet been eliminated. Because, although the annulment of Article 187 of the Law
has eliminated the source of the violation, the fact that there is no regulation yet despite the nine-month
period has expired, creating a new form of violation against the women’s right to surname by the
legislature. In line with the ECHR, the jurisprudence of the ECHtR, the International Covenant on Civil
and Political Rights and the Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination Against
Women (CEDAW), the immediate and complete consideration of the Law articles concerning women's
surname rights by the legislature will ensure that the chronic violation is put to an end.
Keywords: Turkish Civil Code, women’s right to surname, prohibition of discrimination, right to
respect for private life, CEDAW.

* Dr., Trakya University, Political Science and Public Administration, Türkiye, ORCID: 0000-0002-7157-7881, email:

sedaozkan@trakya.edu.tr

       GENDER POLITICS AND SOCIAL MEDIA: DEBATE ON THE
              ISTANBUL CONVENTION ON TWITTER
                                            Selime BÜYÜKGÖZE*

Abstract
Twitter (X) and other social media platforms hold significant importance in public debates, becoming
crucial spaces for monitoring political discussions. These platforms also facilitate discussions on
gender relations, providing an opportunity for raising awareness about gender discrimination and
allowing women to speak out about the gender-based violence they face. The interactive nature of new
media, which allows for anonymity, goes beyond physical gatherings and creates potential for sharing
ideas. Hashtag campaigns such as #MeToo, where women share their experiences of sexual
harassment, exemplify how these platforms enable experience-sharing and politicization of women's
experiences in the public sphere. The suitability of social media platforms for public debates and raising
awareness is not only advantageous for feminists and advocates of gender equality but also presents an
opportunity for opponents of equality. Especially in recent years, anti-gender movements with
significant visibility have started to effectively use these platforms to organize their thoughts and
influence public opinion. The coexistence of diverse and conflicting views on these platforms provides
an environment for analyzing discourses and sometimes contentious discussions. This study will
examine the social media campaigns organized on Twitter in Turkey in 2020 by anti-gender political
groups advocating for Turkey's withdrawal from the Istanbul Convention, and analyze the debates
conducted under highly interactive posts. Through the analysis of discourses expressing different views
on the Istanbul Convention and gender, the role of these platforms in Turkey's gender politics will be
discussed.
Keywords: Anti-gender movements, social media platforms, Twitter, Istanbul Convention

                                                                                           Turkish Presentation




* Asst. Prof., Istanbul Topkapı University, New Media and Communication, Turkey, ORCID: 0000-0003-0044-8377, email:

selimebuyukgoze@topkapi.edu.tr

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV

         THE IMPORTANCE OF GENITAL TRACT INFECTIONS IN
           WOMEN'S HEALTH AND THE ROLE OF MIDWIVES
                                    1. Selin KOÇ*, 2. Ayşegül DÖNMEZ†

Abstract
To emphasize the importance of genital tract infections on women's health and the role of midwives in
this issue. Genital tract infections and Sexually Transmitted Infections (STIs) are a common health
problem worldwide and pose significant health risks, especially in low- and middle-income countries.
This review was conducted to understand the importance and impact of these infections, to emphasize
the importance of early diagnosis and treatment, and to highlight the role of midwives in women's
health. These infections particularly affect women and babies during pregnancy and can lead to adverse
health outcomes. Early detection and treatment through community-based screening tests is vital.
However, many women do not have access to these services or have limited access. The aim of this
review is to understand how genital infections affect women's quality of life and to develop effective
strategies to prevent and manage these infections. It also aims to identify the role of midwives in this
issue and to increase women's awareness of genital health. This review is based on scientific articles
and resources obtained through a literature review. Studies on the importance, effects and prevention
of genital infections were examined and compiled. We also reviewed the literature on the role of
midwives in assessing and treating women's genital health and assessed the contribution that can be
made in this regard. In resource-poor settings, weak health systems and limited transportation
contribute to the increased prevalence and severity of genital infections. The availability of high quality
diagnostic tests and recent advances in recent years offer promising solutions. The shift from
syndromic approaches to more specific and effective diagnostic methods is contributing significantly
to the well-being of individuals. Preventing mother-to-child transmission of infections is important for
maternal and child health. The adoption of these developments in national and international guidelines
and clinical practice can contribute to the overall improvement of women's reproductive health. Genital
tract infections are an important problem that negatively affects women's health and quality of life.
Early detection, treatment and prevention of these infections are vital to protect women's health.
Midwives play an important role in the detection, treatment and prevention of genital infections. By
raising women's awareness about genital health and facilitating their access to health services,
midwives can improve public health and enhance women's quality of life. This requires collaboration
between the global health community, governments and civil society organizations to ensure effective
resource allocation and implementation.
Keywords: Genital Tract Infections, Sexually Transmitted Infections, Midwifery.

           EVIDENCE-BASED PRACTICES IN FAMILY PLANNING
                                       1. Selin KOÇ*, 2. Çiler YEYĞEL†

Abstract
Family planning aims to contribute to the creation of healthy societies by providing individuals with
the freedom to choose the time, number and spacing of their childbearing. It emphasizes the key role
of evidence-based health practices in strengthening the sexual and reproductive health of individuals,
preventing unwanted pregnancies and improving public health. Evidence-based practices in family
planning methods provide a comprehensive assessment of the effectiveness, safety and efficacy of
family planning methods. The aim of this review is to highlight the importance and impact of an
evidence-based approach in family planning practices. It aims to demonstrate the importance of
evidence-based developments in family planning practice for health professionals, policy makers and
the general public. This review is based on a review of clinical guidelines, evidence-based standards
of care and approaches to improve the effectiveness of family planning services. It also provides an in-
depth assessment using information from reliable sources such as data from the United Nations
Population Fund (UNFPA) and reports from other health organizations. Emphasis is placed on the key
roles of the evidence-based approach in family planning practices, such as the role of health
professionals, effective communication with individuals and guidance on appropriate method
selection. Evidence-based family planning practices make important contributions to preventing
unwanted pregnancies, regulating birth intervals and building healthy communities. Health
professionals need to provide accurate counseling based on up-to-date scientific evidence and provide
services that are appropriate to the needs of individuals. This approach is an important step towards
promoting gender equality and improving the well-being of women, children and communities.
However, effective family planning practices need to be implemented and more research needs to be
conducted. Furthermore, the use of evidence-based approaches in family planning practices will ensure
equitable access to health services in the community. In this context, this review was created by
examining clinical guidelines, evidence-based care standards and approaches in this field in order to
increase the effectiveness of family planning practices.
Keywords: Family Planning, Evidence, Contraceptive Methods.

                                                         148
                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

           EXPERIENCE OF BEING A WOMEN TRADESMEN IN A
          NEIGHBORHOOD: BURSA ATICILAR NEİGHBORHOOD
                                                Selin ÖZDEMİR*

Abstract
Women's involvement in working life as active and productive labor is increasing despite many
obstacles. In Turkey, although women, women's labor or women's involvement in business life has
increased and gained visibility, it is not at the expected levels. When we look at TÜİK data, according
to the results of the Household Labor Force Survey, it is seen that the labor force participation rate of
the population aged 15 and over in 2022 is 53.1%, this rate is 35.1% for women and 71.4% for men.
The share of women entrepreneurs in the current proportional distribution is quite large. Women
entrepreneurs have achieved economic independence by establishing their own businesses and have
also contributed to society economically and socially. Studies on women entrepreneurship in the
literature define women entrepreneurs as women who establish partnerships, are in contact with private
and public institutions related to their business, plan for the future of their business, have a say in the
area of investment and use of their earnings, and can take risks. focuses on the problems they face.
This study aims to focus on women entrepreneurs and their experiences specifically in a neighborhood,
rather than making general definitions and explanations about women entrepreneurs. Atıcılar District
of Osmangazi district of Bursa constitutes the research area of this study. In the study conducted with
sociological method, qualitative research method was used. Interviews were conducted with a total of
10 female participants working as tradesmen in Atıcılar neighborhood through semi-structured
interview technique. The data evaluated through descriptive analysis provided important findings about
the personal experiences and experiences of women entrepreneurs regarding the processes of deciding
on a workplace, opening a business and doing business. Among the remarkable findings, it was
revealed that female entrepreneurs complain about their fellow women and tend to postpone their
responsibilities because they cannot receive support in housework and child care.
Keywords: Women's entrepreneurship, gender, neighborhood culture.

         FEMALE POVERTY AND ITS EFFECTS ON PREGNANCY
                        1. Sevilay AYDIN*, 2. Melike DİŞSİZ†, 3. Dilek BİNGÖL‡

Abstract
Poverty refers to the lack of income and physical opportunities, as well as social deprivation, injustice
in resource distribution and powerlessness. Women experience poverty much more than men. For this
reason, the concept of women's poverty emerged. Women struggling with poverty experience serious
health problems due to many negative reasons such as lack of access to health services, malnutrition,
and low education level. This study aimed to examine the impact of poverty on the health of pregnant
women. Poverty, especially women's poverty, remains on the agenda and constitutes a serious problem
that needs to be overcome all over the world. Studies have shown that poverty negatively affects
women's health. In this study, studies examining the effects of poverty on pregnancy were evaluated.
It has been determined that malnutrition due to poverty before and during pregnancy, and not getting
enough and quality food, causes problems such as anemia, preeclampsia in the mother, and intrauterine
growth retardation, low birth weight, and premature birth in the baby. In addition, lack of access to
health services, inadequate immunization, giving birth under unhealthy conditions, and high levels of
smoking and alcohol use are among the other factors that negatively affect the health of the mother and
the fetus. In addition, it has been determined that the risk of perinatal depression is higher in poor
women due to factors such as stress, low education level, unemployment, lack of social support and
domestic violence. Maternal and fetal risks are higher in poor women than in other groups due to
reasons such as low socioeconomic level, malnutrition, late or inability to access prenatal care services,
birth not taking place under healthy conditions, smoking and alcohol use, emotional stress, domestic
violence, discrimination and exclusion. is emerging. It should be aimed to reduce maternal and infant
deaths by diagnosing pregnancy at an early stage, providing adequate antenatal care, detecting risks
early, ensuring adequate nutrition, preventing anemia with immunization, folic acid support, iron
supplementation, evaluating mental health and providing the necessary psychological support. Nurses
should provide training on these issues to poor pregnant women, direct women in need of social support
to authorized institutions, and ensure that their access to health services increases.
Keywords: Poverty, pregnancy, nursing.

                                                                                            Turkish Presentation

 WOMEN'S RIGHTS IN AZERBAIJAN IN TERMS OF TRADITIONAL
                       VALUES
                                               Shahla NURUZADE*

Abstract
In the article, the issue of women's rights in the traditional value system of Azerbaijanis, the place of
the image of a woman/mother in the worldview and culture of the people is considered to be
investigated. To study the issue of women's rights in the historical process in Azerbaijan, first of all, it
is necessary to analyze the attitude towards women in the culture of the Turkic peoples, the position of
women/mothers in the family and society. In Azerbaijan, the attitude towards women/mothers, their
rights and responsibilities in the family and society is connected, first of all, with historical tradition.
With the spread of Islam in Azerbaijan, traditional values also influenced Islamic values. Turkish and
Islamic factors are the basis of family values in Azerbaijan. In the Middle Ages, Khatiba Hanim,
Mehrijan Hanim, Sara Khatun, Mahinbanu Sultan, Pari, Heiransa Beyim, the legendary Tuti Bika, the
khan's daughter Natevan and others were figures who played an important role in the history of
Azerbaijan. Historical processes have also had an impact on women's rights. The Declaration of
Independence of the Azerbaijan Democratic Republic, declared on May 28, 1918, declared human
rights and the equal rights of men and women. Thus, the right of "every person to have all rights and
freedoms" included in the "Universal Declaration of Human Rights" declared by the United Nations in
1948 was declared in Azerbaijan in 1918. In the modern world, many conventions on human rights and
women's rights have been signed, international and state laws have been adopted. However, in our
time, women's rights in the world are still violated. In modern times, it is observed that traditional
traditions and family values are weakening, and people are moving away from traditional family
relationships. Therefore, in order to protect women's rights, as well as human rights in general, it is
important to apply international and national laws along with customary law. At the same time, it is
important that the laws regulating the protection of human rights do not harm the traditional values of
Azerbaijanis. It is intended to examine these problems from a historical-ethnological point of view, the
attitude towards women in modern Azerbaijani society and the analysis of the documents adopted at
the state level.
Keywords: Azerbaijan, Traditional Values, Women's Rights, International Conventions.

                                                                                              Turkish Presentation

sh_nuruzade@yahoo.com

   WOMEN AS HARBINGER OF PEACE IN CONFLICT AND CRISIS
                                               Shamenaz BANO*

Abstract
Since the evolution of this world, the role of women is very vital in any society as they constitute half
of the world population. Though since centuries they have been subjugated by their male counterparts
and have borne different kinds of injustices but now things have changed drastically. In the
contemporary scenario they are playing a crucial part as they are competing with the men in almost all
the fields and even are excelling them. We have also witnessed that women have established themselves
as an harbinger of peace whenever any critical situation has arised. Whenever any war has broken it is
women who have tried to balance the duties inside and outside of their homes. The most important
aspect is that women can maintain patience even in the most turbulent situations which is why in some
cases they are better than their male counterparts. The fact is that women are gifted with extraordinary
patience which keeps them calm amidst all kinds of chaos. Women, the world over have proved that
they can be the voice and power to be the agents of peace in any situation whether it is war between
two countries or any internal conflict. When the whole world was gripped with a corona pandemic at
that time, mostly all the countries which had women as the head of the state had performed better and
in a more sensible way. In my paper, I will be highlighting the efforts of some of those women who
have sacrificed their lives for humanity and have become an epitome of peace for the generations.
Keywords: Harbinger of peace, conflict, crisis, role of women.

  WOMEN'S AUTONOMY IN REPRODUCTIVE HEALTH SERVICES
        DECISION MAKING: A LITERATURE REVIEW
                                      1. Sidar GÜL*, 2. Ayşegül KILIÇLI†

Abstract
Women's autonomy is necessary for making decisions in various healthcare situations, including
obtaining information, seeking care, and choosing between treatment options in reproductive health.
In this study, a literature review was conducted to define the relationship between women's autonomy
and decision-making processes regarding reproductive health services and to determine the factors
affecting decision-making processes regarding reproductive health services. In the review conducted
using various databases (Pubmed, Scopus, and ScienceDirect), the inclusion criteria were 1)
publication in English; 2) original article; 3) publication in the last five years; 4) research on women's
autonomy to make decisions about health and utilization of health services. It was found that some
traditional measures of women's status can play an essential role in improving reproductive health-
seeking behaviors. These measures include women's age, education level, household income,
employment status, and place of residence. Women with these status indicators are more likely to seek
reproductive health services and take responsibility for their reproductive health. Having children is
positively associated with women's autonomy in reproductive health decision-making. Tend to be more
autonomous in reproductive health decision-making. Women exposed to media tend to have more
information and understanding about reproductive health, enabling them to make informed decisions.
The study also found that harmful social norms and cultural practices often limit women's freedom to
make decisions about the use of reproductive health services. These social pressures can be so intense
that women are unable to seek the help they need to protect their reproductive health. In many cases,
women are forced to compromise their health and well-being due to these cultural pressures, which
can have serious long-term consequences. Although women's autonomy is essential for reproductive
health services, there are challenges in conceptualizing and measuring women's autonomy, as the
construct is multidimensional and different researchers focus on different dimensions. Findings suggest
that empowering women and increasing their decision-making abilities can increase their participation
in reproductive health services. By considering the different dimensions of women's autonomy,
policymakers and healthcare providers can develop effective strategies to support women's
reproductive health and well-being.
Keywords: Women's autonomy, decision-making, reproductive health.

    MODEL PROGRAMS TO PROMOTE DIVERSITY IN THE STEM
                       PIPELINE
                                            Sıddıka Semahat DEMİR*

Abstract
STEM (Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics) education and workforce are important
to the innovation and competitiveness of a country. A country’s sustained economic growth can be
created by the STEM workforce who develop, manage, and maintain the processes and products of
innovation. The talent capacity for the STEM workforce must be diversified through a sustained
pipeline of girls and women from K-12 thru university and graduate studies. Science Heroes
Association has programs to attract students to STEM education and to raise awareness on STEM
careers. The association implements several national and international STEM projects and programs
and provides opportunities to disadvantaged and underrepresented groups such as public schools, rural
areas, girls, and immigrants to participate. The association has been running robotics, coding and
research team tournaments for over 20 years. The girls participation has been 33% in these
tournaments. “Science will win because of the youth” program aims to connect high school students
with scientists in Türkiye through workshops to raise awareness and curiosity in science and scientific
processes, to run experiments with simple daily household materials, to test hypotheses and to provide
inspiring role models in STEM careers. The findings demonstrated that we hosted 1072 students from
233 schools in 47 cities at 52 different workshops. %59 of the participants were girls. The survey
feedback received from these girls reaffirmed that they found this program very informative in different
emerging science and research topics. The program also allows the girls confirm which majors they
want to study at the university and their career decisions in STEM. The Newton Mobile Room and
Newton Flight Academy Istanbul are two new programs to increase participation of girls in aviation.
2155 students participated in these educational programs and experienced aviation through unique
flight simulators. %40 of the participants were girls in Newton Mobile Room whereas the girls’
participation at Newton Flight Academy Istanbul was 45%. The association continues developing and
running programs that focus on diversity, equity and inclusion since a diversified STEM talent with
increasing participation of women can refuel a country’s economic growth, the base of its prosperity
and security.
Keywords: Women in Science Technology Engineering and Mathematics (STEM), innovation,
competitiveness, economic growth, prosperity, STEM talent and capacity, diversity equity and
inclusion (DEI).

0000-0003-2086-9953, email: semahatdemir@topkapi.edu.tr

THE EFFECT OF HEALTH LITERACY ON THE FEAR OF BIRTH OF
     WOMEN WITH POLYCYSTIC OVARIAN SYNDROME
       1. Simge ÖZTÜRK*, 2. Beyza Nur YILDIZ†, 3. Deniz DAŞLI‡, 4. Nazife GÜNDOĞAR§

Abstract
The aim of this study is to determine the effect of health literacy on fear of childbirth in women with
polycystic ovary syndrome. This cross-sectional and correlation-seeking study was conducted with
n=147 women with polycystic ovary syndrome between June 2023 and March 2024. The data of the
research was collected via social media with an online questionnaire prepared via Google Form.
Personal Information Form, Turkey Health Literacy Scale (TSOY-32), Wijma Birth
Expectation/Experience Scale-A Version (W-DEQ-A) were used to collect data. It was determined that
25.2% of the women participating in the study had been diagnosed with polycystic ovary syndrome
(PCOS) for 2-3 years, and 29.3% went to the hospital every 6 months due to PCOS. The most common
symptoms experienced by women due to PCOS are weight gain in the last 6 months (78.8%), menstrual
irregularity (72.8%), hair loss (65.3%), hair growth (63.9%), hormone irregularity (57.1%) and
depression (43.5%). was found to be insulin resistance (31.3%). In the multiple regression analysis
conducted in the study, it was determined that there was a significant relationship between the TSOY-
32 scale and hormone irregularity (β-coefficient = 0.208; p = 0.011) and hair loss (β-coefficient = -
0.172; p = 0.035). In the study, W-DEQ-A scale was used to evaluate depression (β-coefficient= 0.232;
p=0.012), presence of birth anxiety (β-coefficient= 0.185; p=0.041), cause of birth anxiety (β-
coefficient= -0.239; p=0.009). It was determined that there was a significant relationship between ) and
explained 15.7% of the total variance. In this study, it was determined that the health literacy level of
women with polycystic ovary syndrome varies according to hormone irregularity and hair loss. It has
been determined that women's birth expectations vary depending on depression, presence of birth
anxiety, and the reason for birth anxiety. Health professionals should evaluate the health literacy and
fear of birth of women with PCOS and provide counseling to women.
Keywords: Polycystic ovary syndrome, health literacy, fear of birth, birth expectation, birth
experience.

     WAR IN GAZA AND ITS IMPACT ON WOMEN AND CHILDREN
                                                  Suat DÖNMEZ*

Abstract
Following an attack by Hamas, Israel launched a massive military operation in Gaza, which is under
the control of the Palestinian authority Hamas. However, this military operation has had a devastating
impact on the humanitarian situation in Gaza, particularly affecting the daily lives of women and
children. Independent human rights experts have expressed concern over the ongoing conflict between
Israel and Palestine and its impact on women and girls. As of 29 April 2024, of 34,488 Palestinians
killed in Gaza, 14,500 have been children and 9,500 women. Another 77,643 have reportedly been
injured, of which 75% are estimated to be female. The international community have called for Israel
to cease immediately all hostilities and for all states to stop exporting weapons. The experts emphasized
that international humanitarian law and human rights law provide special protection to women and
children. As with all wars, this incident once again demonstrated to us that women and children are
among the most affected by armed conflict all around the world. This study aims to investigate the
impact of military campaigns on women and children in Gaza.
Keywords: War in Gaza, Women and war, Children and war, Civilians and war.

                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

THE ROLE OF MIDWIVES IN HEALTH ISSUES ENCOUNTERED BY
          WOMEN ACROSS THE STAGES OF LIFE
                               1. Suna ARAS ÇELİK*, 2. Ayşegül DÖNMEZ†

Abstract
Women's health encompasses the holistic well-being of women, including their physical, social, and
psychological states. It entails not only their physical health but also their social relationships,
emotional, and mental well-being. Women's health is a lifelong process that begins in the preconception
period and continues throughout a woman's life. This underscores that women's health is influenced
and shaped by various factors throughout life, emphasizing the importance of ongoing care and support
for women's health at every stage. The literature indicates that women's health exhibits distinct
characteristics compared to other segments of society. Due to its significant impact on public health,
women's health holds particular importance. Biological factors affecting women's health, especially
periods such as pregnancy, growth, and development, highlight the times when women face health
risks and increased needs for health services. This underscores the necessity for meticulous health
services to protect and improve women's health. In the reproductive age group, commonly known as
15-49 years, pregnancy, childbirth, and the postpartum period require specialized care, making these
periods crucial and directly impacting a woman's health. Women's health is also considered crucial for
child and family health. Due to women's gender roles, they often take on the responsibility of being a
spouse and/or mother, further emphasizing the significance of women's health for family health. Family
health encompasses the well-being of all family members, ensuring they are all healthy and thriving.
Therefore, any deterioration in the health of any family member, especially the mother, can adversely
affect family dynamics. This article, based on literature, aims to share information regarding the role
of midwives in addressing health issues encountered during different stages of women's lives.
Keywords: Women, Life, Health, Midwifery.

                        International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                           Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

    EVIDENCE-BASED PRACTICES IN HIGH-RISK PREGNANCIES
                                  1. Suna ARAS ÇELİK*, 2. Çiler YEYĞEL†

Abstract
The aim is to raise awareness about the importance of evidence-based practices focused on preserving
and enhancing fetal health in high-risk pregnancies, with the goal of increasing awareness in this field.
High-risk pregnancies denote situations where potential risks exist for the mother and/or baby,
necessitating special attention and care during the prenatal period. In this context, preventing or
minimizing complications in high-risk pregnancies is vital for a healthy pregnancy and birth.
Therefore, the adoption and implementation of evidence-based approaches in managing high-risk
pregnancies are encouraged to promote positive outcomes for both the mother and baby. Increasing the
use of evidence-based practices for preserving maternal and fetal health in high-risk pregnancies and
raising the knowledge level among healthcare professionals, policymakers, and the general public in
this field are important. Among healthcare professionals, enhancing knowledge and awareness, as well
as wider adoption and implementation of existing guidelines and protocols, are necessary. Additionally,
community awareness campaigns should be conducted to recognize high-risk pregnancies and facilitate
early intervention. Moreover, it's crucial for policymakers to strengthen health policies related to high-
risk pregnancies and allocate more resources in this area. The World Health Organization emphasizes
in its antenatal care guidelines that an effective strategy for reducing pregnancy and childbirth-related
deaths is to promote evidence-based practices used throughout the preconceptional, antenatal,
intrapartum, and postpartum periods. WHO suggests the continuous care model led by midwives,
which involves considering the woman as a whole and providing continuous monitoring from the
preconception period. In Turkey, the Ministry of Health and relevant health institutions implement
various health policies and programs aimed at preventing, early diagnosis, and effectively managing
high-risk pregnancies. Strategies such as pre-pregnancy health services, regular prenatal check-ups,
educational programs, and emergency interventions are employed within this framework. Evidence
suggests that the use of evidence-based practices in high-risk pregnancies positively impacts maternal
and fetal health and reduces complications. Therefore, widespread adoption and implementation of
these practices can contribute to improving pregnancy outcomes and reducing health inequalities. It is
recommended that healthcare professionals and policymakers follow evidence-based practices in
managing high-risk pregnancies. In this regard, conducting further research and supporting healthcare
professionals through education are crucial for the implementation of evidence-based interventions in
high-risk pregnancies.
Keywords: Pregnancy, high-risk pregnancy, evidence-based practices.

     ANATOMY OF FEMICIDE IN TURKEY: A SPATIAL STUDY ON
                     SOCIAL FACTORS
                                                    Suna TATLI*

Abstract
Femicide is increasing every year in Turkey and is one of the most pressing social problems that need
to be addressed. In terms of social aspects, the social acceptance of gender inequality and men's
perspectives on women's social position contribute to the normalization of femicide. Regarding
government policies, situations like the lack of deterrent punishments and unacceptable disparities
between the incidents and the subsequent punishments, which provoke outrage in society, play a role
in fostering a climate conducive to femicide. Additionally, Turkey's withdrawal from the Istanbul
Convention adds to the concerns, emphasizing the need for addressing the issue with comprehensive
measures. In this study, femicide in Turkey between 2009 and 2023 will be examined in terms of the
socio-cultural and socio-economic variables that triggered it. Since official statistical institutions do
not keep records of statistics on femicides in Turkey, the data used in this study has been compiled
from the Memorial Counter which is an updated daily record that has been documenting femicides in
Turkey since 2008. This compiled data consists of information on who was killed, in which city the
incident took place, the method of killing, and the motive for the killing. In addition to these data on
femicides, socio-cultural and socio-economic variables such as education, economic situation, and
variables related to the development levels of cities compiled from TÜİK will also be included in the
analysis. In this study, spatial panel data models, one of the quantitative econometric techniques, will
be used for data analysis and it will be examined whether the spread of the social variables discussed
is related to femicides on a city basis. In addition, whether there is an interaction effect between
provinces in the distribution of femicides will be reported through spatial maps. The study in question
is unique in terms of its subject, technique and scope, and it is aimed not only to contribute to the
literature but also to guide the policies to be carried out on the solution of a leading social problem.
Keywords: Femicide, gender inequality, spatial econometrics, panel data models.

9894-934X, suna.tatli@mgu.edu.tr

     DETERMINATION OF THE VULNERABILITY OF WOMEN TO
               CLIMATE CHANGE IN TURKEY
                                     1. Süleyman TOY*, 2. Zeynep EREN†

Abstract
Impacts of climate hazards and risks differ depending on geographic regions, economic sectors and
communities. It is indicated in related literature especially those prepared by international
organizations that women are highly vulnerable to the hazards and risks from climate change. It is also
emphasized that the participation of women in the development and implementation of climate change
mitigation and adaptation actions will result in higher benefits. Methods proposed for the vulnerability
levels of women to climate hazards and risks include both the general framework presented in the
IPCC's AR4, 5 and 6 reports and qualitative studies based on field data and observations. There is still
a need to propose new improved methods and data sets for involving more comprehensive parameters
related to the vulnerability levels of women to climate change, particularly under specific factor groups.
In the framework of impact chain assessments described in IPCC reports, it is necessary to weigh and
determine the parameters using concrete indicators. Therefore, the aim of this study is to propose
parameters and data related to the analysis of women's vulnerability in Turkey using the methodology
developed to analyse the vulnerability of various sectors and themes to climate change. In addition to
the data set followed by TÜİK (Turkish Statistical Institute) under the title "Statistics on Women", data
categories that can be used by utilizing fundamental economic, social, and health indicators have also
been incorporated into the study methodology. For this purpose, a methodology including the impact
chain analysis was preferred within the scope of climate change for the most comprehensive
determination of parameter classes. A data set is described to contribute to modelling studies that
provide concrete and comparative insights into women's vulnerability to climate change.
Keywords: Climate change, women, impact chain, vulnerability, climate hazards.
