                      International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                         Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

  WOMEN'S POLITICAL REPRESENTATION AND GENDER QUOTA
                      IN TURKEY
                              1. Şeyma ÇORBACI*, 2. Muhammed BABAR†

Abstract
Women's political representation means women's increased participation in political decision-making
processes and governance. Effective representation of women in politics is important for the healthy
functioning of democracy and the realization of gender equality. Although women constitute half of
the society, they are less represented than men in political and decision-making positions. Women's
political representation contributes to more inclusive and fair policies by ensuring that their needs,
perspectives and experiences are reflected in decision-making processes. Increasing women's political
representation is a sine qua non of a democratic society and steps taken in this regard play an important
role in achieving gender equality. Increased participation of women in politics contributes not only to
the strengthening of women's rights, but also to the overall well-being of society and the healthy
functioning of democratic processes. Therefore, women's political representation is an important issue
in the overall interests of both women and society. However, gender norms in politics today lead to
politics being seen as a masculine sphere and women's representation being lower than men's
representation. In Turkey, women's participation in politics was legalized by the Turkish Grand
National Assembly in 1934 with the encouragement of Mustafa Kemal Atatürk. This egalitarian
political step pioneered and set an example for many states. Nevertheless, women's political
representation in our country has not reached the desired level. Although the role of women in society
is one of the main reasons for the lack of adequate representation of women, one of the most important
reasons is that political parties give more space to male candidates in candidate selection processes. In
this case, the candidate selection processes and policies of political parties gain importance in women's
political representation. The fact that parties pursue policies to give more space to women candidates
while nominating candidates leads to the emergence of practices such as gender quotas. Although
examples of gender quotas vary, they generally aim to nominate women candidates above a certain
percentage in candidate nomination processes. In this study, examples of gender quota practices in
Turkish political parties will be analyzed and their effects on women's political representation will be
evaluated within the framework of gender equality.
Keywords: Political Representation of Women, Political Parties, Gender Quota, Gender Equality.

                       International Congress on Women in A Global World IV
                          Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

 THE VALUE OF PRENATAL EDUCATION IN PREPARING FUTURE
                      MOTHERS
                              1. Şükriye ADIGÜZEL*, 2. Gülseren DAĞLAR†

Abstract
A woman's life undergoes profound physical, psychological, and social changes as a result of
pregnancy, which is a developmental crisis. Birth can be defined as an event that changes a woman's
life. Birth can refer to both the physical arrival of a child and the process of "becoming something new:
becoming a mother". Every element of a parent's life is altered by having a kid. Their external and
internal environments shift, and these shifts are permanent. To meet the demands of the child, the
mother transforms into someone else, leaving lasting wounds in her wake. Throughout history,
pregnant women have turned to more seasoned peers for assistance and have followed customs or
practices. To adjust to the changes that come with pregnancy and delivery, women must be given more
control. Only if she receives help from attending prenatal education sessions will this be possible.
Pregnancy preparedness is ensured and women's physical and psychological preparation for the role of
parenting is greatly aided by their engagement in prenatal education and birthing decision-making.
Women who are expecting frequently feel dread, anxiety, and concern about becoming pregnant and
giving birth. This worry and anxiety intensifies, particularly if they have no prior knowledge or
experience of birthing. These days, women are taught how to regulate the birthing process, manage the
changes that occur throughout pregnancy and labor, and have a special birth experience with their
partner. They are also taught how to manage labor pain using non-pharmacological approaches. By
learning about pregnancy, childbirth, newborn care, breastfeeding, and postnatal care, women can
protect their own health as well as the health of their unborn child. Prenatal education can also help
women feel more confident and less anxious as they begin the process of preparing for motherhood.
Therefore, the goal of assisting the expecting mother in feeling superior during her pregnancy, birth,
and postpartum period can be achieved. Achieving this aim involves receiving high-quality care during
pregnancy with the assistance of a midwife. To sum up, the purpose of this review was to highlight
how crucial prenatal education is in helping women get ready for parenting.
Keywords: Pregnancy, prenatal education, birth, motherhood.

         JOHN STUART MILL’S DEFENSE OF WOMEN’S RIGHTS
                                                 Tuğba TORUN*

Abstract
Although one could begin identifying violation of women’s rights as a sociological problem as
something that is started being classified as such with industrial revolution, which brought women’s
participation in labour force, the fact that there was a growing consciousness regarding these violations
and that subject was considered and discussed as a problem could be traced back to enlightenment. The
important point here is that women's rights are actually among human rights; It is about the fact that
every situation where women's rights are not protected is actually one of the obstacles to the survival
of human rights. England is one of the countries where this global problem was first mentioned,
solutions were sought, and the problem was discussed from cultural, scientific and philosophical
perspectives. At this point, British philosopher Mary Wollstonecraft's work titled Vindication of the
Rights of Women is important as it is the first work of feminist thought. The advocation of women’s
rights, which started in England, moved to legal ground with John Stuart Mill, who was also a member
of the same geography. Mill was a philosopher with a background in law that defended women as
individuals free and entitled to equal rights with men on the basis of political and legal grounds as a
natural human right which opposed the existing conceptions and practices of his time towards women.
The place of women in socio-cultural life, which is tried to be revealed based on England, one of the
leading countries that shape the modern world, and John Stuart Mill, and the resulting deprivation of
their rights, shows us that the deprivation of women's rights is not limited to time and geography, but
is a historical problem. In order to draw attention to the fact that the neglect and violation of women's
rights is a human rights violation and, in this sense, a historical and global problem, and the mistake of
associating the violation and neglect of women's rights primarily and mostly with religion, the
conditions and practices in England are also taken into account within the limits of this notification.
John Stuart Mill's thoughts on women's rights will be included.
Keywords: England, John Stuart Mill, women, rights.

tugbatorun@duzce.edu.tr.

  DETERMINATION OF UNIVERSITY STUDENTS' INTERNALIZED
   MISOGYNY AND ATTITUDES TOWARDS VIOLENCE AGAINST
                       WOMEN
                                   1. Tuğçe AKGÜL*, 2. Gülşen VURAL†

Abstract
Misogyny today causes women to be excluded economically, politically and socially, and to be
restricted in terms of identity and personality. The aim of this study is to determine university students'
internalized misogyny and violence against women attitudes and to examine the relationship between
misogyny and violence against women attitudes. This study is a descriptive and correlational study.
The universe of study consists of the students who have not taken previously the lessons compulsory
on gender, status of women and all kinds of discrimination against women. The sample of study
consisted of a total of 472 students who were in the 1st, 4th and 5th class of the Faculties of Dentistry,
Pharmacy and Atatürk Education Faculty in a private üniversity. Three different measurement tools
were used for data collection: A questionnaire developed by the researchers, Internalized Misogyny
Scale, ISKEBE Behaviour Scale. As a result of study, statistically significant and strong positive
correlation were found between the subgroups of Internalized Misogyny Scales’s devaluating women,
distrust in women, and valuating men more. Statistically significant but weak, negative relationship
were found between devaluating and distrusting the women, valuing men more and violence categories
of the scale (psychological and economic violence, ISKEBE Behaviour Scale). Positive and strong
relationship were found between sexual and physical violence and psychological and economic
violence and ISKEBE Behaviour Scale. Since this research is limited only to university students, it is
thought that it is important to conduct similer studies with individuals of different age ranges.
Keywords: Misogyny, Violence against women, Attitude, Iskebe

                   WOMEN WITH DISABILITIES IN MONGOLIA
                             1. Tumengerel PUREV*, 2. Tsetsegjargal TSEDEN†

Abstract
This abstract presents a study focusing on the social challenges confronting women with disabilities in
Mongolia, examining prevalent obstacles and societal dynamics. Historically, disability issues in
Mongolia received minimal government attention during transitional periods. However, recent
legislative developments, particularly the enactment of the "Law on the Rights of Persons with
Disabilities" in 2016, signify a notable shift towards prioritizing disability rights. This study explores
important quantitative data and research findings to outline the socio-economic landscape for women
with disabilities. In 2022, Mongolia records 115,115 registered persons with disabilities, with women
constituting 42.6 percent. Mental and hearing impairments are notably higher among women.
However, their participation in early childhood education and mainstream schooling remains
disproportionately lower compared to men with disabilities. Furthermore, women with disabilities face
increased unemployment rates, often due to limited opportunities in the formal job market, resulting in
reliance on informal sector employment or caregiving roles, contributing to their increased risk of
poverty. Women with disabilities encounter compounded vulnerability to disability and gender-based
discrimination. Domestic violence is significantly more prevalent among women with disabilities, with
underreporting to authorities due to perceived inadequacies in protection and support mechanisms. Key
challenges faced by women with disabilities in Mongolia include inadequate policy implementation,
specifically in addressing age, gender, residential location, and specific needs. Insufficient funding for
policy execution further hinders sustained support for this demographic. Additionally, physical
inaccessibility of public infrastructure such as buildings and transportation systems limits their
educational and employment opportunities. The study underscores the exclusion of women with
disabilities from decision-making processes that impact their lives, highlighting systemic biases and
underrepresentation. Gender stereotypes and discrimination further delay their access to education and
healthcare services. The abstract calls for inclusive policy interventions that address systemic barriers
and societal prejudices, advocating for robust policy frameworks with adequate resources to ensure
equitable opportunities and social integration for women with disabilities in Mongolia. This research
contributes to advancing understanding and promoting actionable solutions towards enhancing the
quality of life and rights of women with disabilities in the country.
Keywords: Women with disabilities, gender, discrimination, education.

      BAUHAUS THAT LEFT ITS MARK ON THE 20TH CENTURY
      PIONEERING FEMALE GRAPHIC DESIGNER SÖRE POPITZ
                                    1. Ufuk BAKAN*, 2. Zehra ATABEY†

Abstract
Bauhaus Art and Design School, which brings together many important designers and artists in many
fields such as architecture, industrial design, graphic design, furniture design, and urban planning, aims
to develop an interdisciplinary approach by bringing together the concept of art and craft. The basis of
this school's education and training system, where many disciplines come together, is shaped by the
workshop system required by the industry, combining theoretical knowledge and application methods.
The Bauhaus School of Art and Design aims to develop designs based on functionality, innovation,
simplicity, and geometric balance. Distinguished names such as Johannes Itten, Josef Albers, Wassily
Kandinsky, and László Moholy-Nagy also attended this school. Söre Popitz is the only known and
pioneering female name in graphic design at the Bauhaus Art and Design School, which includes
people from different fields. Söre Popitz, a student of Josef Albers, Wassily Kandinsky, and László
Moholy-Nagy, worked in graphic design, such as typography, advertising, and book designs. Popitz,
who studied graphic design, drawing, and advertising at an art school in Germany, received training
from important names of the period and had the opportunity to improve himself in his field. The
designer, who died in 1993 at 97, mostly adopted a constructivist approach, focused on geometry, and
used bright and pigmented colors in his works. However, in their designs, Figurative, typographic, and
abstract effects stand out. This study aims to examine the works of Söre Popitz, the pioneer female
graphic designer of the Bauhaus, in the field of art and design. Within the scope of the study, Popitz's
biography and style in the design field are discussed, and Popitz's graphic design works are examined
within the framework of visual design elements and the constructivism movement. The study evaluates
Popitz's work and working principles in graphic design as a female designer. In the study's conclusion,
it is envisaged to obtain detailed information about Söre Popitz's works.
Keywords: Bauhaus, graphic design, advertising, typography, constructivism.

PROHIBITION OF DISCRIMINATION AND WOMEN'S SURNAME IN
 TURKEY ACCORDING TO SUPREME COURT DECISIONS FROM
                   PAST TO PRESENT
                                              Umay KARAKAYA*

Abstract
Gender equality has been controversial topic from past to present. However, we can’t assert that gender
equality has been literally ensured. While men used only their own surname, women were obliged not
to use their surname, which they used before marriage, without their husband’s surname after marriage
according to the Turkish Civil Code in our country until a few months ago. Although this relevant
regulation was abolished by the law control decision of the Constitutional Court of Republic of Turkey
dated 22/02/2023, it has not been amended regulating this matter. Accordingly the issue of the women's
surname continues to remain unclear in the field of law. Gender equality is included in our legal system
in accordance with the Constitution of Republic of Turkey and the European Convention on Human
Rights which we are a party of. In fact, based jurisprudences related to law control of the Constitutional
Court on the same issue was different from the jurisprudences of the European Court of Human Rights
and the jurisprudence of the Supreme Court of Appeals. These discussions are centered around whether
the obligation of using women’s husband's surname after marriage is necessary for public order and
public welfare in relation to family unity. The Constitutional Court turned from its previous established
jurisprudences by the law control decision, dated 2023 and mentioned at the beginning of this text, and
aligned itself with the jurisprudences of the European Court of Human Rights. However, these debates
will maintain its freshness for a while. This study will be created by analyzing the important decisions
of the supreme courts regarding the woman's surname. Changes in the decisions and current decisions
will be examined. In the context of this topic, it is essential to ensure gender equality through legal
amendments that do not leave room for interpretation and to eliminate uncertainty. In addition, some
supreme court decisions have presented options that shed light on how these law changes can be made.
How the stated uncertainty can be resolved by changing the law in the light of these options will be
presented in this study.
Keywords: Women, equality, gender, surname, marriage, jurisprudence, law.

      AWARENESS OF TURKISH LANGUAGE AND LITERATURE
      TEACHERS AND TURKISH LANGUAGE TEACHERS ABOUT
                WOMEN WRITERS AND POETS
                                   1. Vafa SAVAŞKAN*, 2. Nurşah ÖZER†

Abstract
Turkish literature is shaped by both male and female writers and poets. However, it is a well-known
fact that cultural and sociological factors have hindered the prominence of women in both social life
and our literature. Despite all these obstacles, female writers and poets have existed in our literature
from the XIII century, which is considered the beginning of Divan literature, up to the present day, and
their influence has increased. The increase in their presence cannot be denied to be influenced by the
social and political rights given to women. Bringing our female writers and poets to light and ensuring
that they are not forgotten even today should be considered the primary duty of Turkish language and
literature teachers, and this sensitivity should also be instilled in their pre-service training. In this
context, the aim of this research is to determine the awareness of Turkish language and literature
teachers and Turkish language teachers about the female writers and poets existing in our literature. In
line with this aim, the study used a qualitative research design, specifically phenomenology. The
sample of the research consists of Turkish language and literature teachers and Turkish language
teachers working in middle and high schools in the city center of Artvin. A semi-structured interview
form consisting of open-ended questions, developed by the researcher to determine some demographic
characteristics of the participants, was used to collect research data. Content analysis was used to
analyze the data, and the findings were presented by tabulating them.
Keywords: Authoress, Poetess, Women’s Literature.

       EFFECT OF LAW NO. 6284 TO PREVENT PSYCHOLOGICAL
             CAUSES OF VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN
                                              Yasemin TAŞKIN*

Abstract
The issue of violence is an ancient issue that goes back as far as human history. Today, violence against
women and domestic violence are among the leading social problems experienced at national and
international levels. When we look at the scientific research literature, there are many studies on the
subject. Although it is a psycho-social problem that is being studied and there are legislative studies
carried out in the field of law, it continues to be a social problem that is still valid today. When we look
at the national and international legislation on combating violence, we see comprehensive examples of
laws on the subject. In this study, the law adopted by the Turkish Grand National Assembly on March
8, 2012 and published by the Republic of Turkey on March 20, 2012. The effect of Law No. 6284 on
the Protection of the Family and Prevention of Violence Against Women, published in the Official
Gazette, on preventing the psychological causes of violence against women in Turkey will be
evaluated. The effects of the preventive and supportive precautionary measures mentioned in Law No.
6284 on the psychological processes that lead to the emergence of violence will be examined. The
evaluation criterion will be Turkish Statistical Institute (TUIK) data from recent years (2012 and later).
In the light of TUIK data, the rates of gender and violence-related situations will be examined by year,
the changes in the parameters over the years will be evaluated, thus the effect of Law No. 6284 on the
rates of violence in Turkey will be investigated. In this context, the parameters to be examined as topics
related to violence against women are listed as follows: The state of individuals feeling safe when alone
at home according to gender, the feeling of feeling safe while walking alone at night in the environment
where individuals live, according to gender. The state of feeling safe, the prevalence of physical and
sexual violence according to education level, the prevalence of physical or sexual violence according
to wealth level, age group, education level, IBBS (Statistical Regional Units Classification) 1st level
and when there is a disagreement between spouses according to the big three, spouses according to
men The reactions given by the spouses will be examined as the reactions of the spouses compared to
women when there is a disagreement between the spouses according to their age group, education level,
IBBS 1st level and the big three. On these headings, the rates of violence data in the Turkish sample
and their distribution according to years will be examined with the descriptive research method, and
how the rates of violence have changed on the Turkish scale since 2012 until today, and the effect of
protective and preventive measures to prevent the psychological causes of violence in accordance with
the 6284 law on these rates will be interpreted.
Keywords: Psychological Causes of Violence Against Women, Law No. 6284, Women, Violence.

   MAIN ASSESSMENTS OF VARIOUS TURKISH COLUMNISTS ON
         THE RIGHTS ACQUIRED BY WOMEN (1975-2000)
                                  1. Yaşar ÖZÜÇETİN*, 2. Şeyda AKSOY†

Abstract
Women, who have been in different conditions throughout the historical process, have always been in
a positive development based on their achievements or other rights they will acquire. In particular,
Turkish women, whose education was prioritized with the Republic, have been at the center of
important transformations with the legal and political rights granted to them. The Republic was also
seen as "the name of change, bringing innovation" and was based on the realization of the equality of
women with men as a requirement of national sovereignty; This situation has been considered as a
prerequisite for the rise of society. In Turkey, the idea that there should not be any social, political and
economic difference between men and women and that a modern level cannot be achieved unless
women achieve their status in society has become dominant. In Turkey, as in the whole world, from
past to present, women's political, economic, social, cultural and civil rights have had depth and
breadth, have always maintained their importance and are likely to always maintain their importance.
The point that attracts attention is whether the principle of "equality" can be applied or not, which will
pave the way for women to benefit from and use their rights, regardless of gender, in their relations
with society. Because the issue of women's rights consists of efforts to eliminate situations that arise
when the principle of equality is undermined to the detriment of women. Columns are short opinion
pieces published in newspapers or magazines where opinions on any subject or daily events are
discussed without going into details, and they are texts whose meaning-making frameworks can be
followed. For this purpose, in the research, the common area created by the rights of Turkish women
was examined in the context of texts written by various columnists. Here, it is pointed out that it is not
possible to reverse the current phase and women are accepted as another element of the creation of
civilization. The research model was based on the aim of the research, which is to collect information
about the characteristics of the universe and to describe people, situations or events completely and
accurately; Columns and copyrighted works of "Akşam", "World", "Milliyet", "Tercüman", "Türkiye"
and "Yeni Asır" newspapers were used.
Keywords: Women, Turkish Women, Women's Rights, Equality.

WHAT DO WE THINK ABOUT TECHNOLOGY AND INNOVATION İN
                  WOMEN'S HEALTH?
                                             Yeliz DOĞAN MERİH*

Abstract
With the development of technology and innovation in women's health services, technological devices
have entered the service area, and these applications have begun to be used to improve women's health,
diagnose, prevent, treat and rehabilitate diseases. The study, carried out within the scope of all these
requirements, aimed to determine nurses' views on technological and innovative developments in
women's health services and to evaluate their participation in innovation processes. The study was
conducted as a prospective cross-sectional study in a training and research hospital between August
and November 2022. The sample consisted of 105 nurses working in the relevant hospital who agreed
to participate in the study and whose consent was obtained. The data was collected with a 35-question
survey form created by the researchers within the literature. SSPS package program was used in the
statistical evaluation of the data obtained. It was determined that 65% of the nurses were in the 26-31
age group, 75.6% had a bachelor's degree or higher, 55% had more than 10 years of professional
experience, and 96% were women. It was determined that 97% of the participants stated that
technology and innovation are necessary in women's health, 50% of them follow technological
developments in women's health, and among the innovative developments, they mostly know about
products for the diagnosis and care process (70%). It has been determined that nurses sometimes
express concern about robots and artificial intelligence products, among the innovative developments
in women's health services. It was determined that 40% of the nurses were aware of the innovation
process, 20% had received training on innovation, 12% had previously developed an innovative idea,
6% had applied to competitions regarding their idea, and 1% had a patent application. It was determined
that 60% of the products developed by the participants were for women's health, and the products were
mostly aimed at improving the care process. As a result of the study, it was determined that nurses
considered innovative approaches necessary in women's health services, thought that these
developments increased the quality of service, and engaged in moderate level innovative activities in
their service processes. Regular training, conducting scientific activities to introduce the process, and
sharing good practice examples have a great place in the use of innovative products in women's health
services and in helping nurses take part in innovation processes.
Keywords: Nurse, innovation, woman, health, technology

yelizmrh@gmail.com

 SEXUAL VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN AT HIGHER EDUCATION:
              THE CASES FROM INDONESIA
     1. Zakiyah*, 2. Umi MUZAYANAH†, 3. Nur Laili NOVIANI‡, 4. Siti MUAWANAH§, 5. Arif
                                 Gunawan SANTOSO**

Abstract
This article examined the issue of sexual violence against women in Indonesian higher education
including sexual assaults and sexual abuses. This theme became attention since many cases occurred
in several universities; for instance, 158 students at a university in West Java experienced sexual
violence, 35 university students in Ambon became victims of sexual violence, and 1,011 students of
universities in Indonesia faced such experiences. In those cases, most of the victims were women, and
only a small number were men. This paper was written based on the qualitative research done in 2023
in 19 universities spread over 11 provinces in Indonesia. Data were collected using these methods;
First, interviews with key informants in each university including students, lecturers, deans, rectors,
security teams, board members of gender studies groups, and the administrative staff. Second,
observations were conducted in those 19 selected universities. Third, the documentary study was done
to seek related data from previous research, government policies, books, journal articles, and other
documents. The findings of this study revealed that at least three types of sexual violence happened in
the universities, namely, minor, moderate, and serious sexual violence, ranging from catcalling,
sexually touching, sexual transactions, and attempted rape. In these cases, the victims were primarily
students, with a few lecturers and staff were also affected. Meanwhile, the perpetrators include
students, lecturers, and educational staff. In some of those cases, it can be seen that there was an
unequal power relation between the victims and the offenders, such as senior students against their
Yunior and lecturers against their students. Regarding those sexual violence cases, the universities have
responded with various means like suspending the offenders’ activities on campus for one to two
semesters, giving psychological treatment to the victims, and giving legal aid to the victims, as well as
setting various programs to prevent sexual violence happened.
Keywords: Sexual Violences, Higher Education, Indonesia.

  SYSTEMATIC REVIEW OF STUDIES CONDUCTED ON REFUGEE
                  WOMEN IN TURKEY
                                 1. Zehra ACAR*, 2. Nevin HOTUN ŞAHİN†

Abstract
This study is a systematic review evaluating the results of studies on refugee women in Turkey. The
literature search was performed in Pubmed and Science Directs databases between April 01-30, 2024
using the keywords “refugee”, “women”, “Turkey”. The criteria for inclusion in the systematic review
were that the publication should be in the last ten years, the language of the publication should be
English, the aim of the study should be refugee women's health, it should be a research article and the
full text should be accessible. Retrospective studies and experimental studies were excluded. As a result
of the search, a total of 1878 publications, including Pubmed (124) and Science Direct (1754), were
reached. Twenty-five studies that met the inclusion criteria were included in the study. Ten of the
studies were quantitative, 14 qualitative and one mixed research type. One of the studies was conducted
with Afghani refugee women and all other studies were conducted with Syrian refugee women. The
study groups consisted of refugee pregnant women, puerperium, mothers and women. In the studies
conducted with refugee pregnant women; prenatal attachment, sexual function and attitudes, fear of
childbirth and prenatal care experiences were examined. It was reported that refugee pregnant women
had lower levels of prenatal attachment, sexual function and sexual attitude scores, experienced more
fear of childbirth and received inadequate prenatal care compared to Turkish pregnant women. Birth
experiences, migration and motherhood experiences, breastfeeding, and breastfeeding experiences
were analyzed with refugee puerperium and mothers. It was stated that refugee puerperas mostly had
negative birth experiences. It has been reported that negative emotions, language/communication
barriers, provision of care services incompatible with religious and cultural values, poverty, lack of
social support, insufficient information and prejudices about discrimination are among the factors that
negatively affect refugee women's pregnancy and birth experiences. It has been reported that refugee
women have low levels of knowledge and use of family planning methods and breastfeeding. It was
found that women used traditional methods during pregnancy, delivery, postpartum period, in the
treatment of vaginal infection and for abortion. In addition, women's health and its determinants,
welfare, needs and challenges of refugee women, changing social structure and violence risks, gender-
based violence and empowerment experiences, and the effects of Covid 19 on the integration of refugee
women into Turkish society were examined in the studies. With its mental dimension, the prevalence
and determinants of post-traumatic stress and depression in refugee women in refugee camps and
resettled refugee women, demographic and migration factors in refugee women and the effects of
mental health on quality of life were evaluated. The protection and improvement of refugee women's
health is of great importance in the light of all these results.
Keywords: Refugee, women, Turkey.

                            Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

            DETERMINATION OF THE RELATIONSHIP BETWEEN
          INSTAGRAM ADDICTION AND SOCIAL MEDIA IN HAVING
           AESTHETIC PROCEDURES IN INDIVIDUALS AGED 18-25
       1. Zehra GÜRSOY*, 2. Arzunur KILINÇ†, 3. Kübra KILINÇ‡, 4. Senanur ÖZBEY§, 5. Sinem
                                            DALCI**

Abstract
The research aims to determine the relationship between Instagram addiction and social media in
having aesthetic procedures in individuals between the ages of 18-25 who have or have not had
aesthetic surgery. Conducted at Istanbul Arel University with ethics committee approval, the study
sampled 404 individuals using a Personal Information Form, Instagram Addiction Scale, and the
Perception of Aesthetic Procedures on Social Media Scale. Data were collected both face-to-face and
via Google Forms through snowball sampling and social media outreach. Participants included were
aged 18-25 and volunteered for the study. 404 people participated in the research and their average age
was 21 years old; it was found that 86% of them were women, 96.5% were single, 61% had an income
equal to their expenses, and 75.2% spent most of their lives in the province. The daily screen time of
the research group was 4.74 hours, and it was determined that 74.5% found themselves beautiful, 41%
used filters when uploading content on social media, 81.4% knew someone who had plastic surgery,
and 16.8% were concerned about getting likes on social media. It was found that 40.8% of the
participants wanted to have an aesthetic procedure in the near future, 35% wanted to have a nose
operation, 29% wanted to have a lip filler, and 16.6% wanted to have a breast operation. It was
determined that 98.5% of people use social media tools, and the most frequently used social media tool
is Instagram with 88.4%. A moderate positive relationship was found between the perception of
aesthetic procedures on social media and Instagram addiction (r = .43, p < .01). Additionally, Instagram
addiction was related to age, daily screen time, desire for aesthetic procedures, and specific desires for
lip fillers and nose aesthetics, feeling beautiful, anxiety about social media likes, and Instagram usage
(p < .05). The perception of aesthetic procedures on social media was linked to using filters, anxiety
about likes, the desire for aesthetic procedures, having lip fillers, and self-perception of beauty (p <
.05).
Keywords: Plastic surgery, young individuals, Instagram addiction, social media.

    REVISITING THE HISTORY OF THE HEADSCARF: CHANGING
                    DYNAMICS IN TURKEY
                                                 Zehra YILMAZ*

Abstract
Since the inception of the Turkish Republic, the headscarf issue has been a focal point in both academic
discourse and political agendas. This paper seeks to elucidate the evolving nature of the headscarf in
Turkey over time, examining its significance for wearers and its political connotations. The emergence
of historiography concerning religious women closely aligns with their efforts to document resistance
and stances against headscarf bans, particularly gaining traction in the 1990s amidst escalating
tensions. The prevailing discourse often portrays a narrative of victimization vis-à-vis the regime,
characterized by terms such as "Kemalism," "elitism," and "Westernization." This period coincides
with the rise of political Islam and the women's movement in Turkey, shaping a discourse marked by
resistance and articulations from both sides. This paper lays the groundwork for analysis by
scrutinizing the language of victimhood within historical narratives. It delineates three distinct periods:
firstly, exploring the impact of the headscarf as a symbol of urban women within the emerging religious
women's movement of the late 1960s; secondly, delving into the politicization of the issue amidst
headscarf bans in the 1990s, its evolution into a central axis of secular-religious tensions, and the
transformation of the headscarf into a battleground for women's rights. The paper concludes by
examining how the lifting of headscarf bans in 2013 reshaped the political significance of the headscarf
and altered the experiences of religious women. Unlike previous periods characterized by pluralization,
this new phase is defined by dispersal. Generational disparities, conflicts, and continuities are
considered throughout the periodization. Through this analysis, the paper aims to contribute to existing
literature by exploring the interplay between the subjectification process of religious women and the
evolving meaning of the headscarf within its historical context.
Keywords: Headscarf, historical literature, victimization, generation differences.

                           Uluslararası Küresel Dünyada Kadın Kongresi IV

        RHIZOMATIC ANALYSIS OF FOURTH WAVE FEMINISM: A
              DELEUZE AND GUATTARI PERSPECTIVE
                                            Zeynep Banu DALAMAN*

Abstract
This academic review aims to illuminate the complex foundations of fourth wave feminism, through a
rhizomatic perspective based on the philosophical concepts of Deleuze and Guattari. Deleuze and
Guattari's rhizome theory offers a structure that is interconnected and not based on hierarchy,
challenging traditional, linear narratives of history. The women's movement is considered as a rhizome
in this study, bringing a fresh and innovative perspective to the subject. Fourth wave feminism is
characterized by its complexity, multi-dimensional structure, prominent presence of digital activism,
intersectional understanding, and global reach. This makes fourth wave feminism quite suitable for a
rhizomatic review. Traditional feminist frameworks often fall short in fully grasping the wide-ranging
essence of this wave due to their linear and hierarchical structures. This underlines the importance of
a non-traditional, rhizomatic approach. The aim is to examine the dynamic and ever-changing
landscape of the feminist movement under the light of Deleuze-Guattari theory. The study reveals how
various voices and experiences come together, and how they shape a sturdy, decentralized movement
by rejecting traditional organizational norms. This review argues that using a rhizomatic framework
not only helps in understanding the unique contributions and distinct features of fourth wave feminism
but also provides a transformative perspective for the future direction of feminist advocacy strategies.
This study aims to provide a comprehensive understanding of fourth wave feminism; from illuminating
the complex network of digital activism, to a thorough examination of intersectionality, and
understanding the extensive impact of global feminism. In other words, it aims to contribute to the
evolution of the feminist movement, with the hope of creating more research and debate in the field of
feminist advocacy.
Keywords: Fourth wave feminism, Deleuze and Guattari, rhizomatic approach, digital activism,
intersectionality, global feminism, feminist advocacy.

THE ROLE OF WOMEN IN THE OTTOMAN PRESS IN THE SECOND
  CONSTITUTIONAL PERIOD; OTTOMAN SOCIETY FOR THE
             DEFENSE OF WOMEN'S RIGHTS
                                                 Zeynep GÜNAY*

Abstract
One of the most important elements of the Turkish modernization movement that began with the
Tanzimat period is the change in the economic and social position of women. In this sense, with the
Tanzimat and the subsequent Constitutional Monarchy, the presence of women spread to many fields.
The starting point of this movement was undoubtedly education. Education led to changes in women's
roles that can be called reforms. The door of "freedom" opened by the Constitutional Monarchy
allowed women to establish societies. In this context, the first women's associations in the Ottoman
lands were formed by non-Muslim women. The founding policies of these associations were generally
formed within the framework of charity and aid. Later, after World War I, although more than one
society was established in which women could operate, the Ottoman Mudafaa-i Hukuk-ı Nisvan
Society, which was considered a women's movement and a completed revolution, undertook an
important mission. The society's publication, Kadınlar Dünyası (Women's World), is an independent
women's literary world without any ideological thought. In this work, the great struggles of women,
especially for their employment in public spaces, were addressed and shouted out to the whole world.
When analyzed periodically, the magazine's handling of women's issues from all angles, bringing them
to the agenda and its struggle in this direction became an important media tool for perceiving the world
of women. In addition to the struggle of the magazine, the intellectual developments in the 20th century
were recorded as important developments showing the presence of women in the public sphere. When
we look at the sales figures of the magazine in 1913, the fact that it was purchased by almost 3000
people proves that the magazine was reached by large masses. In this study, attention was drawn to the
place of women in the public sphere in the Ottoman Empire, especially by the Women's World print
media. In this context, almost all issues of the journal were examined. In this study, which was carried
out to read the women's movement in the Ottoman Empire through societies, the changes in women's
roles were investigated within the framework of time, place and events.
Keywords: Woman, Ottoman, II. Constitutional Monarchy, Modernization, Society.
